# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on Keep a Changelog, with project-specific notes for engineering verification.

## [Unreleased]

### Documentation — real-provider local acceptance evidence

- **What changed:** Record current AwwO Go/Pi SaaS acceptance, 57 composite UI cases, 72 screenshot records, real model/database evidence, backup restore, and both browser-discovered fixes.
- **Why:** Source review and protocol fixtures do not prove the existing frontend works against an external model or that every deployment capability is ready.
- **Impact:** The report accepts the tested local text collaboration core and explicitly retains engineering-tool, background-graph, UI edge-case, container and public deployment gaps. It does not certify production readiness or unrelated inherited workspaces.
- **Verification:** Current Web/SaaS/Gateway/Go/Pi suites, negative HTTP cases, real UI/Pi requests, download integrity and local backup restore passed within their stated scopes; report links, screenshot hashes and cleanup are checked.
- **Files:** `docs/awwo-real-provider-acceptance-20260908.md`; local evidence in `.local/awwo-saas/acceptance-20260908/`.

### Fixed — SaaS mobile canvas controls

- **What changed:** Wrap mobile canvas account controls below the workspace title and place run status in the control group flow.
- **Why:** The 390px browser acceptance viewport showed a vertically compressed workspace name and a run notice overlapping the account controls.
- **Impact:** The SaaS canvas at widths up to 760px keeps navigation, workspace selection, account, logout and run controls visible. Existing desktop rules and runtime behavior are unchanged.
- **Verification:** CSS parsing and scoped-rule checks, SaaS build, actual 390×843 CSS viewport screenshots and account dialog interaction pass; desktop canvas was checked afterward. Evidence: `.local/awwo-saas/acceptance-20260908/frontend-mobile-fix-report.md` and screenshots 66–68.
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/saas/saas.css`.

### Fixed — cancelled conversation recovery order

- **What changed:** Anchor locally recovered cancelled output after its matching persisted user message; preserve the latest completed reply and avoid duplicate turns across repeated refreshes.
- **Why:** Real Go/Pi/GPT-5.6 browser acceptance reproduced an old cancelled response appearing after a newer successful response and replacing its card preview.
- **Impact:** Session history and card previews keep chronological order, including repeated prompts and server replies longer than cached partial output. No API or database schema change.
- **Verification:** Three regression cases failed before the fix and passed afterward; four new regressions and related tests pass. Full SaaS suite: 65 files / 612 tests; original Web suite: 91 files / 1,009 tests (overlapping suites), static checks, typecheck and SaaS build pass. Browser refresh/repeated refresh and a subsequent real two-node graph succeeded. Evidence: `.local/awwo-saas/acceptance-20260908/frontend-recovery-fix-report.md`.
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/canvas/runRecoveryDocument.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-recovery-document.test.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-tile-history.test.tsx`.

### Fixed — Fable acceptance gaps in the Go/Pi SaaS integration

- **What changed:** Add signed pagination to all six tenant resource lists and page controls to the original workspace panel; expose additional workspace creation and canvas rename/delete with version and active-run guards. Restore the original appearance catalog, custom colors and import/export with per-user PostgreSQL persistence. Localize run errors and make history/active-run lookups independent of the old 200-row window. Add isolated HTTP fault controls and an explicit real-provider acceptance entry.
- **Why:** Acceptance found inaccessible records beyond 200, missing frontend actions/settings, untranslated failure states and insufficient repeatable negative test conditions.
- **Impact:** Existing canvas documents and account boundaries remain authoritative. Concurrent canvas/appearance writes use CAS; unknown save outcomes require reload. Test fault controls stay outside the application API. No merge, remote publication or external inference is included.
- **Verification:** Real PostgreSQL/race/vet (26 top-level + 22 subtests), SaaS/legacy component suites, typecheck/build, 21 script tests and 14 real HTTP negative-flow checks. Computer Use stopped after the registration form because Chrome returned empty accessibility state and no screenshot. Real provider configuration, remaining GUI scenarios and container/runtime acceptance are still open. Independent review results and exact evidence are recorded in `docs/awwo-fable-remediation-20260907.md`.
- **Files:** `backend/internal/app/{tenant_pagination,appearance}.go`, migrations 005/006 and lifecycle handlers; `apps/web/src/{saas,account,settings}/**`, canvas conversation adapters and regressions; `scripts/awwo-saas-{browser-fixture,acceptance-faults,fixture-control,real-provider-acceptance}*`; backend and SaaS documentation.

### Fixed — Computer Use acceptance findings

- **What changed:** Persist consumed send drafts with the run snapshot; preserve structured login errors for localization; count blocked nodes within the selected run scope; allow cloud run reconciliation while retaining conflict drafts and detached-run snapshots; compare canvas documents independently of JSON object key order.
- **Why:** Real browser actions reproduced sent prompts returning after refresh, English errors on the Chinese login page, a blocked count larger than the selected graph, inaccessible completed runs when an older draft remained, and repeated same-version draft warnings after undoing a plan.
- **Impact:** The original canvas, tenant/account controls and Go/Pi contracts are preserved. Recovery keeps unsent drafts and does not dispatch waiting downstream nodes. Object-key normalization retains actual value, timestamp and array-order differences. No merge, push, external inference or deployment is included.
- **Verification:** Five defects have focused regressions and original-scenario Computer Use verification. The final combined Web suites, builds and independent reviews are recorded in `docs/awwo-computer-use-acceptance-20260907.md`. The GUI exercised 16 Go/Pi SDK/local-provider runs: 13 completed and 3 intentionally cancelled, with database and provider evidence. The report distinguishes covered flows, unexecuted branches, Chrome automation limitations and production gates.
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/canvas/runGraph.ts`, `apps/web/src/saas/{SaaSApp.tsx,canvasDraft.ts}`, related canvas/API/draft tests, and the Computer Use acceptance report and coverage matrix.

### Fixed — SaaS acceptance authorization and lifecycle failures

- **What changed:** Normalize graph revisions before the first run; recheck tenant mutation authorization inside transactions; commit cancellation state/events/audit together; enforce timed SSE authorization during historical replay; reject admission after detected worker lease loss; return 400 for malformed Pi HTTP targets; honor local-launcher shutdown across asynchronous startup phases.
- **Why:** Adversarial acceptance reproduced false cross-tab conflicts, late writes after revocation, continued private event replay, stranded queued runs, a Pi process crash, and services launching after cancellation.
- **Impact:** Existing canvas and API contracts remain usable with stricter admission and lifecycle handling. Single Go worker, periodic SSE revocation and explicit local-provider fixture boundaries remain in force. No merge, release, external inference or deployment is included.
- **Verification:** 58 SaaS-related frontend files/554 cases and original 91 files/1003 cases (47 overlapping files); Gateway 36 files/389 cases; Go 22 top-level plus 16 subcases with real PostgreSQL/race/vet; Pi 22, SaaS scripts 8 and original scripts 12; final-binary API smoke and Go/Pi/SDK/PostgreSQL stack; both Web builds/typecheck, browser protocol acceptance and dependency/config checks. Full findings, evidence and remaining gates are recorded in `docs/awwo-saas-acceptance-20260907.md`.
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx` and first-run regressions; `backend/internal/app/{app,invitations,resources,planning,runs}.go` and authorization/lease regressions; `apps/pi-worker/{server,worker.test,http-boundary.test}.mjs`; `scripts/awwo-saas-{dev,scripts.test}.mjs`; SaaS architecture and acceptance documents.

### Added — original frontend SaaS account and administration integration

- **What changed:** Connected the original account/workspace panel to Go profile and membership APIs; added expiring, revocable, single-use invitations with explicit acceptance. Reused the original canvas for readers with mutation boundaries, restored shared language/theme preferences, exposed server-owned Pi runtime status, and added platform quota forms, cursor pagination and complete JSON export. Added an isolated browser protocol fixture and updated API, architecture, development and acceptance documentation.
- **Why:** The Go/Pi foundation needed the original frontend interactions and role-specific behavior connected to real persisted APIs.
- **Impact:** Tenant and platform permissions remain distinct. Invitations do not send email or elevate existing members. Reader history stays available while editing/execution is disabled. Export follows a creation-time boundary and is not a consistent database backup. Real provider configuration and public deployment remain separate acceptance gates.
- **Verification:** 57 frontend files / 552 tests; 18 Go top-level tests plus 5 admission cases with race/vet; complete Go/Pi/PostgreSQL protocol stack; live API smoke; typecheck/build; browser account/invite/preferences/admin export and two-node Pi protocol execution, history, refresh recovery and cancellation. See `docs/awwo-saas-verification.md` for limits and review evidence.
- **Files:** `backend/internal/app/{invitations,pagination,app,resources,database}.go`, migrations 003/004 and tests; `apps/web/src/{account,canvas,saas}/**`, `CanvasAccountControl.tsx` and related tests; `scripts/awwo-saas-{smoke,browser-fixture}.mjs`; `backend/README.md`; `docs/awwo-saas-*.md`.

### Fixed — saved canvas position and unassigned membership controls

- **What changed:** Retain normalized saved pan/zoom during first layout; show an explicit localized unassigned role so a valid role can be selected directly. Make draft acknowledgement tests wait for the registered real save callback.
- **Why:** Initial auto-fit overwrote a returning user's position, while a fallback role hid the unassigned state. The draft test had unrelated mount/debounce timing dependencies.
- **Impact:** Explicit Fit and new-graph auto-fit remain available. No production draft-save or preview logic changed.
- **Verification:** Added viewport/first-preview component regressions and bilingual null-role assignment tests; draft tests passed three repeated runs; complete 57-file, 552-test regression passed. Codex and Gemini independently passed final remediation review.
- **Files:** `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/account/AccountWorkspacePanel.tsx`, and related canvas/account/draft tests.

### Documentation — frontend-driven SaaS delivery standards

- **What changed:** Added AwwO-specific design/development standards and a frontend integration inventory, with the existing canvas as the implementation and acceptance baseline.
- **Why:** The SaaS foundation and its new shell do not yet cover every original account, invitation, language, theme or settings interaction. The inherited SuperClaw workflow also needs an explicit applicability check against AwwO's actual branches.
- **Impact:** Documents ownership, API/data/runtime boundaries, environment configuration, review and human merge gates, remaining integration work and evidence levels. No runtime behavior or release version changed.
- **Verification:** Documentation is checked against the recorded implementation snapshot and existing acceptance evidence; links and formatting are checked separately. This entry does not claim any additional frontend or real-provider capability passed.
- **Files:** `docs/awwo-saas-design-standards.md`, `docs/awwo-saas-frontend-contract.md`, `docs/awwo-saas-architecture.md`, `README.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Added — Go + Pi multi-tenant SaaS foundation

- **What changed:** Added an independent Go/PostgreSQL API for accounts, tenant membership and roles, versioned canvas documents, agents, persistent sessions, idempotent runs, durable SSE, cancellation, quotas, platform administration and audit. Added a private Pi 0.85.1 execution service with isolated per-run processes, bounded input/history and server-owned model configuration. Added a SaaS user/admin entrypoint that reuses the existing canvas, templates and planner, plus local setup/start/test scripts and a four-service deployment template.
- **Why:** AwwO's local Codex workflow assumed one trusted machine. A public SaaS needs server-owned identity, data isolation, execution state and provider credentials while retaining the existing product interface.
- **Impact:** SaaS uses `setup:saas` / `dev:saas`; the original local mode remains separately available. This foundation supports one Go API instance. Real provider acceptance, background whole-graph scheduling, engineering execution sandboxes, attachments, email, billing and public deployment remain explicit subsequent work.
- **Verification:** Real PostgreSQL/race and authorization tests, real Pi SDK with local protocol providers, complete Go/Pi/PostgreSQL integration, canvas/type/build tests and local browser acceptance. Exact results, corrected findings and unavailable checks are recorded in `docs/awwo-saas-verification.md`; configured health is not a real model inference result.
- **Files:** `backend/**`, `apps/pi-worker/**`, `apps/web/src/saas/**`, scoped adapters in `apps/web/src/canvas/**`, `apps/web/tests/saas-*`, `scripts/awwo-saas-*`, `deploy/saas/**`, and `docs/awwo-saas-*.md`.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-05

- Add account/profile and workspace membership management using the existing control-plane APIs, including copied invitation links, role updates and server-enforced last-owner protection. Local trusted mode is identified explicitly.
- Add Chinese and English canvas interfaces and personalized template text while preserving user content, Session history, field IDs and protocol keys.
- Persist execution identity before dispatch, recover native run state after disconnection, and use browser ownership locks to prevent duplicate canvas execution across tabs.
- Confirm Stop against the native Agent before unlocking, preserve uncertain execution for recovery, and continue within the original Session after confirmed cancellation.
- Validate structured deliveries before downstream execution; preserve historical replies without publishing stale, partial, manual or mismatched results as successful graph outputs.
- Add isolated authenticated account acceptance and real local Codex planning, binding, Session, file-delivery and repair evidence. This remains a local development release; authenticated multi-tenant canvas isolation and production deployment are not claimed.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-09-05

- Establish AwwO as an independent private Forgejo repository with a complete current-source snapshot, preserved third-party licenses and documented SuperClaw provenance.
- Add the conversational Agent canvas, seven personalized templates, compact node workbenches, typed rich-text contracts, Session isolation, graph planning and editable connections.
- Verify real local Codex execution across seven nodes and twelve exact upstream-to-downstream field transfers, including repair and original-Session revalidation.
- Decode and validate native Codex streaming output, recover bounded truncated logs, and surface official dependency-resume errors without claiming an unobserved run.
- Fix compact output summaries, local delivery-file references, gateway test portability and strict TypeScript checks.
- Add repository-relative development setup/start commands and a portable generated knowledge-workbench example with its tests and contracts.
- This release establishes a local development baseline. It does not claim production deployment, complete real identity/business services, or a desktop installer release.

### Inherited SuperClaw unreleased history

### Added

- **Fan-in: several upstreams into one node.** The engine was always written for multiple upstreams (runGraph collects every incoming edge; buildNodeMessage labels each by source) but canConnect refused the second wire, so the capability was unreachable — "combine the plan and the brand voice into the implementation", the shape people actually build, could not be drawn. `context` is now a multi input on every session kind (`reference` stays single: one picture); re-drawing an existing wire is a no-op, not a duplicate. Upstream ORDER in the prompt is the canvas reading order (source node top-to-bottom then left-to-right), never the sequence wires were drawn in — insertion order made the prompt depend on something invisible on screen. Ports show their state: a connected port is filled at full opacity, and a multi input carrying several upstreams grows a count pill. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/ports.ts`, `runGraph.ts`, `TilePorts.tsx`, `canvas.css`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-fanin.test.ts`.

- **Undo / redo.** The canvas had none: one stray Delete removed a node AND the agent session inside it, unrecoverable. Every mutation already funnelled through patchDoc, so history is recorded in one honest place with three rules: user edits are steps; machine writes (server-minted issueId, transcript-derived preview, debounced viewport save) are NOT — undoing "the agent replied" is meaningless, and letting those writes clear the redo stack would destroy a redo mid-reach; a continuous gesture is ONE step (label-coalesced within 900ms, so a drag never rewinds pixel by pixel). ⌘Z/⌘⇧Z/⌘Y plus palette rows that stay listed-but-disabled when empty. Locked during a run like every structural edit. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `useCanvasKeys.ts`, `CommandBar.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-history.test.tsx`.

- **Multi-select that can be moved.** Marquee selection existed but pressing any member collapsed it before a drag could begin, and moveNode moved one node anyway — an arrangement could be selected and never moved. The tile now reports HOW a press changes the selection (additive shift-click / preserve-the-group on press / collapse-to-one on click), separated by a 3px drag threshold; the dragged node takes the absolute gesture position and the rest of the group translates rigidly by its delta, all one undo step. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/SessionTile.tsx`, `CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-group-move.test.tsx`.

- **Outputs that survive, and runs you can scope — the iteration loop.** A node's output lived in a Map local to one runGraph call and was rendered NOWHERE: the canvas threw away everything it produced, every press of 运行图 re-executed and re-billed every tile (a parked unbound scratch tile also refused the whole run in preflight), and the loop the product exists for — look at the output, adjust one node, carry it forward — was inexpressible. `lastOutput {text, at, source, partial}` now persists on the node (sanitised for old documents) and renders as a 产出 block in the tile; a partial off a FAILED run is kept as evidence but labelled and coloured as failure. runGraph takes `scope` + `storedOutput`: an out-of-scope upstream contributes its stored output and reports as `cached` (its own word — never `done`, it did not run), or BLOCKS with a named reason when nothing is stored rather than sending an empty precondition the agent could not detect. Preflight validates only what will run; success counts against the scope; the summary reports how many upstreams were reused. Exposed as 运行选区及其下游 and 只重跑选中节点. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/canvasDoc.ts`, `runGraph.ts`, `CanvasSurface.tsx`, `SessionTile.tsx`, `RunControls.tsx`, `RunTimeline.tsx`, `CommandBar.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-scoped-run.test.ts`.

- **The visual pass — state you can see, type that renders, a canvas that feels like space.** Four verified findings: (1) NO typeface was ever loaded ('Inter'/'IBM Plex Sans' asked for, neither exists in the repo), so six authored weights collapsed into Segoe UI's two — the stack now leads with variable system faces and the ladder is three deliberate steps with whole-pixel sizes (half-pixel type renders soft inside the world transform at any zoom ≠ 1). (2) The run timeline's state classes matched NOTHING in the stylesheet (`st-failed` vs `--failed`), so a failed row was indistinguishable from a done one, and its kind colours read three nonexistent variables whose fallbacks were exactly the generic purple "AI palette" the owner rejected — classes are now templated from the state and point at real brand tokens. (3) Run state now rides the tile's own skin as a 2-3px ring (breathing while running, reduced-motion gated), legible at overview zoom where the old 7px dot rendered ~2px. (4) The dot grid now moves WITH the world on two camera-driven layers (fine fades below ~0.3 instead of moiréing; coarse survives at 8×), so zoom reads as "I moved closer" instead of "the content resized". Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/canvas.css`, `CanvasViewport.tsx`, `RunTimeline.tsx`, `SessionTile.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-grid.test.ts`.

### Changed

- **The canvas was rebuilt from scratch as a SESSION canvas, and fully replaces the old fused canvas.** Owner ruling: "删掉所有这些原本的底层内容吧，你一匹配就是错的，从头开始重构这个逻辑 … 全部取代" — the product is no longer a fleet company world plus a creative node world sharing a surface; it is one canvas where **every node is an agent session with its own chat window inside the tile**, an upstream node's output becomes the downstream node's precondition input, and a form node supplies hand-authored input. New `apps/web/src/canvas/` (24 modules): `canvasDoc` (v2 persisted document + migration off the legacy key + corrupt-backup instead of silent wipe), `ports`/`canConnect`/`reconcileEdges` (typed ports; an edge whose port vanished is dropped from document, run graph and screen), `viewport` (pan/zoom/fit math ported verbatim), `lod` (glance→card→open→focus by effective pixel height, with focus carrying the composer), `sessions` (per-node external store so one node's stream never re-renders another), `runGraph` (topological run with cycle preflight, per-agent serialization, honest blocked/failed states, first-terminal-frame-wins), plus the tile, wiring, marquee, inspector, run controls, timeline, minimap, command palette and key bindings. Deleted in the same pass: `apps/web/src/studio/` entirely, `HireRosterDialog`/`HireAgentDialog`/`RunDetailPanel`/`canvasRunDetail`, the whole `apps/fleet-canvas` package (zero importers remained), and 38 obsolete test files; the internal surface key `studio` was renamed `canvas` (state, dock, CSS, settings-return), with stale `studio`/`fleet-canvas`/`creative-canvas` landing pins still remapping to the canvas so nobody lands on chat. **`apps/creative-canvas` is deliberately KEPT** — the myshell orchestrator imports its embed and builds it in CI, so deleting it would break a separate product line; only the root `Dockerfile`'s two sibling-embed `COPY`s are gone now that `apps/web` is self-contained. Suite went from 213s to ~30s once the legacy tests left. Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm test --prefix apps/web` 620 passed across 52 files, `npm run build` succeeds; browser-verified live against a real dev server — right-click add menu (form/LLM/coding/image), node creation + persistence, typed port wiring (`form.data → llm.context`, dataType derived from the source port), command palette with node search, focus zooming to 1.75× and revealing the per-node composer, and the inspector failing CLOSED with distinguishable reasons ("运行时清单加载失败" vs "没有可用的 LIVE 公司") rather than an empty dropdown. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/**`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-*.test.*`, `apps/web/package.json`, `Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/deploy-aws.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- MyShell Studio Orchestrator is now coupled into SuperClaw as the primary `/dreamy` product surface, with the existing Creative Canvas mounted as a lazy-loaded supplemental “Creative Director” workspace. CanvasPro remains mounted and is restored without losing its iframe session; Creative Director adds AI Script Director, Storyboard, 3D Director, VR360 Panorama, and Pose Editor workflows, and both script/3D video prompts preserve the Seedance four-block format with per-shot sound cues. The imported FastAPI orchestration, durable projects/jobs/evidence, MyShell adapters, CanvasPro compatibility bridge, and SuperClaw HTTP bridge remain intact. Stale maintenance-script imports and preview fixtures were aligned with the uploaded modular backend. The Cloud Run image now builds both coupled apps from the repository root, prepares the CanvasPro compatibility mount through the normal frontend build, includes the backend runtime package despite the repository-wide runtime artifact ignore, installs a hash-locked Python dependency graph compatible with SuperClaw's WebSocket runtime, requires authenticated invocation when secret-backed adapters are bound, restricts credentialed CORS to explicit origins, passes operator identity tokens without logging them, and validates MyShell cookie JSON through stdin instead of exposing it in process arguments. Verification: MyShell Python suites 208 passed plus 13 subtests; frontend unit/smoke-definition tests 14 passed; MyShell and Creative Canvas production builds passed; Chromium exercised both workspace directions and all five Creative Director tools. Files: `apps/myshell-studio-orchestrator/**`, `apps/creative-canvas/src/App.tsx`, `.dockerignore`, `.gitignore`, `pyproject.toml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

- Added a `cyber` appearance preset — deep-space navy surfaces + cyan accent (dark canvas only), ported from the ClawHunt "cyber palette" (bobo/Arxchibobo on `windows-main`, aligned with clawhunt.store). Integrated as a selectable preset via the existing kernel catalog + token whitelist (bg_base `#08081a` / bg_sidebar `#0b0b20` / bg_card `#0f1530` / border `#1e2747` / text `#eef2fb`,`#b8c2e0` / accent `#00d4ff`), so it re-themes the whole super UI and the embedded board (via the board bridge) like the other presets, while `default` stays the original stock look. Scope note: this ports the FLAT color palette only — the source skin's cyan→purple gradient, neon glows, and secondary accent are NOT included (they don't fit the solid-color token model; capturing them would need a contract extension). Verification: `tests/test_appearance.py` 23 passed (override-drift guard confirms all cyber tokens are whitelisted); `ruff` clean. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/appearance.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Redesigned the appearance presets from thin single-accent swaps into COORDINATED multi-token themes, and grew the gallery from 6 to 10. What changed (kernel `superclaw.appearance.APPEARANCE_PRESETS`, the single source of truth surfaced verbatim to CLI/API/Web/Desktop): each preset now overrides the full high-leverage set per canvas — bg_base / bg_sidebar / bg_card / border_light / text_primary / text_secondary / accent — so it reads as a designed look (a tinted surface family + matching accent + readable text) rather than a hue change on the stock greys. New/kept ids: `default` (stock, no overrides), `midnight`, `slate`, `emerald`, `ocean`, `violet`, `rose`, `amber`, `high_contrast`, `oled`. Dark is the primary canvas (the app defaults to dark); light variants are mostly-white surfaces with a faint themed tint + a darker accent for contrast. `warn` / `danger` / `agent_running` are deliberately NOT themed so status semantics ("danger = red") survive. No structural/contract change: still only the 10 whitelisted token ids, validation unchanged, surfaces read the catalog via the contract (no hardcoded palettes). Impact: the preset picker (Settings → color-scheme dialog) now offers 10 cohesive themes that re-theme the whole super UI live. Verification: kernel import + all preset overrides ⊆ token whitelist + `tests/test_appearance.py` (contract lists presets/tokens, set-preset persists, override-drift guard) green; `ruff` clean; browser end-to-end (emerald/violet/rose re-theme the full canvas, not just the accent). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/appearance.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Made the embedded Paperclip company board (CompanyBoard / server/ui) respond to super's color-scheme presets, not just light/dark — closing the prior "custom colors don't reach the board" limitation. How: the board (shadcn) names its design tokens differently from super (`--background` vs `--bg-base`, `--foreground` vs `--text-primary`, …) and — except for `--accent` — none collide, so a CSS rule scoped to `.company-board-root` (and the board's body-portal surfaces) redefines the board's tokens from super's appearance vars. Because the board is mounted natively in the same document, this is a pure-CSS cascade bridge: when a preset changes super's vars, the board re-themes automatically (no JS/observer). Surfaces map to super's `--bg-card` — the SAME surface super paints its content pages (`.workspace-page`) and cards with — so the board sits flush on the super page it's embedded in (an earlier cut mapped to `--bg-base`, which left the board a darker inset than its container; `--bg-card` matches, and also restores the vendored board's original `#161616 = super --bg-card` intent). The board's brand color (`--primary`/`--ring`/`--sidebar-primary`) maps to a new non-colliding `--sc-accent` mirror: the bare `--accent` is deliberately shadowed to the board's neutral gray by the existing de-collision block, so `appearance.ts` publishes the brand accent under `--sc-accent` (base stylesheet default + kept in sync by `syncBoardAccentMirror` in `applyAppearance`/`applyCachedScheme`). Vendored `server/ui` is NOT modified (bridge lives in super's own `styles.css`, respecting the no-fork rule). Known follow-up (non-blocking): the board's hover/active-highlight tokens (shadcn `--accent`/`--sidebar-accent`) stay neutral gray by design of the de-collision; theming those to a subtle accent tint is a separate change. Verification: `apps/web/tests/appearance.test.ts` green (15; the `--sc-accent` mirror doesn't break apply/clear); browser end-to-end measured every board surface (directory + company-detail dashboard + inner sidebar + cards) = `#0e1d16` under emerald, matching super's page surface exactly. Files: `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/src/appearance.ts`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Reworked the Web color-scheme editor from a sprawling inline Settings panel into a focused modal launched from the Appearance row, with the per-token custom editor behind an Advanced disclosure (presentation-only; no kernel/contract/API change). What changed: the old always-rendered `ColorSchemePanel` (preset grid + 10 color pickers stacked on the Preferences page, clashing with the row-based settings surface) is gone; the Appearance row now carries a single palette icon-button (`aria-label="Customize color scheme"`) beside the Light/Dark control that opens a `ColorSchemeDialog` built on the shared `DialogShell` (Esc/backdrop/close dismiss, `role=dialog` + `aria-labelledby`). The dialog's default view is just the constrained **preset** list; the per-token editor + Import/Export collapse into an "Advanced — custom colors" `<details>` (picking the **Custom** preset auto-expands it). Why: owner feedback that the inline panel was ugly/cluttered and the preset swatches didn't react; the ask was an icon-button → dialog using the project's existing modal framework, preset list first and custom behind an expand. Adversarial review fixes (Codex gpt-5.5 + Antigravity Gemini 3.1 Pro, both R1 rejected): (1) **swatch staleness** — `seedColor` read `getComputedStyle` during render for tokens the active preset does not override, capturing the PREVIOUS scheme (App's `applyAppearance` effect clears the vars only after this child renders) with no re-render to correct it, so e.g. Emerald→Default left the Accent swatch stuck green; fixed by seeding overridden tokens from the kernel payload (immediate) and reading the stock-default fallback in a post-commit `requestAnimationFrame` into state (settles to the reverted color; seeded synchronously on mount so there is no open-flash). (2) **state leak** — the dialog was always mounted (only `DialogShell` returned null when closed), so `advancedOpen`/`importError` survived close→reopen; fixed by conditionally mounting the dialog (`{appearance && colorSchemeOpen ? … }`) so it unmounts on close and reopens fresh. (3) **hardcoded canvas contract** — `resetAppearance` sent `custom: { light: {}, dark: {} }`, baking the canvas vocabulary into the web layer; fixed to send `custom: {}` so the kernel normalizes against its own `CANVASES` (a future canvas is cleared without a web change). Also: `<details>` is now fully React-controlled (`summary` `onClick` `preventDefault` + `setAdvancedOpen`) to kill the semi-controlled DOM-vs-state flicker; reverted two swatch borders from raw `rgb()` back to `var(--border-strong)` (settings.css is token-only; the custom-swatch conic-gradient is the sole literal, an irreducible "rainbow = custom" icon). Reset stays a TRUE reset matching CLI `appearance reset` (default preset + cleared palette) via the existing `/api/appearance/set` (no new endpoint, no new kernel semantics). Non-blocking backlog: `DialogShell` has no initial-focus/focus-trap — a pre-existing characteristic shared by every dialog in the app (login gate, company dialogs); adding it belongs in a shell-wide change, not this UI rework. Impact: presentation-only; appearance kernel/contract/persistence semantics unchanged. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` (585 passed), `tests/app-shell.test.tsx` 142 passed incl. a rewritten test (trigger→dialog, Advanced starts collapsed via the `open` attr, expand reveals the token, Reset POSTs `{active_preset:'default', custom:{}}`, reopen starts collapsed — proving the state-leak fix), `tsc --noEmit` (no new SettingsSurface errors), and browser end-to-end on a real Python API: button placement, preset switch live re-theme, **Emerald→Default reverts the Accent swatch to `#7a8cff` (the staleness fix)**, Advanced collapse/expand + Custom auto-expand, conditional-mount state reset on reopen, and Reset clearing the kernel palette (`active_preset: default`, `custom: {light:{},dark:{}}` via curl). Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Creative Canvas now includes a local-first AI directing workflow with AI Script Director, Storyboard, 3D Director, VR360 Panorama, and Pose Editor nodes. Script and 3D director studios support structured shot planning, first-frame/video prompt handoff, collision-safe canvas placement, and accessible modal focus isolation. Final video prompts now follow the Seedance four-block standard (subject, scene, music, camera), with per-shot transitions, framing, focal length, composition, camera motion, visible action, and sound cues. Impact: additive frontend workflow only; generation remains explicit and local-safe, with no automatic external requests. Verification: Creative Canvas TypeScript/build passed; Web TypeScript/build passed; focused Web tests passed (14/14); Chromium smoke verified AI and 3D prompt generation, clipboard output, modal behavior, and zero external requests. Files: `apps/creative-canvas/**`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/creative-canvas-document.test.ts`, `apps/web/tests/creative-canvas-surface.test.tsx`.

- Web loading states now use the SuperClaw pincer loader asset pack in the two visible live surfaces. What changed: running assistant chat turns keep the stable `SuperClaw` label for screen readers but replace the visible label slot with the supplied pincer loader; the live status text row no longer renders its old inline spinner; left sidebar running sessions render the same asset instead of the old spinner; a local stopped/failed/completed assistant tail now suppresses stale backend `is_live` activity so Stop clears the sidebar animation immediately; Chromium gets `/superclaw-pincer-loader.webp` as the animated source with `/superclaw-pincer-loader.png` as fallback, and reduced-motion users get the static first frame; completed/unread sidebar dots inherit the primary text color so they are black in light mode and white in dark mode. Why: the loading affordance should match the supplied SuperClaw loader asset and the current theme, consistently between chat and the session list. Impact: presentation-only; live status semantics and existing session state handling are unchanged. Verification: `npm exec -- vitest run tests/app-shell.test.tsx --config vitest.config.mjs`, `node tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `npm run build`, `git diff --check`, branch naming grep, and browser smoke checks for dark/light WebP frame motion plus black/white sidebar dot colors passed. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/public/superclaw-pincer-loader.webp`, `apps/web/public/superclaw-pincer-loader.png`, `apps/web/public/superclaw-pincer-loader-first-frame.png`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- `claude-fable-5` is no longer offered in the model selector for the Claude agent runtimes (`claude`, `anthropic`, `anthropic-agent`). Owner curation: Fable 5 is a creative model, not a desired worker/agent model for these coding/delivery runtimes, so it should not appear in the model dropdown. Removed it from `ui_contracts.AGENT_CONTROL_SPECS[...]["suggested_models"]` for all three (default_model stays `claude-opus-4-8`), which covers the static/offline fallback AND the frontend's direct-`suggested_models` fallback (when the catalog fetch fails). But the static hint is only one of two catalog sources: with an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` configured, `model_discovery` returns the LIVE Anthropic `/v1/models` catalog verbatim, and `App.tsx` prefers that over the static hint — so a static-only removal still leaked fable to every key-configured user through the live path (Web/CLI/API all read the same catalog via `/api/agents/{backend}/models`). So curation is now enforced on the LIVE result too: a `_CURATED_MODEL_DENYLIST` in `model_discovery` (keyed `claude`/`anthropic`/`anthropic-agent`) strips fable from the deduped probe result before it reaches any surface — a single curation applied to both catalog sources. **Scope ruling** (advisor disagreement adjudicated): all three Claude-model runtimes are curated, not just `claude` — `anthropic` ("Direct Anthropic API completions (plumbing/infra)") and `anthropic-agent` ("Anthropic API agent loop with native tool use") are agent/infra runtimes on this delivery platform (not a creative surface) and were maintained in lockstep with the identical claude list, so the curation applies uniformly. **Scope of the change**: this governs what the catalog OFFERS, not what the kernel ACCEPTS — `--model` (CLI), the free-form `ComboInput` (Web), and the run lane all still accept `claude-fable-5` as free text (verified: no `click.Choice`/enum constrains `--model`; `ComboInput` is a controlled free-text input that renders an out-of-list `value` verbatim, so a saved `claude-fable-5` is preserved, not reset/crashed). The live catalog stays honest about reachability — curation is a deliberate product denylist, not a reachability lie. Verification: `tests/test_model_discovery.py` (live claude catalog strips fable; parametrized `anthropic`/`anthropic-agent` live catalogs strip fable; static fallback excludes fable) + `tests/test_ui_contracts.py` — 34 passed; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — both R1 rejected the static-only first cut (live-path leak + a test pinning `fable in live catalog` + missing this CHANGELOG gate); Gemini also flagged a CLI-`Choice` divergence, a stale-config `ComboInput` crash, and an exact-length assertion risk, all three verified non-issues locally (free-form `--model`, free-text `ComboInput`, no such assertion). Fixed by adding the live denylist + tests + changelog and re-reviewed to no-blocking-findings. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/model_discovery.py`, `tests/test_model_discovery.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Fixed

- **A leftover marker file could take the whole product down, and did.** Every `/gateway-api` call was answering 503 "Node control plane unavailable" — chat and graph runs entirely dead — while `/paperclip-api` was healthy. Root cause: the gateway marker lives in the run dir, which in the container deployment is a PERSISTENT VOLUME, so it outlives the container that wrote it; in the next container that pid number belongs to something else, because PID namespaces restart at 1. `precleanStaleGateway` then reported `identity_unknown` (a live pid it cannot prove is ours) and `startGateway` threw before binding, keeping the marker — a state it could never recover from, so the gateway never started again. The port is now the arbiter: if the marked process really were our gateway it would still hold the port, so a successful bind proves it is not and we take the marker over (logging that we did); only a real `EADDRINUSE` defers, and that path still keeps the marker, preserving exactly the evidence the original guard existed to protect. Production was unblocked by removing the stale marker (retained as `gateway-marker.stale-20260827.json`) and restarting. Files: `apps/gateway/src/index.ts`, `apps/gateway/src/start-recovery.test.ts`.

- Two canvas features were fully written, documented and unit-tested — and called by nothing. `restoreHistory` had no call site, so a bound node with a real server-side conversation rendered 还没有对话。发第一条消息，唤醒这个 agent。 after every reload: an absent history presented as "nothing was ever said". And `node.preview`, which the glance LOD renders and `canvasDoc` documents as persisted for exactly that purpose, was never written, so every glance tile read blank after a reload. Both are now wired through the tile. Three traps handled: the restore effect must not depend on the history state (setting `loading` re-runs it, and the re-run's cleanup aborted the fetch it had just started, stranding `loading` with nothing to retry); it must not be abortable at all, since the transcript store outlives the component by design and cancelling on unmount would strand a tile the user merely panned away from; and the preview is reported only once the stream settles, so a document save does not fire on every delta frame. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/SessionTile.tsx`, `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-tile-history.test.tsx`.

- A stored canvas document that PARSED but was not readable no longer wipes the user's graph. The corrupt-payload protection only covered `JSON.parse` throwing; anything past that fell through to `sanitizeDocument`, which is deliberately lenient and returns an EMPTY document — and the surface persists whatever it loaded on mount. So a value at the canvas key that was a bare scalar, an array, an object with no `nodes` array, or an object whose every node failed sanitisation (a shape from some other/older writer) became an empty canvas AND overwrote the original: graph gone, no backup, no message, which is precisely what that module's own header says must never happen. Readability is now checked past the parse — not-a-document and total sanitisation loss are both treated exactly like a parse failure (original preserved to the backup key, reported, status `corrupt`), while PARTIAL loss keeps the survivors (dropping them too would turn a small defect into a total wipe) and still preserves the original so the dropped nodes stay recoverable. The array case is what caught the first cut of the fix: `[1,2,3]` is `typeof 'object'`, so a naive object check let it through and it still returned `ok`. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/canvasDoc.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-core.test.ts`.

- The run lock now covers wiring. Move, resize, delete and add were all gated on `running`, but connecting and cutting wires were not — the one structural edit left live mid-run. A run executes a SNAPSHOT of the graph, so a wire cut during it changes nothing about what is executing; it just leaves the user looking at a graph that no longer matches the timeline describing the run, reasonably expecting it to have had an effect. Ports stay VISIBLE while locked (they are the graph's structure, not a control) and simply stop accepting input. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-run-lock.test.tsx`.

- Stopping a run reads as a stop instead of `出错：aborted`. The SSE client turns an aborted fetch into an error frame whose detail is the internal token `aborted`; both transports rendered it verbatim, so pressing Stop left the tile reporting a failure — contradicting the timeline (which correctly says 已停止 from the abort signal) and leaking an English internal string into a Chinese transcript. Both the manual composer path and the run-mirror path now write 已停止（本轮由你中止）with an info tone; genuine transport errors are untouched. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/sessionTransport.ts`, `apps/web/src/canvas/runTransport.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-sessions.test.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-run.test.ts`.

- A ROOT node is no longer told to use upstream input it never received. `buildNodeMessage` always closed with 请基于以上前置输入…, including for nodes with no incoming wires — pointing an agent at preconditions that do not exist invites it to invent the context it was just told to rely on, degrading output on every entry node of every workflow, which is the opposite of what wiring a graph is for. Root nodes are now told plainly that they have none. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/runGraph.ts`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-run.test.ts`.

- The canvas empty state no longer dead-ends on the bind path. Binding a node needs a LIVE company and the canvas creates none (the old fused canvas did, via double-click; the session canvas double-click adds an LLM node), so with an empty world the inspector said 先创建公司 and offered nowhere to go — the first thing a new user hits. The hint now carries an inline 去创建公司 hand-off to the team surface; hosts that do not wire it still get the explanation, just without navigation. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/InspectorPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-panels.test.tsx`.

- An unset runtime prompts instead of rendering a blank trigger. `Dropdown` falls back to the raw value when no option matches, so with nothing chosen the RuntimePicker trigger rendered an empty strip — and on the inspector that control is the FIRST step of binding a node, where a blank strip reads as a broken widget. It now shows 选择 Runtime… / `Select a runtime…`, but only where empty means "nothing chosen": under `allowInherit` the empty value is a real choice with its own label and is left alone. Files: `apps/web/src/RuntimePicker.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/runtime-picker.test.tsx`.

- Graph runs go through the executor the tests actually cover. `createGatewayExecutor` existed, documented the per-agent serialization contract and was exercised by tests — but nothing in production called it; `CanvasSurface` hand-rolled its own, which had already drifted to keying serialization on `agentId` alone instead of company+agent. The tested code was not the running code, and the running code was the weaker of the two. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`.

- Two session-canvas defects caught by driving the canvas in a real browser rather than only in jsdom, both of which stranded the user in the state they are most likely to be in. (1) The tile action row (配置 / 聚焦 / 删除) rendered only at the `open` level-of-detail, so **focusing a node removed the only route to the inspector** — and focus is exactly where an unbound node has to be bound, leaving a fresh node permanently unconfigurable. Actions now ride `open` AND `focus`. (2) `focusNode` / `fitAll` bailed out whenever the observed viewport size was still `0`. That size comes from a mount measurement plus a `ResizeObserver`, and both can legitimately report `0` at the moment of the request — RO callbacks land on frame boundaries, which stall in a backgrounded/non-compositing tab — so ⌘E and fit-all silently did nothing with no way to tell why. They now fall back to measuring the canvas root live, and no-op only when there is genuinely no layout. Also dropped a duplicated `canvas-tile--focus` class (the LOD class already carries focus, since `lodFor` short-circuits on it). Verification: new `apps/web/tests/canvas-tile.test.tsx` (action row at both levels, single focus class, unbound composer states its reason and refuses to send, bound composer sends) and `apps/web/tests/canvas-surface-fit.test.tsx` (focus works under a never-delivering `ResizeObserver`; still no-ops when nothing is laid out) — **each verified to FAIL with its fix reverted**, then green: 620 passed across 52 files, `tsc --noEmit` clean, build succeeds. Files: `apps/web/src/canvas/SessionTile.tsx`, `apps/web/src/canvas/CanvasSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-tile.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/canvas-surface-fit.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Creative Canvas now mounts its previously unreachable multi-track timeline and persists it independently alongside the node document. Node drags commit as one undo step and roll back on pointer cancellation; timeline cross-track dragging works under pointer capture; locked tracks cannot delete clips; trims keep source out-points aligned; muted tracks are omitted from render plans; and empty timelines report their real zero duration. Defensive document, timeline, render-config, metadata, and frame-conversion validation now rejects malformed, duplicate, incompatible, non-finite, or unsupported persisted/runtime values instead of allowing corrupted state or invalid renders. Clean install reliability was restored by repairing missing optional lockfile entries. Verification: Web 70 test files / 765 tests plus static UI and production build; Creative Canvas clean `npm ci`, TypeScript, 66 focused tests, production build, browser interaction smoke, and a real 180-frame Remotion MP4 render; MyShell Studio 14 frontend tests/build; Fleet Canvas TypeScript/build. Files: `apps/creative-canvas/**`, `apps/web/tests/creative-canvas-*.test.*`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Desktop/frozen writes through BOTH front doors no longer lose their request body (the `/api/chat/stream returned 400` outage), with the fix now applied uniformly to the gateway front door too. Root cause (verified against httpx 0.28.1): the co-launched Node control plane reads a **chunked** request body as EMPTY. In the PyInstaller-frozen desktop runtime (and behind a body-replaying Starlette `BaseHTTPMiddleware`) the inbound `Content-Length` is dropped from the ASGI scope; the front door forwarded the write body as a stream with no length, so httpx framed it `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` → Node saw an empty body → every write (chat / create-company / hire) 400'd while reads (no body) proxied fine. The prior `_ALWAYS_BODY_METHODS` fix made the body forward but still streamed it length-less, so it did NOT resolve the 400; and `gateway_front_door.py` (`/gateway-api/*`, a SECOND wired front door) carried the identical bug entirely unpatched. Fix: a shared `_prepare_forward_body` (in `node_front_door.py`, used by both front doors) guarantees a forwarded write body is ALWAYS `Content-Length` framed, never chunked — when a single valid inbound length survives it streams and restores that exact length (memory-efficient; httpx honours it and does not chunk), otherwise it buffers the body once, bounded by `_MAX_BUFFERED_BODY_BYTES` (64 MiB → `413` beyond it, never OOMing the proxy), and lets httpx set an accurate length from the bytes. `_declared_content_length` rejects ambiguous framing (absent / negative / malformed / duplicate-conflicting `Content-Length`, or `Transfer-Encoding` co-present per RFC 7230 §3.3.3) by falling back to buffer-and-recompute — a safe normalization behind uvicorn (the real HTTP parser), not a smuggling surface. A mid-upload client disconnect during the eager buffered read is caught in both front doors (`_ClientDisconnected` → log + return), mirroring the streaming path. Impact: request-body forwarding only; the SSE / response streaming path is unchanged. Verification: `tests/test_node_front_door.py`, `tests/test_gateway_front_door.py`, and a NEW `tests/test_front_door_body_wire.py` — a **real-wire** regression (a real `httpx.AsyncClient` over a real loopback TCP socket, inspecting the raw upstream bytes) asserting forwarded writes are `Content-Length` framed and never chunked, AND pinning the exact httpx behaviour the fix guards against (stream + no length → chunked); `MockTransport` cannot reproduce chunked wire framing, which is why the outage shipped green. Front-door tests pass; `ruff check packages apps` clean. Frozen `.app` live smoke is the release-acceptance step (source-green ≠ frozen-works). Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — Antigravity PASS; Codex R1 rejected (uncaught `_ClientDisconnected` on the buffered path, wire test untracked, stale module docstring, missing this CHANGELOG entry), all fixed and re-reviewed. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/node_front_door.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/gateway_front_door.py`, `tests/test_node_front_door.py`, `tests/test_gateway_front_door.py`, `tests/test_front_door_body_wire.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Local PR-gate tests now match the current `dev/server-refactor` API/Web surfaces. What changed: request-logging tests promote dynamically added test routes ahead of the SPA fallback so they still exercise 500 and streaming middleware paths, and the Web static surface test now checks the current Fusion evidence bundle / eval report markers instead of removed run-artifact and adversarial endpoint strings. Why: the PR gate should validate the source-of-truth branch as it exists now, not fail on test harness route order or UI surfaces intentionally removed by the server-refactor trunk. Impact: tests only. Verification: `pytest -q tests/test_request_logging.py tests/test_web_static.py`, `ruff check packages apps tests/test_request_logging.py tests/test_web_static.py`, `scripts/run-ci-tests.sh`, `superclaw doctor`, API `/health`, `npm ci --prefix apps/web`, `npm test --prefix apps/web`, and `npm run build --prefix apps/web`.

- ClawWork chat no longer "hangs" ~120s before failing, and a default-model ClawWork chat now actually runs. Root cause (reproduced via a source uvicorn server — packaging-independent): ClawWork's RPC loop ACKs every prompt with `{"type":"response","command":"prompt","success":bool}`; on `success:false` (it refused the prompt — no API key / no model selected) it then stays ALIVE in RPC mode awaiting the next command, emitting neither `agent_end` nor EOF. `ClawWorkBackend._spawn_rpc`'s read loop only recognized `tool_execution_*` / `agent_end` / process-exit as terminal, so a rejected prompt spun the loop until the run budget expired (a 40–120s phantom "hang" that surfaced only as a bare `timeout`). Three fixes: (1) **terminal-on-reject** — `_spawn_rpc` now treats `success is False` (strict, never a missing field; `success:true` stays a mere ACK that the turn's `agent_start`/…/`agent_end` follow) as an immediate terminal failure, returning `prompt_rejected` + the error tail; `run()` maps `prompt_rejected` to a fail-closed exit 125 (no completion marker, never billed completed), mirroring `model_error`/`ungoverned`. (2) **default relay tier** — ClawWork's only provider is the relay (`clawrelay`), which REQUIRES a package model and has no usable "no model" fallback; unlike the native CLI backends, an unset model produced no `--model` and the exact rejection above. `run()` now defaults the model to the base relay tier (`relay_packages.SUPERCLAW_BRIDGE_TIERS[0]` = `core`) when neither a per-run override nor `SUPERCLAW_CLAWWORK_MODEL` is set — so the composer's "use the relay default" package option (which sends `model:''`) and a worker with no model both actually run on `core` instead of being rejected; an explicit override still wins, and the standard tier→slug map stays contract-constant / network-free. The shared contract was corrected so the CLI/API/Web display default matches what executes (zero-drift, 铁律 2): `ui_contracts.AGENT_CONTROL_SPECS["clawwork"]["default_model"]` AND the `runtime_config` `SUPERCLAW_CLAWWORK_MODEL` spec default both moved from the placeholder `"configured-default"` to `"core"` (mirroring how claude pins both to `claude-opus-4-8`), pinned by a test against the kernel constant. (3) **diagnosable timeout** — a genuine ClawWork chat timeout now folds the child's stderr/output tail into the failure message instead of a bare `timeout: … exceeded Ns`, which is what a packaged-app diagnosis needs. Verification: `tests/test_worker_backends.py` (rejected-prompt terminal-not-hang with an injected 3s budget + behavioural `prompt_rejected` proof — no wall-clock assertion; `success:true` ACK is NOT short-circuited; `run()` maps prompt_rejected→125; default-no-model→`--model superclaw-core`; contract↔kernel default no-drift guard), `tests/test_clawwork_native_chat.py` (timeout surfaces stderr tail / falls back to output tail), and the existing clawwork worker/native/session suites — 264 passed, 1 opt-in skip; ruff clean; plus an end-to-end source-uvicorn + bundled-binary + real-relay check: `model:''` (real composer shape) now completes in ~7s instead of hanging. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — Codex R1 rejected (contract `default_model` drift, missing CHANGELOG gate, and a real-subprocess `sleep(30)`+`budget=20` test violating the repo's no-wall-clock discipline); all fixed and re-reviewed to no-blocking-findings. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `tests/test_clawwork_native_chat.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Markdown link styling in chat replies (Web): links were `color: var(--border-focus)`, which is pure `#000` (light) / `#f4f4f4` (dark) — i.e. body-text colour — so a link read as heavy fully-underlined black/white text with no real link affordance. Switched to a muted, warm burnt-orange — dedicated link colours (NOT `--accent`, so buttons/focus/heading ticks stay indigo): `#9e5a2a` in light (~5.3:1 on white) and `#e0a06a` in dark (~8:1 on the chat bg), both clearing AA. The underline (currentColor, 70% at rest → full on hover) stays clearly visible as the non-colour cue (WCAG 1.4.1). A low-saturation, warm link per common content-UI practice. List markers also dropped to `--text-tertiary` so data/source lists read as content rather than bold bullets. Presentation-only; theme-aware via the existing tokens. Verification: `npm run build`; live demo confirmed the link color/decoration via computed styles in both light and dark and a clean source-list render. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) both no-blocking-findings. Files: `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Chat transcript UI polish (Web), addressing five issues raised after the live-status/streaming rework shipped. (1) Waiting felt dull: the running indicator is now a `ChatLiveStatus` component that prefers the REAL stage (tool → reasoning → generating) and, only when no signal has arrived, gently rotates "正在思考 / 正在梳理上下文 / 正在串联线索 / …" so it never looks frozen; the activity word carries a pure-CSS gradient shimmer plus an animated "…", with a `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback. (2) Streaming Markdown felt janky: `StreamingAssistantMarkdown` now batches SSE deltas to ONE parse per `requestAnimationFrame` (smooth, paint-aligned — replacing the steppy 60ms interval) and renders through `useDeferredValue` so a long reply degrades to "Markdown lags a frame" instead of blocking input/scroll. The pending frame is never cancelled per-delta (that starved the flush on sub-frame streams and froze the UI) — only on unmount; the component is keyed by turn identity (`run_id`/`startedAt`) so live state can't bleed across sessions even when a new reply shares a prefix with the old (the prior structural-chunking approach was dropped because splitting Markdown at blank lines corrupts loose lists / blockquotes / nested fences — O(n) chunked parse is filed as a perf backlog gated on a real long-reply metric). `closeStreamingMarkdown` now closes an open `~~~` fence too, not just ```` ``` ````. (3) Hover action buttons overlapped the last line: `.chat-turn-actions` moved from `position:absolute; bottom:-2px` (which sat on the text) to an in-flow grid row with a small reserved gutter, revealed on hover/focus, per-role aligned — geometrically below the content, no overlap, no layout shift. (4) Replies lost visual hierarchy after the left rule was removed: top-level `> h2`/`> h3` in an assistant reply get a short accent tick (not the old full-height bar; scoped to direct children so a heading inside a blockquote doesn't stack two left rules) plus more heading contrast and paragraph spacing. (5) The loading spinner wobbled off-centre: `.spin` now sets `transform-box: fill-box; transform-origin: center` (lucide's SVG otherwise rotated around the layout box) so every spinner turns concentrically. A11y: the visual indicator is `aria-hidden`; a sibling `sr-only role="status"` announces only the stable stage (no per-second 用时 / no rotating-phrase spam). Verification: `npm run build`; `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx` 132 passed; live demo confirmed the spinner `transform-box`/shimmer/dots via computed styles and the action row below the content (no overlap). Adversarial review across rounds: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — R1 both rejected (rAF starvation under fast streams, cross-session stale, chunk stale-handler closure, Markdown-structure-breaking chunk boundaries, `aria-live` announcement spam); all fixed and both signed off (the structural-chunking perf optimization is a documented non-blocking backlog). Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Added

- Appearance / color-scheme management — Codex/VSCode-style curated **presets** + a **custom** palette for the Web surface, persisted as a kernel-owned config file (`~/.superclaw/appearance.json`, env `SUPERCLAW_APPEARANCE_CONFIG_PATH`) that is importable/exportable. Implemented kernel-first (铁律 1/3): the new `superclaw.appearance` module is the SINGLE source of truth for the preset catalog (`APPEARANCE_PRESETS`: default / emerald / amber / rose / high-contrast / oled), the editable-token whitelist (`APPEARANCE_TOKENS`: ~10 semantic anchors — accent / surfaces / text / status), and all validation. A color scheme only OVERRIDES tokens on top of the existing light/dark canvas (`data-theme` is untouched). Persistence reuses the shell-config lock + `tempfile.mkstemp`→`os.replace` atomic discipline; validation is fail-closed (`_normalize_hex` rejects non-hex, unknown presets/tokens rejected, imported junk dropped with warnings, corrupt file → default). Surfaces are thin and contract-driven: CLI `superclaw appearance list-presets|show|set-preset|set-color|reset|export|import`; Python API `GET /api/appearance`, `POST /api/appearance/set`, `POST /api/appearance/custom-color` (single-token ATOMIC merge — never a whole-map replace from stale client state, so concurrent edits can't clobber), `GET /api/appearance/export`, `POST /api/appearance/import` (all `require_control_token`, `ValueError`→400); Web `apps/web/src/appearance.ts` resolves the active overrides into CSS vars (deriving `--*-soft` rgba from the contract's `soft_alpha`) and injects them on `documentElement`, with a Settings → Preferences color-scheme panel (preset cards + per-token color pickers + import/export). CSS-injection-safe: token keys come only from the trusted kernel contract, every user value must pass the exact hex regex before `style.setProperty`. The FOUC `localStorage` cache holds ONLY a pre-rendered, re-validated CSS-var snapshot (never the contract) so it can never act as a second source of truth. Known limitation (non-blocking): custom colors apply to the super UI; the embedded board (`BoardThemeBridge`) and the desktop shell still follow only the light/dark base. Verification: `tests/test_appearance.py` 22 passed (kernel validation / lock+atomic / export-import roundtrip / junk-drop / corrupt-file fallback / API get-set-export-import-custom-color / 401 / 400), `tests/test_cli_api_parity.py` 5 passed, `apps/web/tests/appearance.test.ts` (hex helpers / resolve / apply set+clear on jsdom / validated cache / injection-drop), `npm run build`; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — Codex R1 flagged a lost-update race on concurrent custom-color edits (fixed with the atomic `custom-color` endpoint), the FOUC cache acting as a second source of truth (fixed by caching only a validated resolved-var snapshot), and the missing CHANGELOG/CLI-doc gate (this entry + README); all fixed and re-reviewed. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/appearance.py`, `ui_contracts.py`, `cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/appearance.ts`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css`, `tests/test_appearance.py`, `apps/web/tests/appearance.test.ts`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Board-inbox (the human-escalation queue) is now manageable from chat, closing the last P2 operational-write gap in the company control plane. The board inbox holds durable `ESCALATE_TO_BOARD` `IssueThreadInteraction`s awaiting an operator decision; it was already on the CLI (`team board-inbox`) and REST (`/api/team/board-inbox`) but not chat. New chat tools: `board_inbox.resolve` (clear an escalation without touching its issue — idempotent), `board_inbox.assign` (assign the escalation's issue to an agent through the same gate as `issue.assign`, then optionally resolve), and the read tool `board_inbox.list` (see the queue). All route through the SAME `execute_company_command` / `execute_company_read` choke points + 6-gate fail-closed pipeline as every other company op. **Operator-only by construction**: the two write commands are NOT enumerated in the autonomy gate's confined-allowed set, so a confined (non-operator) agent hits the existing DEFAULT-DENY → `AutonomyGateError` — an agent must not clear/redirect its own escalation to the human board. Both are reversible queue management → LOW risk (direct, no approval). Scope resolves the escalation's company via its ISSUE (authoritative, not the interaction's denormalised copy); `board_inbox.assign` enforces B8 nested-ref same-origin (issue company == assignee company, with `assign_issue`'s own cross-company guard as backstop); the lifecycle gate refuses a frozen company (B5, on every touched company). **Single source**: the mutation logic was consolidated into `team_kernel.resolve_board_inbox_item` / `assign_board_inbox_item` (idempotent resolve; assign requires the item be PENDING; assignment goes through `assign_issue`), and the CLI + REST were migrated to call them — so the "only a board item, only when pending, assign through the assignment gate" rules can no longer drift between surfaces. `state.get_issue_interaction` (bounded PK lookup) replaces the old O(n) `list_issue_interactions(limit=10000)` scans; `state.list_board_inbox_items` is store-bounded (company column + `json_extract` policy filter, capped) and `board_inbox.list` reports truncation honestly (fetches cap+1). **Governance ruling (owner, 2026-06-26)**: workspace create/trust/containment is DELIBERATELY not exposed to chat — trust/containment decide an agent's filesystem reach + sandbox tier, so "move the boundary" stays a human ceremony (CLI `workspace trust` + the desktop trust dialog), outside the agent-reachable command set; recorded as a code comment, no command added. 24 write + 11 read company chat tools. Verification: `tests/test_company_board_inbox.py` (contract/risk/dispatch/idempotency/operator-only-confined-denied/same-origin/frozen/unknown-fail-closed/read-scoping) + count bumps in `test_company_tools` (22→24), `test_company_commands` (`_ALL_MODELS`), `test_company_read` (`_EXPECTED_READ_TOOLS`); the company command/read/scope/risk/handler/autonomy/lifecycle/mcp-proxy/cli/api/parity/state suites pass; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — Codex R1 flagged a false-positive `capped` flag (exactly-cap rows reported truncated) and the changelog gate, both fixed; its stale-base findings (the branch was 6 commits behind `origin/main`) were resolved by absorbing `origin/main`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/team_kernel.py`, `state.py`, `company_commands.py`, `company_scope.py`, `company_handler.py`, `company_risk.py`, `company_read.py`, `ui_contracts.py`, `cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_company_board_inbox.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Workshop uploads now SIGN submissions with the developer's escrowed key (`capabilities workshop submit-review`). Previously every upload went out unsigned (`signature_verified=False`), so a clean user machine fail-closed when installing a remote plugin. What changed: a new `capability_devtools.sign_capability_submission` signs the canonical `trust_contracts.capability_signed_core` (kind/capability_id/version/package_digest/SANITIZED artifact_ref) with the developer's Ed25519 key via `sign_envelope`, and `build_review_submission_payload`/`submit_capability_for_review` attach `signature`/`signer_keyid` — the exact pair ClawHunt re-verifies against the registered public key (cross-repo byte-identical signed core, pinned by the existing golden test). The CLI `submit-review` gains `--sign/--no-sign` (default **sign**): it recovers the account's escrowed signing key via `_resolve_workshop_signing_key`, which reuses `developer_identity._read_local_key_material` (0600 self-heal + ed25519 format validation + corrupt-key fall-through to a lazy `ensure_developer_key_registered`), and on a missing key points the user at the real `clawhunt account-login` command. Fail-closed throughout: a malformed private key, or a PARTIAL signature (only one of `signature`/`signer_keyid`), raises rather than silently downgrading to unsigned; the key resolve + lazy ensure run inside the command's `try` so a network/OS error normalizes to an error line, never a raw traceback. The "create" half (login-time escrow key mint/recover via `/v1/capabilities/developers/key/ensure`) was already implemented and is unchanged. Behavior change: `submit-review` signs by default — the two ClawHunt workshop verifiers that assert the UNSIGNED contract (`signature_verified is False`) were updated to pass `--no-sign` explicitly. Verification: `tests/test_workshop_submit_signing.py` (cross-repo verify round-trip, artifact_ref binding tamper→reject, secret-scan-safe payload, fail-closed malformed key, partial-signature raise, CliRunner default-sign / `--no-sign` / missing-key-with-correct-command paths) + `test_capability_workshop_devtools.py` updated; ruff clean. Adversarial review across rounds: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — both rejected R1 (partial-signature silent downgrade, lazy-ensure raw traceback, wrong `login-password` hint command, raw `read_text` bypassing the permission-self-heal/recovery reader, and a ClawHunt verifier regression under the new default); all fixed and signed off. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_devtools.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_workshop_submit_signing.py`, `tests/test_capability_workshop_devtools.py`, `CHANGELOG.md` (ClawHunt side, separate repo: `scripts/verify_capability_workshop_local_superclaw_cli.py`, `scripts/verify_capability_workshop_staging.py`).

- Local worktree-isolation guards — optional Git hooks that give the "parallel work must be worktree-isolated" constitution rule teeth, without any GitHub/platform gate (so [[no-github-branch-protection]] is untouched). What it does: `.githooks/pre-commit` **refuses a feature-branch commit in the PRIMARY checkout** and `.githooks/post-checkout` **warns** when the primary switches to a feature branch — the exact misuse that let a parallel session stash/clobber another's uncommitted WIP (composer WIP + a chat-UI edit were both lost from the shared primary checkout this way). Install once per clone: `sh scripts/install-local-guards.sh`. Self-gating so it never blocks unrelated work: it fires ONLY when (a) the repo root carries the tracked marker `.githooks/.superclaw-guard` (so a global `core.hooksPath` can't leak the guard into other repos), (b) it's the PRIMARY working tree — `git-dir == git-common-dir`, resolved via `cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" && pwd` so it works on old Git instead of silently failing open (a linked worktree differs → exits 0, never restricted), (c) HEAD resolves to a branch — a detached HEAD (which is where an in-progress rebase lives) is left alone, so history maintenance is free, and (d) the branch isn't `main` or a user-configured `guard.allowBranch` glob (e.g. `git config --add guard.allowBranch 'work/main-latest-*'` for a local main-mirror; matched with `set -f` so the pattern can't be pathname-expanded). It is installed under BOTH `pre-commit` and `pre-merge-commit`, so a clean (no-conflict) `git merge` on a feature branch — which auto-commits and skips `pre-commit` — is caught too; a cherry-pick's clean auto-commit is an accepted residual (rare/deliberate; this is a poka-yoke, not a security lock). `git commit --no-verify` is the rare deliberate override. The installer is non-destructive: it refuses to overwrite a foreign same-named hook (husky/lint/secret-scan), refuses to write into a `core.hooksPath` outside this repo's git dir, is idempotent, and auto-allows the current `work/main-latest-*` mirror branch. Verification: a re-runnable `scripts/test-local-guards.sh` builds a throwaway repo and asserts the covered gate branches (documenting the cherry-pick residual rather than asserting it) — main commit allowed, feature commit blocked, a clean (auto-committing) `git merge` that lands a commit ON a feature branch blocked via `pre-merge-commit` (a clean `cherry-pick` auto-commit is NOT caught — the documented accepted residual), commit allowed on detached HEAD (covers in-progress rebase), main-mirror commit allowed via `guard.allowBranch`, the installer refuses to overwrite a foreign hook and refuses a missing external `core.hooksPath`, and a linked-worktree feature commit allowed. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) across rounds — R1–R3 surfaced and rejected real defects, each fixed and re-verified: destructive hook overwrite; rebase/detached false-block; old-Git silent fail-open; global-`core.hooksPath` cross-repo leak and a `..`-path containment bypass; machine-specific allow-list; a dead-end hint command; a history-maintenance bypass that let a clean feature-branch `git merge` commit through `pre-commit` (fixed by also installing `pre-merge-commit`); an installer false-success when `core.hooksPath` pointed at a missing external dir; an unquoted `guard.allowBranch` pattern vulnerable to pathname expansion (fixed with `set -f`); and a BRE `grep` idempotency bug that re-added the mirror pattern on every run (fixed with `grep -Fqx`). Files: `.githooks/pre-commit`, `.githooks/post-checkout`, `.githooks/.superclaw-guard`, `scripts/install-local-guards.sh`, `scripts/test-local-guards.sh`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- `superclaw migrate-home`: a one-time maintenance command that relocates legacy cwd-relative `.superclaw/` data into the HOME data root (`~/.superclaw`), so data created before the data-root change actually leaves an iCloud-synced repo checkout. What it does: from `./.superclaw` (or `--from <dir>`) it moves only the known HOME data roots — `state.db`/`telemetry.db` plus their WAL/SHM/journal sidecars, and the `artifacts`/`plugins`/`companies`/`registry`/`capabilities`/`evals`/`keys` trees — into `superclaw_home()`. It is an ALLOWLIST move: repo/workspace-bound trees that legitimately stay in the repo (`chat-attachments` — read inside the backend's repo sandbox, `worktrees` — git-worktree semantics, and the fusion/acceptance reports) are reported as `skip` and left in place. It NEVER overwrites an existing destination (those names are reported as `conflict` and left untouched, re-checked at move time with `os.path.lexists` so a broken symlink at the destination also counts as a conflict), NEVER migrates a symlinked data root (reported as `symlink` and left in place — moving the link would leave HOME pointing back at the original and import an untrusted link), refuses nested source/destination, supports `--dry-run` (mutates nothing) and `--json`, and is a no-op when the source is already the HOME root or absent. SQLite DBs migrate as ATOMIC families — `state.db`/`telemetry.db`/`streamtest-state.db` and their WAL/SHM/journal sidecars move together; if any family member conflicts/symlinks the whole family stays put, and a partial move failure rolls the family back — so a DB and its sidecars are never split (which could orphan committed WAL data). The rollback is safe under a cross-filesystem partial move (`shutil.move` = copy-then-unlink across filesystems): if the source survived it only clears the partial/duplicate at the destination (never overwrites the intact source); if the source was already gone it moves the destination copy back into the free source slot. Rollback touches ONLY command-owned members (the ones it moved, plus a member whose own move failed mid-copy) — never a foreign object that appeared at the destination since planning — and attempts every member without short-circuiting. CLI-only by design (a local filesystem migration; the CLI is the source of truth and no client surface needs it); operators should stop any running `superclaw service`/`desktop` first so the state DB isn't open during the move. Verification: `tests/test_migrate_home.py` (dry-run plan buckets, real move of data roots while repo-bound trees stay, never-overwrite conflict, no-op when absent / source==HOME) + `tests/test_cli.py` 131 passed; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini) both no-blocking-findings. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_migrate_home.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Promoted Fusion to a first-class sidebar entry with per-surface run controls (Web). What changed: the workbench rail now has a Fusion button alongside Plugins and Team (no longer buried under Settings → Open Fusion), opening the Fusion workspace and lazily loading run state.
