Each imported product card (OSIRIS / Open Design / OpenPencil) gains a Start/Stop button and a live status badge wired to the kernel run-lifecycle API (`/api/fusion/start|stop` with `execute`, `/api/fusion/run-status`); "Open surface" is disabled until the surface is confirmed running. Presentation-only: all docker orchestration stays in the kernel (the Web only calls the API, zero new semantics), run state loads lazily on entering the workspace (no docker probe at app start), and the component→profile map mirrors `FUSION_PROFILES`. Fail-closed throughout: a structured execution failure (HTTP 200 with `execution.ok=false`) surfaces an explicit error message mirroring the CLI's non-zero exit; a failed run-status (HTTP 200 `ok=false` or a rejected fetch) shows `status unavailable: <status>` (never a fake "not running"); the pre-load window shows `checking…`. Verification: `npm run build`, `npm test` 302 passed + `static-ui` source assertions, and live preview against a worktree API confirming the sidebar entry, card controls, the docker-`failed` and 401-`unreachable` error badges, disabled Open surface, and a clean console. Adversarial review across rounds: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Gemini both no-blocking-findings. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Fixed

- Hardened the native-skill projection flow (create → sync → runtime) on four fronts after an adversarial review of the end-to-end path. (A) **Collision/force safety** (`skill_sync.sync_native_skills`): `--force` could silently overwrite or delete a same-named skill SuperClaw never projected (a user's own / another tool's `~/.claude|.codex|.gemini/skills/<slug>`), and the per-file collision check could inject our asset files into a stranger's same-named directory. Now a directory-atomic foreign guard holds back EVERY file of a skill whose `SKILL.md` slot exists without a projection-ledger record — even under `--force` (force only re-asserts SuperClaw's OWN projections, it never seizes unrecorded files). Also fixed a latent bug where the "unchanged" check trusted the ledger digest (`existing.render_digest == render_digest`) so `--force` failed to restore a hand-edited projection when the store content was unchanged; it now compares the on-disk file digest. The `--force` help text was corrected to match. (B) **Revocation epoch** (`capability_surface.collect_revocation_epoch`): the resume guard digested only the plugin revocation list, so revoking a native skill (its own `~/.superclaw/skills/revocations.json`) did not register as a capability-surface change and an already-projected, now-revoked `SKILL.md` kept loading in-session with no notice. The epoch now folds in BOTH lists (domain-separated so an entry moving between lists can't collide); all `current_capability_surface` callers benefit automatically. (C) **Honest receipt** (`skill_author.created_skill_receipt`, used by `chat_turn` + `apps/api/main`): the chat confirmation said "Created … (governed; usable across runtimes)" — overstating, since a created skill is registered in the store but NOT active in any runtime until an explicit `skill sync`, and ClawWork/anthropic-agent have no native skill discovery. The message is now a single shared source ("Registered … saved to your skill library. Not active in any runtime yet — run `superclaw skill sync` …"), eliminating the two-surface drift. (D) **Drift visibility**: added read-only `skill_sync.native_skill_status` and `superclaw skill status [--skill --target --json]`, reporting one row per (skill, target) — `ok`/`not_projected`/`foreign_collision`/`drift_local_edit`/`drift_store_updated`/`missing_projection`/`stale_revoked` — aggregated worst-first over SKILL.md AND every asset (an asset-only edit/deletion is no longer masked as `ok`); native projections are deliberately left byte-faithful (no in-file marker) to keep frontmatter discovery and the authored prose intact. Deferred by owner decision (backlog, not bugs): auto-sync on create, and native skill support for ClawWork/anthropic-agent. Verification: `tests/test_skill_sync.py` (foreign-not-overwritten-even-with-force, no-asset-injection-into-foreign-dir, force-reasserts-own-hand-edit, status full lifecycle + asset-drift + foreign + single-row stale), `tests/test_capability_surface.py` (epoch folds in native list + domain separation), `tests/test_skill_author_proposal.py` (honest receipt), and the two conversational-create integration tests updated to assert the new copy + negative-assert the retired wording; relevant suites green, ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) — both flagged the `native_skill_status` asset-drift false-`ok` and the duplicate `stale_revoked` rows, fixed before sign-off (agy's two CLI "blocking" claims — `_default_skill_revocation_file` NameError / missing `json` import — were verified false: aliased at `cli.py` import and `json` imported; `skill status` executes clean). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/skill_sync.py`, `capability_surface.py`, `skill_author.py`, `chat_turn.py`, `cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_skill_sync.py`, `tests/test_capability_surface.py`, `tests/test_skill_author_proposal.py`, `tests/test_chat_conversational_create.py`, `tests/test_chat_stream_conversational_create.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Desktop app no longer shows a white window because the backend "never became ready". Root cause: the shipped ad-hoc-signed PyInstaller onefile backend pays a one-time macOS Gatekeeper/`syspolicyd` assessment of its bundled native dylibs on the first COLD launch (a fresh `_MEI...` extraction whose dylib cdhashes are not yet assessed), which blocks the early `dlopen` (e.g. `_pydantic_core`) for ~10–20s+ on a contended host before the interpreter starts. The launcher's readiness budget was 10s, so it killed a backend that was merely cold-starting and the GUI rendered blank. What changed: the desktop boot-readiness timeout default is raised from 10s to 60s and made overridable via `SUPERCLAW_DESKTOP_BOOT_TIMEOUT` (a finite positive float, capped at 600s; `inf`/`nan`/≤0/non-numeric fall back to the default). The timeout is a CEILING, not a fixed wait — `desktop start` polls `/health` every 0.1s and returns the instant the backend answers (warm starts stay sub-second) and still converges to an error in bounded time for a genuinely dead backend. The fix is applied at the ACTUAL shipped entry too: the frozen desktop shim (`apps/desktop/backend/superclaw_service.py`) now passes `--boot-timeout` through as `None` when the GUI omits it (instead of hardcoding 10s), so the env/default resolver governs; and the Rust packaged workbench smoke's hardcoded 10s gate is aligned to the 30s runtime-smoke budget. Verification: `tests/test_desktop_runtime.py` 54 passed (incl. 6 new: default=60, env override, explicit-wins-over-env, bad/`inf` env falls back, supervisor uses resolved value) + `test_desktop_backend_shim`/`test_cli`/`test_cli_api_parity` green; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) flagged that the first fix missed the frozen shim entry + accepted `inf` + missed the workbench-smoke gate + the env-var config-surface; all fixed before sign-off; Antigravity (Gemini) PASS. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/desktop_runtime.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/desktop/backend/superclaw_service.py`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `.env.example`, `tests/test_desktop_runtime.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- ClawHunt marketplace dock now displays without a login, and the CLI/API browse is context-aware. What changed: the chat dock's `/api/clawhunt/tasks[/{id}]` and `clawhunt browse`/`detail` now go through a new kernel helper `ClawHuntClient.browse_marketplace` / `get_problem_marketplace` that picks the endpoint by linked credentials — with NO agent key it browses ClawHunt's public, anonymous web board (`GET /api/problems/`, no `Authorization` header) so the market shows without connecting an account; with a key it keeps the agent-gated, personalized listing and the privileged post-claim detail (`GET /api/v1/problems[/{id}]`). `--public`/`--agent` force either side. Why: the dock previously hard-required an agent-platform key (account login alone never linked one), so it showed "market unavailable" for logged-out users; meanwhile a solver must still get its post-claim private fields, so the fix must not hard-route everyone to the redacted public view. Impact: behavior change — logged-out dock/CLI now show the public market; the `clawhunt browse`/`detail` default is auto (was agent-only) so it works without a key while preserving the agent view when one is linked. Fail-closed: the detail endpoint now mirrors the list — an upstream failure (CF-gate challenge / 401 / 5xx) yields `ok:false` with `task=null`, `problem_payload=null`, `raw=null`, and the Web detail loader only adopts an `ok` payload (keeping the teaser otherwise). The public list applies no post-fetch filtering, so a short page is the true end of the market (a full page reports "more" and at an exact-multiple boundary costs one extra empty fetch). Verification: ruff clean; `tests/test_clawhunt_client.py` (anonymous public list/detail send no Bearer; browse_marketplace auto-routes by key and honors the override), `tests/test_api.py` (public-shape list, full-page has_more, list + detail fail-closed), `tests/test_cli.py` (default/`--public`/`--agent` forward `public=None/True/False`); web `tsc --noEmit` adds no new errors and `npm test` 385 passed; plus a live staging check: with no key, `browse_public(limit=3)` and `get_problem_public(6)` both return 200 anonymously. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini 3.1 Pro) across rounds — the context-aware redesign and the detail fail-closed landed in response to their blocking findings before both signed off. - Unified the plugin/skill user-global state roots onto the single SuperClaw data root after merging #351 (`plugin state user-global`). What changed: `plugins.plugin_state_root()`, `plugin_config._default_plugin_config_file()`, `skill_store.default_skill_store()` / `default_skill_revocation_file()`, and `skill_sync`'s projection lock now anchor their default on `environment.superclaw_home()` (env `SUPERCLAW_HOME`) instead of a hardcoded `Path.home()/.superclaw`, so a single `SUPERCLAW_HOME` moves ALL user data together (state/telemetry/artifacts/companies/registry/… AND plugins/skills). Why: the merge left two parallel state-root designs — `superclaw_home()` (this PR) and #351's hardcoded `Path.home()/.superclaw/plugins` — that didn't move together. Preserved from #351 (verbatim behavior): the `SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_STATE_ROOT` / `SUPERCLAW_SKILL_STORE_DIR` overrides still win (layered on top), and the plugin/skill state deliberately uses `superclaw_home()` WITHOUT the cwd-legacy fallback of `superclaw_data_path()` — a cwd-relative plugin cache is exactly the sync-skills cross-cwd deletion bug #351 fixed, so plugin/skill state stays always-user-global. The 4 CLI `--lock` options (skill projection lock) were also converted from the frozen `DEFAULT_PROJECTION_LOCK` import-time snapshot to `None` + call-time resolution, so the default lock follows `SUPERCLAW_HOME` set after import (caught by review; regression test added). #351's regression test (`test_cloud_sync_governance_targets_gate_state_root`) and its conftest isolation continue to pass. Verification: ruff clean; 668 tests green across plugin/skill/cli/api/ui/catalog/team/data-root suites (#351 regression + skill provenance/sync + this PR's data-root tests). Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini) both no-blocking-findings. Files: `plugins.py`, `plugin_config.py`, `skill_store.py`, `skill_sync.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Relocated the remaining SuperClaw cwd-relative `.superclaw/` data roots to the HOME data root `~/.superclaw` (completes the full data-root sweep after state.db/telemetry.db and artifacts). What changed: the plugin governance/cache/cloud roots (`plugins/cache`, `plugins/revocations.json`, `plugins/entitlements.json`, `plugins/runtime-policy.json`, `plugins/local-config.json`, `plugins/cloud`, `plugins/submissions`, the plugin local-state root), the catalog/company/registry roots (`companies`, `registry`, `companies/revocations.json`), the capability submission store (`capabilities/submissions`), the eval root (`evals`), and the local plugin-verification-key fallback (`keys/official-root.pub`) now resolve at **runtime** under the HOME data root. Mechanism: module-level `DEFAULT_*` path constants became `default_*()` functions returning `superclaw_data_path(...)` (so the path resolves at call time honoring `SUPERCLAW_HOME`/legacy-cwd-fallback, never frozen at import); every consumer (`x or default_*()`), env-or-default helper, frozen dataclass field (`field(default_factory=...)`), and argparse default was updated; all `--revocation-file`/`--entitlement-file`/`--policy-file`/`--submission-root`/`--cloud-root`/`--local-state-root` CLI options became `None`-defaulted with in-body resolution (replacing the old sentinel-compare-to-constant pattern). All env overrides (`SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_CACHE_PATH`, `SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_CLOUD_PATH`, `SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_SUBMISSION_PATH`, `SUPERCLAW_CAPABILITY_SUBMISSION_PATH`, `SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_LOCAL_STATE_PATH`, `SUPERCLAW_EVAL_ROOT`, etc.) and every explicit flag still win. Why: same iCloud-sync off-machine hazard — governance state (revocations/entitlements/policy), catalog/registry, submissions, and evals must not default into an iCloud-synced repo checkout; the legacy cwd read-fallback keeps existing data reachable until `superclaw migrate-home`. Deliberately NOT moved (workspace/repo-bound by contract, with rationale in code): chat attachments (`<repo>/.superclaw/chat-attachments` — must stay inside the backend's read-only repo sandbox so the model can open them), daemon git worktrees (`<repo>/.superclaw/worktrees` — git-worktree semantics), the fusion-native report (read relative to the fusion source tree), and the dev acceptance reports (workspace-passed). Verification: ruff clean; package-wide import smoke + `superclaw doctor` green; relevant suites green across plugin/catalog/cloud/trust/company/team/capability/skill/cli/api/ui/evals — sidecar-execution and signature-flaky subprocess tests (`test_plugin_proxy`/`test_plugin_cloud`/`test_plugin_mcp_proxy`/`test_plugin_submission`) confirmed green when run serially (their failures under `-n auto` are the known load/order flakiness, not this change); three tests that pinned the old cwd-relative default (`test_team_api` company-catalog ×2, `test_plugin_cloud_api` registry-root ×1) were updated to pin the HOME root explicitly. An adversarial-review round also caught a governance regression introduced by making the company-revocation default a runtime resolver: the bootstrap human-approval persisted `None` for a default revocation source and re-resolved it at grant/resume, so the now-dynamic default (which can drift between the HOME root and a legacy cwd path as files appear) could let resume re-verify against a DIFFERENT revocation list than the proposal used (approval-resume revocation TOCTOU). Fixed in depth: (1) the approval persists the RESOLVED absolute revocation path at proposal time (never `None`); (2) `_same_revocation_source` compares resolved paths and refuses to alias onto a stored-`None`/missing source (so a new request can't dedupe onto a legacy approval); (3) the grant/resume path fails closed when a COMPANY approval has a missing/None revocation source (legacy/malformed) instead of re-resolving the dynamic default. Two regression tests cover the persisted-path invariant and the grant fail-closed. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini) both no-blocking-findings. Files: `plugins.py`, `plugin_proxy.py`, `plugin_cloud.py`, `plugin_config.py`, `plugin_runtime_projection.py`, `plugin_mcp_proxy.py`, `catalog_resolver.py`, `company_template.py`, `capability_surface.py`, `skill_sync.py`, `skill_store.py`, `team_templates.py`, `team_bootstrap.py`, `team_kernel.py`, `tui.py`, `ui_contracts.py`, `evals.py`, `cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_team_api.py`, `tests/test_plugin_cloud_api.py`, `tests/test_team_bootstrap_company_gate.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Relocated the SuperClaw run-artifacts root from a cwd-relative `.superclaw/artifacts` to the HOME data root `~/.superclaw/artifacts` (continuation of the state.db/telemetry.db data-root move). What changed: a new `environment.default_artifact_dir()` (honors `SUPERCLAW_ARTIFACT_DIR`, else `superclaw_data_path("artifacts")`) is the single source every artifacts entrypoint shares — the orchestrator (`child_artifact_dir` / `execution_context["artifact_dir"]` / run-root resolution), the heartbeat daemon (`HeartbeatDaemon.artifact_dir`), the FastAPI surface (`create_app` run/chat request models via `Field(default_factory=...)`, runtime-status, plugin-diagnostics), and every CLI `--artifact-dir` option (`run`/`chat`/`shell`/`validate`/`tui`/`daemon start`/`daemon tick` now default to `None` and resolve in-body, so the default is computed at call time — not frozen at import — keeping per-invocation/test isolation correct). Sub-roots route through `superclaw_data_path("artifacts", …)`: plugin evidence/proxy (`plugin_evidence.default_plugin_evidence_dir()` replacing the cwd module constant, `plugin_proxy`, `plugin_mcp_proxy` dataclass `default_factory` + argparse defaults), media (`runninghub_artifact_root`), fusion (`fusion_artifact_dir`), and the TUI plugin-artifact path. Why: same iCloud-sync off-machine hazard as the state/telemetry move — run outputs (worker artifacts, plugin invocation evidence, media, fusion) must not default into an iCloud-synced repo checkout. Impact: artifacts now land under `~/.superclaw/artifacts`; `SUPERCLAW_ARTIFACT_DIR` and every `--artifact-dir` flag still override; legacy cwd `.superclaw/artifacts` is read-compatible (via `superclaw_data_path`) until `superclaw migrate-home`. Verification: ruff clean; relevant suites green under `-n auto` — fusion/media/plugin_evidence/plugin_proxy/plugin_mcp_proxy/tui/cli (313+48 passed), orchestrator/daemon/daemon_races/escalation/api/plugin_submission (352 passed), adversarial_hardening/plugin_cloud/plugin_cloud_api/codex_app_server/plugin_devkit (127 passed); two tests that wrote artifacts to the old cwd default were updated to pin the artifact root explicitly (parity with how they already pin state/cache paths). - Relocated the SuperClaw runtime data root for `state.db` / `telemetry.db` from a cwd-relative `.superclaw/` to the HOME data root `~/.superclaw` (like `~/.codex` / `~/.claude`). What changed: a new single source of truth in `environment.py` — `superclaw_home()` (env `SUPERCLAW_HOME`, else `~/.superclaw`), `superclaw_data_path(*parts)` (HOME-first, with a legacy cwd `.superclaw/` read-fallback consulted ONLY when defaulting to `~/.superclaw`, never when `SUPERCLAW_HOME` is set explicitly), and `default_state_path()` (honors `SUPERCLAW_STATE_PATH`, else `superclaw_data_path("state.db")`). Every `state.db` entrypoint now shares `default_state_path()` — the CLI (`_state_path()`), the FastAPI `create_app()` default, the desktop sidecar fast-path (`apps/desktop/backend/superclaw_service.py`), and the streamtest harness (`apps/streamtest/serve.py`, via `superclaw_data_path`); `resolve_telemetry_path()` defaults to `superclaw_data_path("telemetry.db")` (its emergency journal is a sibling, so it follows). The module-level ASGI `app` in `apps/api/main.py` was made lazy (PEP 562 `__getattr__`) so importing the module for its `create_app` factory no longer has the side effect of opening a default DB; only an actual `apps.api.main:app` access (uvicorn) builds it. Why: this repo commonly runs from `~/Documents/superClaw`, an iCloud-synced checkout, so a cwd-relative data root synced operational state and diagnostics telemetry off-machine (the multi-machine concurrency hazard and the Tier-C "backup leaves the machine" root cause); credentials already lived under `~/.superclaw`, this aligns operational data with them. Impact: new installs/data land under `~/.superclaw`; existing cwd data is not orphaned (legacy read-fallback) until a follow-up `superclaw migrate-home` relocates it; explicit `SUPERCLAW_STATE_PATH` / `SUPERCLAW_HOME` overrides still win. Tests pin `SUPERCLAW_HOME` and drop ambient `SUPERCLAW_STATE_PATH` at collection time + per-test for hermetic isolation. Verification: new `tests/test_environment_data_root.py` (HOME default / env override / legacy-only-when-default-home / explicit-home-not-cwd-hijacked / `create_app` explicit-path-no-default-DB / subprocess no-import-side-effect) + `tests/test_api.py` 163 passed + cli_api_parity/fusion/unified_entry/request_logging/display/team/api_preview 153 passed + state/diagnostics/runtime/credential_guard suites; ruff clean. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Antigravity (Gemini) both no-blocking-findings. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/environment.py`, `cli.py`, `diagnostics_store.py`, `runtime.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/desktop/backend/superclaw_service.py`, `apps/streamtest/serve.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_environment_data_root.py`, `.env.example`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Fusion workspace no longer renders blank white boxes / an unreadable white panel in dark mode. What changed: the surface preview grid only embeds a live `<iframe>` for a surface that is actually running (run-status `ok=true` and the service running) — a non-running dev-server URL renders the browser's blank error page, a white box CSS cannot recolor — and otherwise shows a dark `.fusion-preview-placeholder` ("Start <surface> to preview it here."); the dark-theme override block now also covers `.fusion-tab-panel`, and the preview iframe background moved from a hardcoded `#ffffff` to `var(--bg-card)`. Why: the dark theme re-skins via CSS variables but `.fusion-tab-panel` and the preview iframes were left on hardcoded white, and the iframes were always rendered regardless of run state, so the page showed three white rectangles and a white-on-light unreadable panel. Impact: presentation-only — no API/kernel/run-status contract change; the only behavior change is the intended one (non-running surfaces show a placeholder instead of a blank iframe); light theme is unchanged (`var(--bg-card)` resolves to `#ffffff`). Verification: `npm run build`, `npm test` 302 passed + `static-ui`, and live preview confirming zero iframes + three dark placeholders + a dark readable panel (`bg rgb(22,22,22)`) with a clean console. Adversarial review: Codex (gpt-5.5) + Gemini both no-blocking-findings. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Request-validation (422) responses no longer echo the submitted input. What changed: added an app-wide `RequestValidationError` handler that keeps the 422 status and each error's `loc`/`msg`/`type` but drops FastAPI's default `input`/`ctx` fields, so a malformed request body/query never reflects attacker-controlled values (or accidental secrets) back in the error. Why: the default behaviour mirrored request content in error bodies, conflicting with the project's no-echo convention (e.g. the fusion actions endpoint already sanitized its 400s). Impact: every endpoint is covered; the `{"detail": [...]}` shape and 422 status are preserved (existing 422 assertions check status/shape, not the dropped fields). Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py` (wrong-typed + missing-field bodies return 422 with no `input` echoed) and full `tests/test_api.py`; ruff clean. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Added

- Projected the fusion run lifecycle onto the API. What changed: added `POST /api/fusion/start`, `POST /api/fusion/stop` (body: `profile`, `execute`) and `GET /api/fusion/run-status?profile=` — all behind `require_control_token`, each calling the same kernel functions (`fusion_start_plan`/`fusion_stop_plan`/`fusion_run_status`) with no parallel logic. Unknown profiles return HTTP 400 with a sanitized `"unknown fusion profile"` detail (the request profile is never echoed); valid requests return the kernel's structured payload (including fail-closed `ok=False` statuses for docker/parse problems) with 200 so clients can render run state. Why: the Web sidebar surfacing (next phase) needs a governed way to start/stop/inspect the imported product surfaces; per the CLI-is-source-of-truth law the API only mirrors the kernel. Impact: additive API surface; existing fusion status/capabilities/audit/actions endpoints unchanged. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py` (start plan + stop execute + run-status happy path with mocked docker, unknown-profile 400 without echoing input, and control-token 401 gating) and `tests/test_api.py`; ruff clean. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Completed the fusion run-orchestration lifecycle in the kernel and CLI. What changed: `fusion stop [--execute]` emits/runs `docker compose --profile <all|osiris|design|pencil> down`, and `fusion ps` reports live run status via `docker compose ps --format json --all`, mapping each compose service back to its fusion component with a `running` flag, container `state`/`health`, and the surface URL. The shared docker invocation and fail-closed error classification (`compose_file_missing`, `docker_unavailable`, `timeout`, `exec_error`, `failed`) were extracted into one helper reused by start/stop/ps; unknown profiles fail closed with a structured `unknown_profile` status across start/stop/ps (no more bare `ValueError`/empty output); `fusion ps` never surfaces raw compose stdout and fails closed (`parse_failed`) when compose returns non-empty output that is not a recognizable service shape. Execution-text redaction now routes through the canonical `superclaw.secrets_scan` scanner (single source of truth, no fusion-local regex) plus host-root/home-path scrubbing. Why: `fusion start --execute` could launch the imported product surfaces but there was no governed way to stop them or read their live state, which the Web sidebar surfacing needs to call into. Impact: additive kernel + CLI behavior; start-plan output, permission gates, capability audit, and third-party source trees are unchanged. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py` covers stop plan/execute/compose-missing, ps NDJSON + array parsing + warning-line recovery, valid-but-wrong-shape fail-closed, component mapping, docker-unavailable/permission-error fail-closed, unknown-profile across start/stop/ps, and canonical secret + home-path redaction; ruff clean. Reviewed adversarially by Codex (gpt-5.5) and Gemini until both passed. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Delegated-child context pointers (Paperclip §2.7 ②): a resumed parent agent now
  sees WHICH FILES a delegated child changed, so it integrates incrementally
  instead of re-reading the whole tree. New `context_pointers.py` captures a run's
  changed paths via git (diff against a run-start baseline sha ∪ untracked, MINUS
  the paths already dirty at start — per-issue worktrees are reused) with a
  structured fail-open status (captured/no_changes/failed/skipped/unavailable),
  surfaced through the A2A delegate tool result, the issue-completion reflow, and
  the daemon continuation brief. Files: `context_pointers.py`, `orchestrator.py`,
  `team_kernel.py`, `daemon.py`, tests.
- Cost ledger time-window queries + fail-open cost logging (Paperclip company cost
  governance). `cost list`/`cost summary` and `GET /api/cost/{events,summary}` gain
  `--today`/`--since`/`--until` resolved by one kernel `resolve_cost_window` shared
  by CLI and API (zero divergence; half-open `[since, until)`; fail-closed on
  non-finite or inverted windows). The chat and worker `CostEvent` recorders now log
  fail-open failures instead of swallowing them silently. Files: `state.py`,
  `cli.py`, `orchestrator.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, tests.
- Relay super-package (套餐) display for the ClawWork relay runtime. What changed:
  added a kernel `relay_packages()` (new `relay_packages.py`) that, after ClawHunt
  login, lists the relay's super-group tiers (core/plus/max…) — priority dynamic
  catalog (`/api/v1/bridge/packages`) → groups (`/api/v1/groups`) → built-in
  defaults, all fail-safe — instead of the relay's raw `/v1/models`; rerouted
  `model_discovery._probe_clawwork` and added `superclaw relay packages`,
  `GET /api/relay/packages`, a contract flag `uses_relay_packages`, and a Web
  package selector so EVERY surface shows packages, never raw model ids; the
  selected package id is translated once to the hidden relay group slug
  (`plus` → `superclaw-plus`, dynamic ids via the live catalog) at run time. Why:
  ClawHunt's super grouping exposes curated package tiers, not raw models; the
  surfaces must mirror that abstraction (CLI is the single source of truth) rather
  than leaking the relay's underlying model pool. Impact: clawwork model selection
  now reads/shows packages across CLI/API/Web with zero divergence; raw `/v1/models`
  is no longer surfaced for clawwork; group_slug values are validated to stay
  `superclaw-*` (no raw-model injection). Verification:
  `/Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m pytest

- App environment configuration defaults for local and production builds. What changed: added a shared `APP_ENV` resolver, local-first development defaults, production-required URL validation, environment-scoped ClawHunt auth paths, configurable Fusion/RunningHub/Anthropic endpoints, Vite proxy configuration, and regression tests for those contracts. Why: useful recent local `main` work made SuperClaw easier to test against local services without hard-coding production or localhost behavior into unrelated call sites. Impact: development keeps safe localhost defaults, while staging/production must provide explicit external URLs for network-facing integrations; no Paperclip tenant or SaaS multi-tenant behavior was imported. - Escalation framework and native approval integration on `dev/roadmap`. What changed: recorded and absorbed the D1/D2/D3 escalation branches while preserving the newer roadmap implementation: durable escalation envelopes, fail-closed state storage, B-class tool suspension/resume, CLI/API list/show/respond surfaces, `runtime_tool` native approval envelopes, Codex app-server delayed approval broker wiring, and the Web escalation gate with pending badges. Why: recent worktree history already contained these capabilities, but the old branches were stale against Paperclip, Display, Capability, and environment-config code and needed a traceable integration record without regressing newer hot paths. Impact: escalation requests remain governed through the same signed queue and are actionable from CLI/API/Web; unresolved or expired requests still fail closed. Verification: focused escalation, native approval, API/CLI, Codex app-server, and Web gate tests are part of the final roadmap verification. - Paperclip Team Kernel completion on `dev/roadmap`. What changed: completed the bootstrap pipeline from `agentcompanies/v1` template preview to proposal validation, approval-gated/idempotent commit, durable `CompanyProfile`/`WorkspaceProfile`/`AgentProfile`/seed issue materialization, API/CLI `mode=commit`, and a usable four-step Team Workbench bootstrap flow that previews, reviews risk, commits only after the backend confirms, and keeps approval-required proposals pending. Why: the earlier Paperclip migration still stopped at side-effect-free proposals and a disabled bootstrap shell, so SuperClaw could show an organization design but could not actually instantiate it. - Cross-runtime delegation P1-2b/P1-3 reviewed delegate result gate. What changed: when a delegated child reaches a terminal status, the orchestrator now materializes a bounded parent-visible `delegate_tool_results` entry tied to the original parent tool-call id, marks it `pending_review`, records the linked child evidence pointer and output summary, and blocks `resume_run()` from re-entering the parent while any delegate tool-result is unreviewed or any delegated child is still running. A new kernel review method approves or rejects the result; approved results remain in the parent execution context for the next resumed worker turn, while rejected results fail the parent closed with evidence. Why: P1-2a made child waits durable, but child completion still could not safely become a parent tool-result; P1-3 explicitly forbids model self-approval of delegated child completion. Impact: parent continuation now has a human-reviewed state boundary instead of auto-continuing from child terminal status; fuller child stream replay/resume consumption, API/CLI review surfaces, and thread-table hardening remain follow-up work. Verification: focused delegate regression 4 passed, related Python regression 267 passed, full Python regression 2523 passed / 9 skipped / 1 warning, full ruff passed, web test 141 passed, web build passed with the existing Vite chunk-size warning, `git diff --check` passed, Gemini final review PASS, and Claude Sonnet QA final review PASS. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/ROADMAP-STATUS.md`.

- Cross-runtime delegation P1-2a durable broker suspension. What changed: the orchestrator now catches `DelegationRequested` from eligible api-agent tool loops, authorizes it through the existing broker, requires an explicit target runtime for execution, creates exactly one linked child run keyed by parent tool-call id, persists `child_delegation_waits` in the parent run context, parks the parent at a new `WAITING_FOR_CHILD_DELEGATION` status before starting a newly created child, forwards child worker display events to the parent stream as nested `child_run.event` records, and marks the wait record when the child reaches a terminal status. Why: P1-1b made `delegate(...)` physically callable, but without a durable orchestrator catch path the parent run failed through the generic exception handler. Impact: cross-runtime delegate calls now fail closed or become resumable child waits instead of crashing; P1-3 completion review, full parent tool-result continuation, and event sink/thread-table hardening remain follow-up work. Verification: full `uv run --extra dev pytest` 2520 passed / 9 skipped / 1 warning, `uv run --extra dev ruff check .`, `npm --prefix apps/web run test` 141 passed, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, `git diff --check`, related Python regression 264 passed, focused delegate/liveness/model tests 5 passed, scoped ruff and compile checks passed, Claude Sonnet QA final review PASS, Gemini final review PASS. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/models.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/liveness.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_liveness.py`, `tests/test_models.py`.

- T11 low-trust containment runtime fence. What changed: runs now carry a resolved `ContainmentPolicy` with strictest-source-wins semantics across company, workspace, issue, and repo risk; delegated children inherit the fence; unsupported runtimes fail closed instead of downgrading; B-class in-process tool loops enforce low-trust as read-only/no-shell/no-write with secret-read fencing; A-class/native runtimes refuse low-trust until they can prove containment. The second pass keeps ordinary `plan` mode from delegating, but lets low-trust reviewer agents raise a governed `DelegationRequested` when the policy allows one analysis layer, and closes secret-read bypasses through symlinks and hardlinks. Why: untrusted review work needs a real runtime fence before Capability Workshop company/catalog flows can safely dispatch work. Impact: low-trust workspaces and review issues now run only on containment-capable backends and reject unsafe chat/direct runtime paths. Verification: full `uv run --extra dev pytest` 2495 passed / 9 skipped / 1 warning, `uv run --extra dev ruff check .`, `npm --prefix apps/web run test` 141 passed, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, Claude Sonnet QA PASS, Gemini review PASS after blocker fixes. - Cross-runtime delegation P1-1b api-agent injection slice. The SuperClaw-owned tool loops now physically inject a `delegate(subtask, runtime?, model_tier?, profile?, budget_seconds?)` tool only when the shell-scoped delegation switch is strictly enabled, the source backend is delegation-eligible, and the parent run is top-level (`delegation_depth == 0`). The tool loop never spawns directly: a valid call raises structured `DelegationRequested` with the parsed `DelegationRequest` and parent tool-call id for the future orchestrator broker; invalid calls fail closed as tool errors. Eligibility was corrected to the real tool-loop backend name (`anthropic-agent`, not the single-completion `anthropic`). P1-2 durable async/resume and P1-3 child completion review gate remain follow-up work. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cross_runtime_delegation.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, tests.

- PromptEnvelope IR contract foundation (Prompt Envelope roadmap PR-1, inert). New `prompt_contracts.py` defines the kernel-side layered prompt contract that later PRs will project per runtime capability: a six-layer `PromptEnvelope` ordered static->dynamic (governance core / runtime adapter / tool contract / agent charter / task+context / user turn) so the stable head can become a cache-friendly prefix; each layer's `LayerAuthority` is **derived from its kind** (the constructor takes no `authority` argument, and the public `LAYER_AUTHORITY` map is exposed read-only) so a surface cannot mislabel a governance layer as user-writable; construction is fail-closed (unknown kind, non-string content, duplicate / out-of-order / non-iterable layers raise `PromptContractError`). `LocalAgentRuntimeSpec` gains the `SystemChannel` capability dimension (`system_channel` plus `per_call_system` / `append_preserves_default` / `override_replaces_default` / `supports_cache_control` / `supports_tool_schema`), defaulting to the most conservative `flatten_only` and validated at construction against `SYSTEM_CHANNELS`. No execution-path behavior change — nothing projects the envelope yet (builder/projector land in later PRs); `LocalAgentRuntimeSpec.to_dict()` gains six declarative keys read by no consumer. See `docs/prompt-envelope-roadmap.md`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/prompt_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/local_agent_runtime.py`, `docs/prompt-envelope-roadmap.md`, `tests/test_prompt_contracts.py`.

- Cross-runtime delegation foundation (Cross-Runtime Delegation roadmap PR-0). The agent inventory becomes the single capability descriptor surfaces project (`HARNESS_CAPABILITIES` stays the harness-native source): `build_agent_inventory` now folds in each backend's harness sub-spawn / parallelism bits (`task_spawn` / `parallel_agents`, sourced from `HARNESS_CAPABILITIES` via a per-backend `harness` mapping) instead of leaving a second descriptor for surfaces to read directly — a backend with no harness mapping (e.g. the Gemini/Anthropic API-agent loops, which are not the CLI harnesses) reports `null` (unknown), never a fabricated `False`. Each runtime also gains a short declarative `strengths` hint (a judgment cue the orchestrator model reads to self-select a delegation target; NOT a routing rule). New `superclaw runtime list` command emits, in one shot, availability ∩ model selection ∩ harness capabilities ∩ strengths (JSON; the CLI fact source every surface projects). New kernel `delegation` toggle (`RuntimeConfigSpec`, shell-scoped, fail-closed default off, renders as a toggle) — when off the future delegate tool is not injected and the model physically cannot delegate; it is a kernel config (CLI shell `/config set delegation true` / Web slider), not a frontend-only switch. No delegate tool yet — the inward chat delegation tool + governance wrapper land in PR-1 (after Display Protocol streaming). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `docs/cross-runtime-delegation.md`, tests.

Why: the roadmap requires the sidebar to expose kernel-owned workspace state and safe conversation movement instead of relying on ad hoc UI grouping. - Claude Code chat now rides its NATIVE session with streaming (unified chat entry, PR-B). One SuperClaw chat session binds to ONE persistent claude session — first turn `claude -p --session-id <uuid>`, later turns `--resume <uuid>` sending only the user's message (no transcript replay); streaming rides claude's `stream-json` through `run_claude_stream`, emitting the same `message.delta`/`message.completed` SSE events the codex inline channel uses (zero frontend change). The ask/allow preset passes through as `--permission-mode`; an existing conversation getting its first native session is seeded once; a broken resume retires the binding. The binding layer (`ChatSession.metadata.native_sessions` + state accessors) is generic so cursor/grok/hermes/opencode (native resume verified in their CLIs) wire in next. Verified: real e2e — a resumed turn recalled a codeword from claude's native memory with zero replay. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/state.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, tests.

- The chat runtime selector is tier-aware (`chat_tier` contract field): `native` (codex-app-server, wired) / `upgradeable` (claude/opencode/grok/cursor/hermes/openclaw) / `oneshot` (codex exec, bobo, gemini, anthropic-agent — runs only) / `infra` (local/http/gateway/clawwork/anthropic — plumbing). The composer lists only native+upgradeable; codex-app-server renders as "Codex"; the current selection always stays listed. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, tests.

- codex model catalog goes LIVE via the app-server protocol's official `model/list` JSON-RPC method — the account's real catalog over the ChatGPT-subscription credential plane (both advisors rejected the OPENAI_API_KEY route as a different billing plane; it is the route Paperclip itself uses). Verified live: gpt-5.5 (default) / gpt-5.4 / gpt-5.4-mini / gpt-5.3-codex-spark — the exact codex picker list. claude additionally gains a live channel via the Anthropic `/v1/models` API when an API key is configured (no key → refreshed static hints with a guidance error). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/model_discovery.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, tests.

### Fixed

- Team workbench bootstrap proposal now re-hydrates from the pending
  `team.bootstrap.commit` approval instead of polling a
  `GET /api/team/bootstrap/proposal` endpoint the API never implemented (a dead
  404 that was swallowed, so reload never restored the pending blueprint and the
  call was a CLI/API/Web contract divergence). The kernel already persists the
  full proposal on the approval's `resume_action.proposal`, so the dead fetch is
  dropped and the Web re-hydrates from the approval queue. To reach the company
  workbench, `StateStore.list_approvals` now scopes an issue-linked approval by
  the issue's AUTHORITATIVE company (a stray `affects.company_profile_id` can
  never re-route it into another company's actionable inbox), falling back to
  `affects.company_profile_id` only for issue-less approvals; the frontend match
  also requires `affects.company_profile_id === company`. Files: `state.py`,
  `apps/web/src/TeamWorkbench.tsx`, tests.
- Staging desktop bundles now use launchable local signing instead of hardened
  runtime signing by default. What changed: `prepare-macos-bundle.mjs` only adds
  `--options runtime` for production builds or explicit
  `SUPERCLAW_DESKTOP_HARDENED_RUNTIME=1`, while staging/development acceptance
  bundles keep plain local signing. Why: non-notarized hardened staging bundles
  could be killed by macOS LaunchServices/AMFI immediately on open, producing
  `err=-600` and no app window. Impact: `/Applications/SuperClaw.app` can now
  open for local staging acceptance; production release signing remains
  opt-in/production-scoped. Verification: staging config, Tauri build, installed
  bundle smoke, runtime smoke, launch smoke, codesign verification, hash match,
  and Computer Use window inspection all passed. Files:
  `apps/desktop/scripts/prepare-macos-bundle.mjs`.

- Hardened desktop runtime onboarding and Capability Workshop surfaces. What
  changed: the web shell now treats persisted backend config as the source for
  default runtime selection, opens the agent setup dialog when desktop runtime
  agents need an explicit choice, filters composer choices to chat-capable
  runtime agents, prevents Agent company API calls until the desktop runtime
  session is connected, and routes those calls through the runtime base URL
  with the desktop control token. The user-facing plugin marketplace copy is
  also aligned to Capability Workshop terminology across English and Chinese
  shell text and tests. Why: local main carried desktop-first fixes that should
  be reviewed from a worker branch instead of staying as dirty work on `main`.
  Impact: frontend/runtime readiness behavior and copy only; backend runtime
  contracts, packaging, deployment, and production configuration are unchanged.
  Verification: root static contract pytest, `npm --prefix apps/web test`,
  `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, root pytest, `git diff --check`, and
  GitHub Actions before merge. Files:
  `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`,
  `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`,
  `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_web_static.py`,
  `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Cleared frontend build audit and chunk warnings. What changed: upgraded the Web Vite toolchain to Vite 8 with the matching React plugin, refreshed the Web lockfile to remove audited Vite/Babel/esbuild advisory ranges, and added explicit Rolldown code-splitting groups for React, Markdown, icons, and remaining vendor modules. Why: the packaged app build was valid but still surfaced npm audit advisories and a single oversized Web JavaScript chunk. Impact: production Web assets now build without audit findings or Vite chunk-size warnings; runtime behavior is unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web audit` and `npm --prefix apps/web run build` passed before the final desktop package rebuild. Files: `apps/web/package.json`, `apps/web/package-lock.json`, `apps/web/vite.config.mjs`.

- Stabilized roadmap CI and desktop smoke verification. What changed: cross-runtime child-result rejection review now idempotently preserves the rejection evidence finding, the plugin verification warm-cache showcase no longer fails CI on wall-clock timing noise, desktop smoke mode can run before Tauri window initialization, macOS headless smoke uses the release binary instead of directly executing the signed `.app/Contents/MacOS` entry, and workbench smoke seeds a trusted workspace through the normal API before exercising chat/run paths. Why: the roadmap PR's full GitHub Actions run exposed one real audit-evidence edge and one flaky timing assertion, while local app verification showed the old installed-bundle smoke was testing an invalid macOS direct-exec path. Impact: no Paperclip or runtime product semantics change; CI and packaged-app verification now fail on meaningful contract errors instead of harness artifacts. Verification: focused Python regression, Rust smoke tests, desktop launch smoke, installed bundle smoke, and signed macOS bundle build passed. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_plugin_warm_cache.py`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-*.test.mjs`.

- Hardened Capability Workshop developer submission blob signing. What changed: `sign_reviewed_submission()` now derives the stored blob from `submission_root / submission_id / "package"` instead of using record-controlled paths, rejects symlink/escaped blob directories, treats recorded `blob_path` only as a consistency check, recomputes the canonical package digest from stored bytes, and refuses signing unless both `artifact_blob_digest` and reviewed `package_digest` match. Why: submit/signature decoupling still needed a hard local blob-store boundary so a tampered review record could not redirect signing or rewrite digest facts after review. Impact: stricter fail-closed local Phase 4A signing; submit-alone and API submit-time signing-key rejection remain unchanged. Verification: `uv run --extra dev pytest` 2516 passed / 9 skipped / 1 warning, `uv run --extra dev ruff check .`, `npm --prefix apps/web run test` 141 passed, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, focused `uv run pytest tests/test_plugin_submission.py tests/test_plugin_cloud_api.py -q` 58 passed, Gemini review PASS, Claude Sonnet QA PASS. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_submission.py`, `tests/test_plugin_submission.py`.

- CW trust same-id signer conflicts now fail closed. What changed: plugin cache writes compare the existing cached `id@version` signer identity against the incoming package and refuse a silent overwrite when they differ; runtime plugin listing now detects the same plugin id under multiple signer identities before choosing the latest version and excludes that id from both `available_plugins()` and `gate_passing_plugins()`. Why: the Capability Workshop trust roadmap requires same-id different-signer packages to be a hard conflict, so a newer local/developer package cannot shadow an older official package. Impact: stricter plugin install/listing behavior for conflicting identities; valid same-signer packages keep their existing flow. Verification: full `uv run --extra dev pytest` 2461 passed / 8 skipped / 1 warning, `uv run --extra dev ruff check .`, `npm --prefix apps/web run test` 141 passed, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, Claude Sonnet QA PASS, Gemini review PASS. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugins.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_runtime_projection.py`, tests.

- Cross-runtime handoff gaps closed (advisor-reviewed G1-G5 + B1-B3). Native-session bindings carry a `last_seen_message_id` watermark (ChatMessage gains a stable `message_id`; legacy rows degrade to full sync); a resumed session receives a one-time catch-up block covering exactly the turns it missed while the chat ran on another runtime — both directions, any number of switches; the codex inline thread shares the same mechanism (covers app restarts). The claude native branch now applies the capability-surface resume guard (widened posture hard-blocks the binding); native turns serialize on a per-session lock with the binding persisted BEFORE execution; bindings record repo_path and retire on a workspace switch; seeds/catch-ups sync only real user/assistant dialogue (system handoff markers and `run_id=...` placeholders never reach a model); codex's HARD_BLOCK wipes the persisted store bindings too. Known follow-up: the sync SSE generator's unbounded `queue.get()` leaks a threadpool thread on early client disconnect. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/models.py`, `state.py`, `chat_turn.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, tests.

### Changed

- Repository development workflow now documents the Goal Go worktree-first
  pattern. What changed: added root `AGENTS.md` guidance and updated
  `coordination.md` so active development happens in isolated worker worktrees,
  `dev/roadmap` is only the local integration/test worktree, staging-first
  acceptance is conditional on an enabled remote `staging` branch, and PRs are
  opened from the verified original worker branch instead of defaulting to
  `dev/roadmap` or `staging`. Why: the project rules
  must prevent direct `main` edits and match the requested Goal Go development
  paradigm. Impact: documentation and agent workflow only; no runtime behavior
  changes. Verification: `git diff --check`, targeted rule grep, advisor
  review, and local `dev/roadmap` merge verification. Files: `AGENTS.md`,
  `.gitignore`, `coordination.md`, `docs/roadmap-development-status.md`,
  `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Unified the app's three modals onto one DialogShell primitive (settings-redesign PR-6b). What changed: new `apps/web/src/ui/DialogShell.tsx` owns the backdrop/panel/header skeleton (status-pill slot, kicker/title/subtitle, close button, Escape + backdrop-press handling) with size variants mapping the legacy footprints (default = agent setup, wide = ClawHunt login, full = plugin configuration); the agent-setup dialog, ClawHunt login gate, and plugin-config modal now render through it with their bodies untouched, and the three legacy shell class sets (`.agent-setup-backdrop/panel/header`, `.login-setup-panel`, `.plugin-config-backdrop/modal/modal-header`) plus the plugin modal's standalone Escape effect are deleted. Dismissal semantics are preserved exactly: blocking gates (agent setup, login) close only via their header button — `dismissable={false}` ignores Escape and backdrop presses — while the plugin modal keeps both; secret non-echo and the basic/advanced step contract in the plugin body are untouched. Also re-homed the `.context-hint` rule from `settings.css` to `styles.css` next to its cockpit-drawer consumers, closing the temporary placement noted in PR-4. Why: the final step of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — three hand-rolled modal shells were the last unshared settings-adjacent chrome. Impact: presentation-only; aria roles/names, focus targets, and every close pathway keep their old behavior. Verification: new `tests/dialog-shell.test.tsx` (6 tests pinning the dismissal contract incl. blocking gates ignoring Escape/backdrop), `npm test --prefix apps/web` 73 passed, `npm run build`, `tsc --noEmit` error set identical to HEAD, zero remaining references to the legacy shell classes, and live preview: agent-setup opens through the shell (alertdialog name, header pill), stays open on Escape, closes via its button. Files: `apps/web/src/ui/DialogShell.tsx` (new), `apps/web/tests/dialog-shell.test.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css`, `apps/web/package.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Removed

- Removed the dead settings CSS left behind by the row-based conversion (settings-redesign PR-6a). What changed: the `.theme-segmented-control` (4 rules) and `.language-switch` (2 rules) blocks are deleted from `styles.css` — both classes lost their last JSX reference when PR-3 replaced the theme/language cards with the shared `SegmentedControl` primitive, confirmed by a zero-reference grep across `src/`. Why: the redesign plan's cleanup step; dead selectors in a 5k-line stylesheet are exactly the rot the settings rework set out to stop. Impact: none at runtime — the rules were unreachable. Verification: zero references confirmed, `npm test --prefix apps/web` 67 passed, `npm run build`. The remaining cleanup items (DialogShell modal unification, re-homing `.context-hint`, `.control-card` retirement once the account/runtime cards are converted) stay tracked in `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` as the rest of PR-6.
