### Added

- The web settings runtime card now renders runtime config from the kernel's field-level UI schema (settings-redesign PR-5b, the surface half of PR-5a). What changed: new `apps/web/src/settings/RuntimeConfigFields.tsx` maps `entries[].ui` from `/api/config` onto the settings primitives — basic-tier entries render as rows in a panel (kernel-enforced enums as dropdowns, paths/urls/text as draft inputs whose Save button appears only when dirty, numbers as `step="any"` inputs per the kernel's float semantics), boolean guards as immediate-apply toggles, and everything advanced sits behind an "Advanced configuration" disclosure with secrets shown as set/unset pills only (the kernel refuses secret writes on this path and never serializes their values); entries from an older/frozen backend without `ui` degrade to advanced text fields, so the surface keeps working against a stale kernel; the old hardcoded key-picker + value-input editor, its draft states, and its repopulation effects are removed from `App.tsx`, replaced by one `saveRuntimeConfigValue(name, value)` path through `/api/config/set`. Why: completes step 5 of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — the kernel decides what each config value is; the surface only renders, so a new kernel config key now gets a correctly-typed editor with zero frontend changes. Impact: presentation consumes the contract verbatim; validation stays kernel-side (save errors surface the kernel's message). Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 66 passed (new test drives the contract fixture end to end: prefilled url field → dirty → Save posts name/value; mode dropdown lists the kernel choices and saves; toggle posts '1'; secret renders as text-only, no input), `npm run build`, `tsc --noEmit` error set identical to HEAD, live preview against the running stale-kernel API confirming the degradation path, and a temporary main-tree API instance confirming the real contract end to end (ui_sections, mode select choices, path/url/toggle/secret descriptors, secret values absent). Files: `apps/web/src/settings/RuntimeConfigFields.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added a kernel-side field-level UI schema to runtime config entries (settings-redesign PR-5a, contract first). What changed: `RuntimeConfigSpec` gains `choices` / `ui_section` / `ui_type`, and every `runtime_config_payload()` entry now carries a derived `ui` descriptor (`type` ∈ text/path/url/number/toggle/select/secret, `section` ∈ basic/advanced mirroring the plugin-config two-tier precedent, plus optional `choices`); the payload exposes `ui_sections`; `mode` is declared a kernel-enforced select (auto/chat/delivery), executables/paths render as path fields in the basic tier, boolean guards (SSRF/insecure-gateway/plugin-trust toggles) as toggles, integer knobs as numbers, secrets as secret fields that never persist; declaring `choices` on any spec is now generically enforced in `set_runtime_config`, so the schema never promises an enum the kernel would silently accept other values for. Why: step 5 of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — both advisors drew the contract boundary at "anything with validation/default/secret semantics goes through kernel schema; surfaces only render"; the web settings runtime card currently hardcodes a name/value pair editor and cannot know which fields are paths, enums, or booleans. Impact: additive contract only — existing payload fields, CLI behavior, and `/api/config` semantics unchanged; the web renderer lands as the follow-up step (deliberately split because a concurrent change is reworking the settings form controls). Verification: new `tests/test_runtime_config_schema.py` (8 tests pinning descriptor validity, secret/persist coupling, kernel-enforced mode choices, per-kind derivations, override precedence), `pytest tests/test_runtime_config_schema.py tests/test_ui_contracts.py tests/test_api.py tests/test_cli.py` 232 passed, full `pytest` 1170 passed (the single failure, `test_web_static.py::test_web_workbench_wires_permission_preset`, is a static source check tripping over another session's uncommitted working-tree edits — this commit's snapshot keeps the asserted markup), `ruff check` clean. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `tests/test_runtime_config_schema.py` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Changed

- Made the chat composer's runtime selector configuration-aware. What changed: the composer backend dropdown now ranks available (configured) runtimes first and pushes unconfigured ones to the bottom with a "Not configured" badge plus a hint line; picking an unconfigured runtime no longer silently selects a backend that cannot run — it selects it AND routes straight to the settings Runtime section (`openRuntimeSettingsForAgent`) so the selector doubles as the configuration entry point; `ui/Dropdown.tsx` gains an optional per-option `badge` (unlike `disabled`, badged options stay clickable). Why: the `/api/agents` contract already serves the full backend list with per-agent `available`/`reason`, but the composer rendered all 14 backends indistinguishably — a new user could pick an unconfigured runtime and only learn at send/block time; hiding them outright was rejected (kills discoverability and empties the dropdown on a fresh install), so unavailable runtimes are marked and turned into a guided path instead. Impact: presentation-layer only — option data still comes verbatim from the runtime-selector contract (zero hardcoded lists), kernel/orchestrator fail-closed checks and the existing `agentSetupRequired` blocking modal are unchanged. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 55 passed (one new test: contract listing an unconfigured agent first still renders it last with the badge, and clicking it lands on runtime settings with the agent selected), `npm run build`, and live preview against the real local API: available runtimes (local/codex/codex-app-server/claude/hermes/openclaw) rank first unbadged, unconfigured ones (bobo/gemini/anthropic/anthropic-agent) badged at the bottom, and clicking `gemini` opens the Runtime settings card with gemini selected and readiness "missing". Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/ui/Dropdown.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Reorganized the settings information architecture into four task-oriented groups and rewrote the surface copy (settings-redesign PR-4). What changed: navigation collapses from six entries (General / Diagnostics / ClawHunt / Runtime / Desktop / Operations — three of which landed on the same section) to four (Preferences / Account / Runtime / Diagnostics); Desktop alerts moved into Preferences next to appearance and language; the ClawHunt login card got its own Account section; Desktop shell stays under Runtime; crash/log export and the manual-update row moved under Diagnostics; the full ClawHunt task browser moved out of settings into the run cockpit drawer (a "ClawHunt tasks" details section next to Recent runs, where task-derived delivery runs are launched), with a transitional link row in Diagnostics; the decorative shell-identity chips were dropped; every section heading/description was rewritten in plain language (en/zh), legacy nav copy keys removed, and old target ids (`settings-operations` etc.) keep working via legacy mappings. Why: step 4 of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — the old 6-to-4 nav mapping was the core of "I don't know what this page is telling me", and both advisors ruled the task browser is an operations flow that does not belong in settings. Impact: presentation/IA only; all handlers, API calls, and persistence semantics unchanged; the task browser keeps its exact behavior in its new home. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 54 passed on the isolated staged snapshot (`git checkout-index` materialization — one new test covers the settings link expanding the cockpit task browser; other assertions updated to the new IA, including "settings no longer contains the task browser"), `npm run build` on the same snapshot, `tsc --noEmit` error set vs HEAD baseline shows no new errors from this change, and live preview on the main-tree dev server walking all four sections, the diagnostics link row, and the cockpit task browser. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Note: a concurrent session is editing `App.tsx`/`styles.css`/`app-shell.test.tsx` in the same checkout (composer dropdown framework); this commit was staged hunk-selectively to exclude that work, and the commit snapshot was validated in an isolated worktree.

- Introduced row-based settings primitives and converted the local-preference blocks (settings-redesign PR-3). What changed: new `apps/web/src/settings/primitives.tsx` (SettingsPanel / SettingRow / SegmentedControl / ToggleSwitch / StatusPill — token-driven, semantic-state-only APIs) and `settings.css` (panel/row/segmented/toggle styles plus the settings surface's first narrow-viewport support: single-column layout, horizontally scrollable nav, wrapping rows under 860px); the Appearance + Language cards became two rows in one panel (macOS-System-Settings style; theme keeps its radiogroup/radio semantics, language buttons became radios in a segmented control); the Desktop alerts card became a panel with an on/off ToggleSwitch (replacing the Enable/Disable button pair, whose copy keys are removed) plus a test-alert row with the history list as the row footer; `main.tsx` now imports the global stylesheet before component modules so module CSS wins equal-specificity ties (this was hiding the responsive rules). Why: step 3 of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — both advisors converged on rows over card piles for scannability, and the page previously had zero responsive handling. Impact: presentation-only; handlers and persistence semantics unchanged (auto-save local prefs as before). Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 53 passed (assertions updated: language radios, alerts switch with aria-checked transitions), `npm run build`, `tsc --noEmit` error set identical to HEAD baseline (18=18), and live preview on a dedicated main-tree dev server (port 5174 via a local launch config — gitignored, not part of this change — because the shared 5173 was found serving a different worktree, which had silently invalidated earlier preview evidence): rows render in light/dark, theme/language/toggle interactions work, mobile viewport shows the responsive nav and wrapped rows. Files: `apps/web/src/settings/primitives.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/settings/settings.css` (new), `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/main.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Isolated the web settings surface into its own module (settings-redesign PR-2, zero behavior change). What changed: the ~560 lines of settings JSX inlined in `App.tsx` (sidebar + overview/runtime/operations/agents sections) moved verbatim to `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx` as two presentation components (`SettingsSidebar`, `SettingsContent`); all state, derived values, and API handlers stay in `App.tsx` and arrive via typed props, so behavior is identical; the 15 payload/type declarations the props need are now exported from `App.tsx` (type-only imports, erased at build — no runtime cycle); the global blocking modals (agent setup / ClawHunt login) deliberately stay in `App.tsx` because delivery flows outside settings also raise them (dialog unification is a later plan step). `App.tsx` shrinks from ~11350 to ~10790 lines. Why: step 2 of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — cut the monolith open before reworking visuals, so the primitives/IA/contract steps land in an isolated module instead of growing `App.tsx`. Impact: pure refactor; no kernel/API semantics touched. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 53 passed (the static-ui source check now reads the combined shell source), `npm run build`, baseline-vs-after `tsc --noEmit` shows no new type errors (38→20 pre-existing), and live vite preview walking all four settings sections, theme switch, and locale switch through the extracted components. Files: `apps/web/src/settings/SettingsSurface.tsx` (new), `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Pruned the web settings surface (settings-redesign PR-1, pure subtraction). What changed: removed the never-implemented settings search input (the box filtered nothing — pure placebo) together with its copy keys and orphaned `.settings-search-input` CSS; compacted the Manual update path card from a four-paragraph card with an embedded `<pre>` block down to a compact card (version status-pill + one line pointing at the update guide), preserving the backend-provided `workspaceUpdateCommand` behind a new `Copy update command` action instead of a permanently rendered command block. Why: first step of `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md` — the fake search box erodes trust in the settings page and the update card is documentation, not configuration; the full IA regroup (4-group nav, Task Browser relocation) lands in later steps of the plan. Impact: presentation-only subtraction; no kernel/API semantics touched; Task Browser, Desktop alerts, and Crash export cards unchanged. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 53 passed (assertions updated to the compact card, including copy-button presence in Tauri mode and absence without a workspace command), `npm run build`, and live vite preview against the main worktree confirming the search box is gone and the card renders compact. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `docs/settings-redesign-plan.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Added

- Live model discovery — the runtime selector now lists REAL models from each runtime instead of hand-written hints. What changed: a new kernel `model_discovery.py` probes each backend through its native listing channel (opencode/cursor/grok via their own `models` CLI commands; clawwork + http via OpenAI-style `GET /v1/models` on the configured relay/endpoint; anthropic/anthropic-agent via the Anthropic `/v1/models` API; gemini via its OpenAI-compatible base) and returns a `ModelCatalog` with an honest provenance marker — `source: "live" | "static" | "unavailable"` plus the probe `error` — degrading fail-soft to the static contract `suggested_models` so a stale list is never presented as live. Surfaces: CLI `superclaw models <backend> [--json]` + shell `/models [BACKEND]` (kernel-first), API `GET /api/agents/{name}/models` (probed on demand so `/api/agents` stays fast), and the web composer model field fetches the live catalog on focus (datalist prefers live, static hints stay as the instant fallback). Why: the hand-written lists had drifted hard from reality — verified live: opencode actually serves a 13-model gpt-5.4-era catalog (contract said sonnet-4.6/gpt-5.2) and grok serves grok-build/grok-composer-2.5-fast (contract said grok-4). Verification: 9 new kernel tests (live parse per channel, fail-soft degradation, honest static fallback) + 2 API tests; full suite 1273 passed; real smokes against installed opencode/grok/cursor. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/model_discovery.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/test_model_discovery.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Changed

- Unified chat entry absorbed + chat turns are now NATIVE runtime turns on every backend (supersedes the earlier read-only-safe-backend design in this changelog). What changed: merged the `feat/unified-task-entry` worktree — `parse_chat_route` (one runtime turn + optional @plugin/@skill overlays; `classify_intent` demoted to a legacy projection), the `agent_runtime` adapter substrate (`RuntimeCapabilities`/`AgentRuntimeAdapter`/`AgentRuntimeManager`), lazy overlay projection (pure chat projects nothing), and delivery isolated into a deprecated `_run_legacy_delivery_turn` (future: Agent company). On top of it, chat was fully nativized: the `_CHAT_READONLY_SAFE_BACKENDS` whitelist and the forced `plan` posture are GONE — every registered runtime is a first-class chat substrate, and the user's ask/allow permission preset passes through to each runtime's OWN sandbox mapping exactly like a delivery run (claude `--permission-mode`; codex exec sandbox + bypass flag; codex app-server sandbox/approval/decision with the permission mode in the session rebuild key; B-class `posture_for_mode`). `direct_chat_prompt` lost its persona/behavioral constraints — with no history and no context refs the user's message reaches the runtime verbatim; permission is enforced by the sandbox, not prompt text. CLI shell gains `/permission [ask|allow]` (same `PRESET_TO_MODE` projection, zero CLI/APP divergence). The interim "chat_mode" (no tools, no ambient MCP) experiment was reverted as a wrong turn. Why: the product decision is a single chat entry = native access to the underlying agent runtime, with plugins/skills as the only additive overlay and heavier orchestration arriving later as an Agent company — not a read-only Q&A mode. Verification: full suite green across the chain (1260 → 1273 passed); live: claude routes cleanly, hermes (previously refused by the whitelist) actually executes its CLI. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/agent_runtime/`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_chat_route.py`, `tests/test_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_unified_entry_api.py`, `tests/test_chat_runtime.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Fixed

- An explicit `runtime_id` on `/api/chat/stream` is validated BEFORE any channel dispatch. The unified-entry merge had left the generic-chat early-return ahead of the runtime negotiation, so an explicit but unknown `runtime_id` silently fell through to the sticky/generic path (exposed by a regression test the moment a real claude install came back to life). Now: adapter aliases (`claude-cli`) normalize onto backend names, unknown ids fail closed with `unknown runtime_id`, overlay turns (@plugin/@skill) on a non-MCP runtime refuse with `RUNTIME_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` instead of running overlay-less, and a plain chat turn with an explicit non-codex runtime executes natively over the kernel generic channel (the pre-nativization "declared but not wired" refusal is superseded). Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_unified_entry_api.py`.

- Chat failures and replies are no longer mangled into a wall of MCP tool names. Two layers: `_extract_direct_chat_response` parses the leading JSON value with `raw_decode` (run_command output is stdout+stderr+a failure line, which broke whole-blob `json.loads`) and, when the evidence cap (`PRIMARY_EVIDENCE_TEXT_LIMIT`) tail-truncated a large event array (a logged-in claude's init event carries its full MCP tool list), salvages the trailing result-event object from the text; the generic chat channel additionally reads the FULL stdout from the transcript artifact (before the run temp dir is deleted) whenever `output_truncated` is set. Symptom chain this kills: "claude direct chat failed with exit code 1: mcp__plugin_playwright..." where the real text was `Not logged in · Please run /login` — or, when logged in, the real answer itself. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `tests/test_chat_runtime.py`.

- Agent setup dialog: the agent grid re-probes `/api/agents` whenever the dialog opens and gains an explicit Recheck button (the backend probes live per request, so a CLI installed seconds ago shows ready without restarting the app); the executable-path draft resets only when the SELECTED agent changes, not on every availability re-probe (which wiped a path mid-typing — caught by the existing setup-dialog test); the dialog centers within the main content area instead of the full window (the open sidebar made it look shifted left). Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`.

- Direct chat now follows the chat's selected runtime instead of always using codex. What changed: `execute_direct_chat_turn` dispatches by backend — the codex family (codex/codex-app-server) keeps the fast `exec --ephemeral --sandbox read-only` path, while every read-only-safe backend answers over the kernel's `WorkerBackend.run()` channel (same registry / executable resolution / model fail-closed / secret redaction as delivery, no parallel impl) under an EXPLICIT `PermissionPolicy(mode="plan")`. The API `/api/chat/turn` + `/api/chat/stream` no longer default `direct_chat_backend` to codex — a plain chat turn executes the resolved runtime (selector > sticky > default); the Web composer stops hardcoding `direct_chat_backend: 'codex'` and shows the real executing backend. Security (Codex review): `policy=None` is NOT uniformly read-only across the registry (bobo runs `--full-auto`; the API-agent backends map None to a read/write workspace), so direct chat passes an explicit `plan` policy AND restricts to `_CHAT_READONLY_SAFE_BACKENDS` (claude/gemini/anthropic/anthropic-agent — each verified read-only under plan); any other backend fail-closes before `worker.run()` (use @delivery under governance instead). Sticky correctness (Codex review): a plain chat turn persists its runtime as sticky ONLY after it actually answers (`status==completed`) — a failed/unavailable backend never becomes the chat's sticky runtime (stream persists inside the worker thread post-success). Why: selecting a runtime for chat was meaningless when chat always ran codex; the iron law puts the dispatch in the kernel so API/Web only mirror it. Impact: codex chat behaves as before; non-codex chat now actually runs the selected runtime, one-shot (no token deltas yet — native streaming needs a per-backend thread protocol, only codex app-server has it). Verification (test -> Codex gate -> commit): test_chat_runtime + test_api + test_worker_backends 300 passed (new: safe backend receives plan policy across all four, unsafe backend fail-closed without running, failed chat not sticky / success sticky, claude event-array unwrap); ruff clean; empirically confirmed live — `/api/chat/turn` and `/api/chat/stream` with backend_policy=claude route to claude (errors only on "Not logged in", proving real dispatch). Codex: PASS. KNOWN: the symptom "switched runtime but still codex" was a stale FROZEN desktop backend (built 50min before this source change) — the source was already correct; the desktop app needs a backend rebuild + hot-swap to pick it up. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/test_chat_runtime.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added the ClawWork worker backend (experimental) — SuperClaw's model-relay coding harness (PR-3, D3+D4). What changed: ClawWork is a **thin white-label fork** of `earendil-works/pi` pinned to v0.79.1 (lives at `../clawwork`; rebrand is config-only — `piConfig.name/configDir` + `bin.clawwork`, zero source lines changed so upstream rebases stay trivial). The new `ClawWorkBackend(_AgentCliBackend)` drives it over Pi's RPC mode (`clawwork --mode rpc`, strict JSONL: one `prompt` in, `agent_*`/`tool_*` events out, terminal `agent_end` mapped to a WorkerResult). Governance is **one policy, two executors** (D4): a new `clawwork_policy.py` writes an HMAC-signed policy snapshot (mode + tool allow/deny + pay-switch, 0600, per-run secret) before spawn; the `superclaw-governance` extension running INSIDE ClawWork loads it and enforces the same posture in-process (denylist > allowlist > pay/scan hard gates with an approval POST > read-only mode), failing closed on a missing/unreadable/forged/unknown-version snapshot. The backend refuses to run ungoverned (`SUPERCLAW_CLAWWORK_GOVERNANCE_EXT` mandatory), fail-closes on plugin_dirs/mcp_configs, cancels before any spawn, and forwards the per-run model to the `clawrelay` provider (`SUPERCLAW_RELAY_BASE_URL`/`_API_KEY`). Registered in `default_backends()` (matrix 15→16) and `AGENT_CONTROL_SPECS` with a new `maturity: experimental` contract field (projected to `/api/agents`) so surfaces can badge it; not a default-path backend. Why: bare-model relay endpoints (an OpenAI-/Anthropic-compatible gateway with no agent loop) need a harness — ClawWork supplies the loop, SuperClaw supplies governance — and the decision was to fork Pi rather than re-build a runtime (runtime is hard). Impact: additive experimental backend; no existing backend or default changes. Verification (test -> Codex+Gemini gate -> commit): 7 new Python tests (signed-snapshot roundtrip + 0600, snapshot written/signed/mapped, ungoverned refusal, plugin-policy + cancel fail-closed, governance-block surfacing, registered-experimental); 4 hermetic spike gates in the clawwork repo (rpc+block end-to-end, missing-env / tampered-signature fail-closed, hook contract pins) all pass; affected suites 174 passed + ruff clean; **real end-to-end smoke** against the built clawwork v0.79.1 binary — `_spawn_rpc` drove a real subprocess, the in-process governance extension blocked a `bash` tool call under plan mode, and `agent_end` mapped to output. Hardening (Codex review, three rounds — defense in depth): the gate is no longer the extension alone — a read-only posture (mode=plan) drives ClawWork's own `--tools read,grep,find,ls` allowlist (CLI-layer hard bound that holds even if the extension fails to load), the agent dir is isolated per-run so ambient `~/.clawwork` extensions cannot register bypass tools, the extension writes a liveness ready-file AFTER its tool_call handler registers (the backend waits for it before the first prompt and fail-closes — exit 126 — if a silently-failed `-e` load left the run ungoverned), a governance-blocked tool yields a non-zero exit and NO `status=completed` (no false-positive success), the backend seeds the per-run `clawrelay` models.json, and a new `POST/GET /api/governance/approvals` human-gate inbox lands the approval requests the extension files when it blocks a pay/scan intent. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/clawwork_policy.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `tests/test_ui_contracts.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `docs/clawwork-two-track-dev-plan.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (+ the `../clawwork` fork repo: governance extension + 4 spike gates).

- Absorbed `origin/main` (38 team commits) into the runtime-selection framework branch with a local-framework-first merge. What changed: merged the team's capability atlas (245-unit atlas + `superclaw capability` CLI/API surfaces), the governed RunningHub media chain (generation/render/doctor + run-bound evidence attachment + media CLI), API-agent `stream_events` evidence capture, textual-as-default TUI runtime, TUI acceptance runner/viewer, ClawHunt live readiness gate, and the desktop-home local dry-run shortcut. Conflict resolution kept the local framework authoritative in all 11 conflicted files: the kernel keeps `_SHELL_MODES` aliased to the single kernel source and the hardened `_transcript_safe_policy_dict` transcript view (the team's `stream_events` field was grafted INTO that hardened payload instead of reverting to the raw policy dict); `launchGoalRun` (team's run-start refactor powering the home dry-run shortcut) was adopted but re-grafted with the local readiness gates, `permission_preset` forwarding, `rememberRunSession`, and the `selectComposerBackend` rule so a programmatic backend switch still clears the per-chat model; the redesigned chat shell, settings declutter, and composer semantics stayed as-is — the old console-shell markup and the settings cards removed by the declutter were not re-introduced, and their still-live actions (Run local dry-run, Open marketplace, review evidence) were re-homed onto the run-cockpit evidence card; a minimal `Fusion Shell` entry was added to the settings sidebar because the redesign had orphaned the fusion workspace that the team's Media Studio lives in; team tests asserting the superseded old shell were re-pointed at the redesigned navigation (delivery view / cockpit drawer / settings→Fusion) instead of resurrecting old markup. Why: the runtime-selection framework (model kernel, chat stickiness, selector contract, composer pill) is the base going forward; team capabilities are absorbed onto it, never the other way around. Impact: all 38 team commits' capabilities are present on the branch; no team feature was dropped — only superseded presentation was. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 53 passed + `npm run build`; full `pytest` suite post-merge; `git diff --check` clean. Files: 11 conflicted files resolved (`backends.py`, `cli.py`, `runtime_config.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_clawhunt_client.py`, `tests/test_ui_contracts.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `CHANGELOG.md`).

- Added the per-chat runtime selector to the web/desktop composer (the macOS client is the Tauri shell over the same web app). What changed: the composer footer gains a backend pill (options + display labels come from the `/api/agents` runtime-selector contract — zero hardcoded lists) and, for backends with `supports_model_selection`, a model field with contract `suggested_models` as datalist suggestions plus free-form input (placeholder shows the backend's `default_model`); the visible composer state IS the per-turn request, mirroring the CLI shell ("what the pill shows is what runs", `model: ''` = explicit backend-default); switching backend clears the model selection (kernel rule: model ids are not portable across runtimes); switching to a chat restores the backend+model it runs on from `ChatSession.metadata.runtime` (per-chat stickiness); a backend without the contract loaded degrades to the current selection only and hides the model field. Why: this is the user-facing end of the runtime-selection chain (kernel `--model`/stickiness → contract → API → composer), giving每个 chat 独立可切换的 runtime/model。 Impact: presentation layer only — selection semantics, fail-closed rules, and handoff markers all live in the kernel/API; the chat status pill keeps showing the active backend. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` 52 passed, including three new tests driving the full path (contract-driven options render + model reaches the chat request + backend switch clears the model field; sticky restore on chat switch + reset-to-default for chats without a recorded runtime; delivery activation keeps the selection); `npm run build`; live preview confirms graceful degradation without a backend API. Reviewed by Codex across three adversarial rounds (cross-chat selection leak, late-arriving metadata, hidden stale model submission, delivery-branch reset, and every user-triggerable backend switch路径 clearing the model — all fixed; final verdict PASS), which also surfaced and fixed a pre-existing footgun where every config refresh treated the literal 'claude' selection as an untouched sentinel and stomped it with the configured default. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Wired the per-chat runtime selection into the shared contract and the API surface. What changed: `AGENT_CONTROL_SPECS` (ui_contracts.py) now carries the runtime-selector contract per backend — `label`, `supports_model_selection` (False ⇒ the kernel fails closed on an explicit override and the UI must not offer a model picker, e.g. local/openclaw-gateway), `default_model`, `suggested_models` (dropdown candidates; free-form stays allowed), `chat_capable` — projected into `build_agent_inventory()` so `/api/agents` serves it; the channel-migration backends (opencode/grok/cursor/http) are now first-class contract entries, and their env vars (`SUPERCLAW_{OPENCODE,GROK,CURSOR}_{EXECUTABLE,MODEL}`, `SUPERCLAW_HTTP_{URL,METHOD,HEADERS,TIMEOUT_SEC,ALLOW_PRIVATE}`) are registered in runtime_config (HTTP headers secret/non-persist). API: `ChatTurnRequest.backend_policy` is now optional and `model` is new — every chat endpoint (`/api/chat/turn`, `/api/chat/stream`, legacy `/api/chat`) resolves the turn through the kernel's `resolve_chat_runtime()` (request > sticky > default), persists the chat's sticky runtime, appends the `system` handoff marker on a mid-chat backend switch, and threads the resolved backend+model into the run; `RunRequest`/`DirectChatRequest` gain `model`; the chat's model selection reaches the codex direct-chat path ONLY when the selected runtime is codex (another runtime's model id must not leak into codex), and the streaming chat's cached codex app-server session is now keyed by model — a model change rebuilds the session via `thread/resume` (which carries the model, PR-A) so native memory survives without silently answering on the old model (the exact risk Codex's PR-B review flagged). Why: surfaces must mirror kernel runtime semantics, not reimplement them; the Web/macOS composer dropdown reads this contract instead of hardcoding lists. Impact: additive; requests that keep sending explicit `backend_policy` behave as before, and omitting it now engages the chat's sticky runtime. Verification: 5 new API tests (sticky recorded + reused when omitted, handoff marker + model drop on switch via API, codex-only model forwarding into direct chat, `/api/runs` model → execution_context, `/api/agents` selector contract incl. the four channel backends) + updated inventory contract test; api/ui_contracts suites 120 passed (1 pre-existing unrelated failure). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_ui_contracts.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added per-chat runtime stickiness: a chat session now remembers the backend + model it runs on, with kernel-owned switch semantics (`chat_runtime.py`). What changed: `resolve_chat_runtime()` resolves every turn as explicit request > the chat's sticky runtime (`ChatSession.metadata["runtime"]`) > configured default, and encodes the in-chat handoff the user chose: switching backend mid-chat stays in the SAME chat, drops the sticky model (model ids are not portable across runtimes; an explicit same-turn model survives), and yields a `handoff_note` the surface appends as a `system` transcript marker; `REQUEST_CLEAR` ("") is the explicit clear-model request, distinct from "nothing requested". `StateStore` gains `get_chat_runtime`/`set_chat_runtime` (idempotent row writes). CLI: `superclaw chat --backend/--model` defaults now fall back to the chat's sticky runtime instead of hardcoding claude; the interactive shell gains `/model [NAME|clear]`, clears the model on `/backend` switch, shows the model in the bottom toolbar, passes the visible state as the per-turn request (what the toolbar shows is what runs), and adopts a resumed session's sticky runtime on `--session-id`/`--continue`; `execute_direct_chat_turn` accepts the model (codex exec `--model`, legacy CLI mode fails closed with `MODEL_OVERRIDE_UNSUPPORTED` — same rule as the kernel backend). Why: the user-facing design is a per-chat runtime selector (composer dropdown on Web/macOS); the iron law requires the stickiness/handoff semantics to live in the kernel and CLI first so the API/Web layers can only mirror them. Impact: additive; a chat that never selects a runtime behaves exactly as before (configured default + env model). Verification: 11 new kernel tests (`tests/test_chat_runtime.py`: request>sticky>default, switch-drops-model, explicit-model-survives-switch, REQUEST_CLEAR, idempotent apply, store roundtrip, direct-chat exec `--model` + legacy fail-closed) and 5 new CLI tests (sticky recorded + reused across turns, handoff `system` marker on switch, `/model` set/clear + `/backend` drop, shell passes model to both turn kinds, sticky adoption on resume); cli/chat/state/api suites 195 passed with only the 3 pre-existing unrelated failures. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_runtime.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/chat_turn.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/state.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_chat_runtime.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added a first-class per-run model selection to the kernel (`superclaw run --model`, `WorkerLimits.model_override`). What changed: `WorkerLimits` carries an optional `model_override` that takes precedence over each backend's `SUPERCLAW_*_MODEL` env default, resolved through one shared `_resolve_model()` helper; the orchestrator threads `model` through `run_goal` / `run_existing_goal` / `create_run_session` / `start_existing_goal` / `execute_existing_session`, persists it in `execution_context["model"]` (so `resume_run` re-applies it), records it on the `run.started` event, and child runs inherit the parent's model only when they also inherit the parent's backend (model ids are not portable across runtimes). Per backend: claude/opencode/grok/cursor/bobo/hermes/openclaw pass `--model <override>`; codex passes `--model` in exec mode (new `SUPERCLAW_CODEX_MODEL` env) and fails closed in legacy CLI mode; codex-app-server forwards the model to `thread/start`/`thread/resume` (protocol field verified against the generated app-server JSON schema and a live codex which echoes the negotiated model back) and keys its cached sessions by model so a different selection never silently reuses an old thread; anthropic/gemini/anthropic-agent use the override in their API payloads; http forwards an optional `"model"` body field for the endpoint to honor (new `SUPERCLAW_HTTP_MODEL`); local and openclaw-gateway cannot honor a model and refuse the run (`MODEL_OVERRIDE_UNSUPPORTED`) rather than silently executing on a different model. Why: runtime/model selection is becoming a per-chat, per-turn choice across CLI and the client apps, and the iron law requires the capability to live in the kernel first — surfaces only call it. Impact: additive; runs without a model selection behave exactly as before (env-default path unchanged). Verification: 27 new tests (`tests/test_worker_backends.py` override-beats-env across 7 CLI backends + codex exec/legacy + local/gateway fail-closed + http forward/omit + anthropic/gemini/anthropic-agent payloads; `tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py` session-separation-by-model + thread/start/resume model params; `tests/test_orchestrator.py` WorkerLimits threading, context persistence, resume re-application, child inheritance rules; `tests/test_cli.py` `--model` lands in execution_context); affected suites 304 passed (2 pre-existing unrelated failures); real smokes: `codex app-server` accepts and echoes `thread/start.model`, `codex exec --help` confirms `--model`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/codex_app_server.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added Cmd/Ctrl `+` / `-` / `0` whole-page UI zoom to the web workbench (and therefore the embedded Tauri desktop webview). What changed: a new `apps/web/src/zoom.ts` listens for the standard zoom shortcuts (including numpad), steps through a browser-style zoom ladder (0.5–2.0), applies the level as CSS `zoom` on the root element, persists it to `localStorage` (`superclaw.uiZoom`), and restores it on startup via `initUiZoom()` in `main.tsx`. Why: WKWebView (Tauri) has no native Cmd+/− page zoom, so the desktop app could not be scaled at all; one frontend implementation keeps Web and Desktop behavior identical. Desktop follow-up: CSS `zoom` in WKWebView does not compensate the layout viewport (shrinking collapsed the shell into a corner instead of reflowing the frame), so inside Tauri the same shortcut path now calls a new `desktop_set_webview_zoom` command (`WebviewWindow::set_zoom` → native `pageZoom`, browser-zoom semantics, factor clamped 0.25–4.0) while browsers keep the CSS `zoom` path. Impact: presentation-layer only — no kernel/CLI semantics touched; CSS `zoom` was chosen over root font-size scaling because the stylesheet mixes `px`/`rem` (font scaling would skew proportions) and standardized `zoom` leaves `vh`/`vw` units unscaled, so the full-height shell does not overflow. In Chrome the handler `preventDefault`s to avoid double zoom; Safari-the-browser keeps its native menu shortcut (page never sees the key), which yields the same visual result. Verification: `npm test --prefix apps/web` (48 passed, incl. 3 new shortcut/persist/clamp tests) plus live preview checks: key dispatch steps the ladder, reload restores the persisted level, and a 1.25× screenshot matches an unzoomed 80%-sized viewport (pre-existing small-viewport layout, no zoom artifact). Files: `apps/web/src/zoom.ts`, `apps/web/src/main.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added an OpenClaw Gateway worker backend (`openclaw-gateway`) that drives OpenClaw over its WebSocket Gateway protocol instead of the local CLI. What changed: a new `openclaw_gateway.py` client speaks the gateway handshake (server `connect.challenge` → Ed25519-signed v3 device identity + shared token → `hello-ok`) and runs one agent turn (`agent` → `runId` → `agent.wait`); `OpenClawGatewayBackend` wires it into `default_backends()`, maps the terminal status to a WorkerResult, and `runtime_config.py` exposes `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_URL` / `_TOKEN` (secret) / `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_DEVICE_KEY_PATH` / `_ALLOW_INSECURE`. Why: SuperClaw should be able to reach an OpenClaw instance over its native gateway (native auth/connect semantics, remote nodes) and not only the local `openclaw` CLI. Impact: additive backend + `websocket-client>=1.8` dependency (Ed25519 reuses the existing `cryptography` dep); existing backends and governance are unchanged. Security: refuses plaintext `ws://` to non-loopback hosts (token/prompt/signature would be sniffable) unless `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_ALLOW_INSECURE=true`; writes the device key 0600 and refuses a group/world-readable or non-Ed25519 existing key; rejects a token containing the v3 delimiter; strips URL userinfo/query from every logged/transcript surface; verifies the negotiated protocol before sending an agent turn; fail-closed on plugin_dirs/mcp_configs. A new device must be approved once by an operator (`openclaw devices approve`); a configured key then stays paired. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py -k openclaw_gateway` (22 tests: backend mock, scripted-socket protocol, ws-loopback policy, URL redaction, missing-dep, timeout→124, key-0600/loose-key/Ed25519, protocol-negotiation, cancel-before-start, cancel-after-connect), full worker-backends + permission_presets + ui_contracts green, and a real end-to-end smoke against a live local `openclaw gateway` (2026.6.5): connect → agent → agent.wait round-trips (the agent itself erroring only on OpenClaw's own missing `node:sqlite`, not the protocol). Reviewed by Codex + Gemini. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/openclaw_gateway.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added four agent-runtime worker backends — OpenCode, Grok, Cursor, and a generic HTTP backend — porting Paperclip's adapter channel-access knowledge into SuperClaw's `WorkerBackend` contract. What changed: `OpenCodeCliBackend` (`opencode run --format json --pure`), `GrokCliBackend` (`grok -p`), and `CursorCliBackend` (`cursor-agent -p --output-format stream-json --workspace --trust [--force]`) drive their local CLIs headless and are registered in `default_backends()`; `HttpBackend` POSTs the framed task to an operator-configured endpoint (`SUPERCLAW_HTTP_URL`) and reads `{output, exit_code, usage}` back, the lowest-friction way to bring a bring-your-own runtime in over the wire. All four are fail-closed on plugin_dirs/mcp_configs (each remote/CLI runtime resolves its own tools; SuperClaw does not project MCP), use preset-driven or honestly-declared `preset_driven=False` permission mappings, and resolve executables via `SUPERCLAW_<NAME>_EXECUTABLE` without hard-coded paths. Why: SuperClaw's runtime matrix should cover the mainstream local coding agents and a generic network seam, reusing one governed backend contract rather than a parallel implementation per surface. Impact: additive worker-backend capability and `_transcript_safe_policy_dict` transcript hardening (mcp/plugin paths collapse to counts) only; existing backends, permission projection, and plugin governance are unchanged. Security: `HttpBackend` blocks non-global (private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT) hosts by default (SSRF), refuses redirects, caps the response read (OOM), and redacts URL credentials + reflected auth tokens from evidence; OpenCode's real-smoke exposed and fixed an exit-0 auth-failure false-success via a JSON `error`-event marker. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py tests/test_permission_presets.py tests/test_ui_contracts.py` (124 passed), real smokes against installed opencode 1.16.2 / grok 1.0.1 / cursor-agent 2026.06.04 plus a live local-server HTTP smoke, and adversarial review by Codex + Gemini per backend. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `docs/clawwork-two-track-dev-plan.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added a graded capability-surface resume guard (PR-1). What changed: a new `capability_surface.py` fingerprints the skills / plugin-tool catalog / permission mode / backend / model / revocation epoch a chat session was started under, and grades a pre-resume diff into actions — permission widening (ask→allow) hard-blocks (drops the codex thread so the over-authorized world-view resets), a revoked capability injects a CRITICAL "do NOT use" notice, a changed schema/skill body confirms, and pure additions inject a soft note; `ChatSession` persists the surface in metadata (idempotent) and the `/api/chat` codex-thread reattach point applies the guard with a new `strict_resume` flag. Why: the governance gate keeps execution safe when a session's world-view goes stale, but it cannot erase a capability the model still remembers — a resumed session otherwise burns turns on a revoked tool or resumes under wider authority than it was planned against. Impact: additive resume-time guard on direct chat only; runs without a stored surface behave exactly as before. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_capability_surface.py` (21 passed) and `tests/test_api.py` / `tests/test_desktop_runtime.py`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_surface.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/state.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_capability_surface.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Fixed

- Eradicated ghost "in progress" states: old sessions and runs no longer spin forever in the sidebar. What changed (kernel-first, 4 commits): (1) `superclaw.liveness` is the single liveness contract — a stored `running/verifying` status must be backed by an unexpired `RunMutationLease` whose owner still renews it (`last_renewed_at` heartbeat at TTL/3 with jitter, TTL 900s→120s) or `effective_run_state()` reads it as `failed(worker_lost)`; `execute_existing_session` now catches `BaseException` (was: only the lease-lost RuntimeError) and closes any escaped error out with reconcile semantics — resumable frontier ⇒ `queued`, else `failed`, never a lingering `running` claim. (2) Reconciliation is automatic: one synchronous sweep at API startup, a 60s background sweeper, lazy reconcile on `GET /api/runs/{id}`, and `superclaw reconcile --all`; every path goes through the full `reconcile_run()` state machine, never a bare status overwrite. (3) Chat messages are a pure transcript again: session activity (`live/waiting/pending/interrupted/idle` + `is_live`) derives from the liveness of the newest linked run via `chat_session_activity()`; sessions whose transcript ends in a user message nothing alive backs read as `interrupted` with `legacy_incomplete_tail` — no fabricated assistant failure messages. (4) The web sidebar spinner renders only from liveness proof (`activity.is_live` / `run.is_live` / this tab's own in-flight turn), and a stale `working` tail rehydrated from localStorage proves nothing. Why: the stored status was trusted as if it were liveness, and the frontend invented business state from message shapes — both lied after any crash/kill/reload. Impact: kernel + API + CLI + Web; desktop inherits via `superclaw service` → `create_app`. Real-data smoke: the production state.db's stuck `running` run converged to `queued (resumable)` and all 9 dangling-tail sessions read `interrupted/is_live=false`. Verification: full pytest suite (1175 passed, 1 pre-existing worktree-environmental failure), `npm test --prefix apps/web` (54 passed incl. a new never-spin-from-message-shape regression test), `npm run build`. Design rationale + two-round Codex/Gemini adjudication: `docs/ghost-state-eradication-plan.md`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/{liveness.py,models.py,state.py,orchestrator.py,cli.py}`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/{test_liveness.py,test_orchestrator.py,test_api.py,test_cli.py}`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `docs/ghost-state-eradication-plan.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Separated the Skills tab from plugin marketplace install controls. What changed: the desktop plugin workspace now keeps marketplace hero/search/filter/create/install actions on the Plugins tab only, lets the Skills tab ignore stale plugin search/filter state, and renders skill-origin rows without plugin install buttons. Why: selecting Skills should show skill projection and skill inventory semantics instead of continuing the plugin marketplace installation flow below the fold. Impact: desktop plugin workspace UI and regression coverage only; plugin installation APIs, skill projection APIs, and governance checks are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx --testNamePattern "filters the marketplace skill view"` and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added PaySwitch Browser Relay extension readiness to plugin configuration. What changed: the PaySwitch plugin config panel now probes browser-relay status/install actions, shows connection and heartbeat details, displays user-facing Chrome extension install guidance without raw relay tokens, and continuously refreshes the status while the PaySwitch config is open. Why: PaySwitch needs both the configured Chrome profile and its browser extension relay before real browser-backed payment actions are safe to run, and extension installation must remain explicit and visible to the user. Impact: PaySwitch plugin configuration UI only; plugin-side browser-relay gating is implemented in the PaySwitch source package. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Fixed PaySwitch plugin chat execution prompts so explicit purchase requests map to live intent fields. What changed: `@plugin` chat turns now tell the projected plugin agent to pass `execute=true` and `dry_run=false` for concrete current-turn purchase/subscribe/top-up/pay/confirm-payment requests, while keeping inspect/prepare/test/question requests in `execute=false` and `dry_run=true`, and reusing a reported `configured_profile` instead of asking for a Chrome profile again. Why: a user prompt such as "buy the cheapest monthly Kimi membership" was concrete enough to request execution, but the generic payment safety prompt left the model on the manifest's safe defaults. Impact: SuperClaw plugin-task prompt construction only; PaySwitch still performs its own authorization/profile/live-execution gates and real payment remains blocked for ambiguous requests. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py::test_plugin_task_prompt_maps_explicit_payswitch_purchase_to_live_fields tests/test_intent_routing.py -q`, desktop backend rebuild, installed app strict codesign, and installed backend PaySwitch auth/profile smoke. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Fixed desktop backend startup so persisted plugin runtime settings reach the service process. What changed: the desktop runtime supervisor now includes non-secret persisted runtime environment values when launching the bundled backend, while preserving explicit environment variables and supervisor overrides. Why: saved settings such as `SUPERCLAW_PLUGIN_LOCAL_DEV_TRUST=1` appeared configured in the UI but were absent from the backend process, causing local dev plugin execution to fail with `PLUGIN_SIGNATURE_INVALID`. Impact: desktop-launched backend environment only; secret values remain excluded, explicit overrides keep precedence, and plugin signature policy is unchanged. Verification: `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_desktop_runtime.py -q`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/desktop_runtime.py`, `tests/test_desktop_runtime.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Bridged SuperClaw ClawHunt login to Pay-Switch scoped authorization. What changed: the plugin proxy now supports a manifest opt-in `clawhunt_account_bridge` that uses the local SuperClaw ClawHunt account token inside SuperClaw to exchange for a scoped PayAgent token, injects only the declared `PAY_SWITCH_AGENT_TOKEN` secret into the sidecar, keeps plugin config URL/panel URL settings exportable as runtime env, passes only safe runtime variables plus a certifi CA bundle to plugin sidecars for HTTPS token validation, and reports `PLUGIN_ACCOUNT_BRIDGE_FAILED` when the ClawHunt-to-PaySwitch exchange fails before the sidecar starts. Why: logging into ClawHunt through SuperClaw should automatically authorize PaySwitch without exposing the ClawHunt account token to plugin code, the PaySwitch sidecar must be able to validate the scoped token over TLS in SuperClaw's sanitized environment, and bridge failures should be distinguishable from a manually unconfigured plugin. Impact: plugin proxy, plugin manifest schema, and the locally installed PaySwitch dev package only; payment execution still remains behind PaySwitch Human Gate and existing plugin entitlement/policy checks. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_plugin_proxy.py`, manifest schema validation for PaySwitch source/cache manifests, local-dev-trust PaySwitch `auth_status` and dry-run `submit_intent` proxy smoke, installed bundle strict codesign, and installed backend `/health`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_proxy.py`, `schemas/superclaw-plugin.schema.json`, `tests/test_plugin_proxy.py`, `.superclaw/plugins/cache/dev.clawhunt.pay-switch-agent/0.2.0/superclaw-plugin.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Fixed plugin configuration option scans that posted JSON without a JSON content type. What changed: the shared web `readJson` helper now defaults string request bodies to `Content-Type: application/json` when the caller has not provided an explicit content type. Why: dynamic plugin configuration calls such as Pay-Switch Chrome profile scanning sent `JSON.stringify(...)` bodies without the header, so FastAPI parsed the request body as a string and returned `422`. Impact: frontend API request headers only; backend validation, plugin manifests, Pay-Switch scanning, and runtime authorization semantics are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, live 56800 options request with `Content-Type: application/json`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

Impact: desktop/web collapsed sidebar layout only; runtime APIs, delivery flows, plugin contracts, and installed backend behavior are unchanged. - Fixed ClawHunt account login post-configuration in the desktop shell. What changed: after a ClawHunt account login, the shell now automatically creates the SuperClaw Desktop agent key from the account's first agent when no agent key is stored yet, and the sidebar account block/account menu no longer show the misleading `ClawHunt linked` / `ClawHunt unset` status line. Why: `ClawHunt unset` described the runtime agent-key layer, not the website account login layer, so a successful Google login could still look unconfigured. Impact: desktop/web shell login completion and account display only; the shared account token store, CLI account login effect, backend source metadata, and manual agent-key fallback remain unchanged. - Fixed the desktop sidebar operator identity after ClawHunt account login. What changed: the shell account block and account menu now prefer the shared ClawHunt account user from `/api/auth/status`, show its avatar when available, keep the previous agent-profile fallback for agent-key-only setups, and treat a ClawHunt account login as linked even before an agent key is created. Why: Google/website login completed successfully, but the sidebar still read the legacy agent-key profile surface and showed `local-user` / `ClawHunt unset`. Impact: desktop/web shell identity display only; login callback, stored auth, CLI auth, and backend account semantics are unchanged. Verification: ClawHunt app-shell test and web build. - Fixed ClawHunt website login opening from the desktop app. What changed: desktop ClawHunt browser login now asks the native Tauri shell to open the ClawHunt login URL in the system browser, while browser mode still uses `window.open` and reports popup-block failures immediately. Why: the prior Settings button relied on WebView `window.open`, which can be ignored in the packaged desktop app so clicking the login button appears to do nothing. Impact: ClawHunt account login launch UX only; callback, shared CLI/desktop auth storage, agent key creation, and backend login semantics are unchanged. Verification: desktop config test, web app-shell test, desktop build, installed launch smoke. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/desktop.ts`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-config.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Fixed macOS desktop bundle signing after post-build plist preparation. What changed: the macOS prepare step now removes stale bundle signature resources, signs the main desktop executable before signing the top-level `SuperClaw.app` with WebKit-compatible hardened-runtime entitlements, and rebuilds the DMG from the signed app while avoiding recursive `codesign` signing that breaks LaunchServices smoke startup. Why: the previous script patched `Info.plist`/`PkgInfo` after Tauri build but intentionally skipped re-signing, leaving `codesign --strict` to report an incomplete resource seal; signing only the top-level bundle can validate but still be killed from `/Applications`; Tauri also generated the DMG before the post-build prepare step, so the packaged DMG could still contain the stale-signature app. Impact: macOS desktop packaging and installed-app verification only; runtime auth, CLI behavior, web UI, and backend APIs are unchanged. Verification: desktop config test, desktop build, strict codesign verification, DMG verification, installed launch smoke, and installed workbench smoke. Files: `apps/desktop/scripts/prepare-macos-bundle.mjs`, `apps/desktop/scripts/macos-entitlements.plist`, `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-config.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Hardened ClawHunt browser-login callback state handling and desktop polling. What changed: callback validation now uses constant-time state comparison, consumes a valid browser-login state on the first source/state-valid callback even when ClawHunt returns an error or token completion fails, and the desktop Settings login flow now polls auth status until the shared account appears or the state TTL expires. Why: Gemini acceptance identified low-risk replay/state-pinning and OAuth UX follow-ups after the website login bridge landed. Impact: ClawHunt browser callback security and desktop login completion UX only; CLI handoff, stored auth shape, and agent-key behavior are unchanged. Verification: targeted ClawHunt API/web tests. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/test_api.py`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Marked legacy ClawHunt password login and login-probe requests with SuperClaw source headers. What changed: factored ClawHunt account request source headers into a shared helper and reused it for password login, login probe, CLI handoff exchange, and bearer account APIs. Why: Gemini acceptance review caught that the compatibility password/probe endpoints were still backend-visible login traffic and must also identify `superclaw` as the source. Impact: ClawHunt account compatibility login/probe metadata only; Google bridge, browser callback, CLI handoff, and stored auth semantics are unchanged. Verification: `./.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_clawhunt_auth.py`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/clawhunt_auth.py`, `tests/test_clawhunt_auth.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Added

- Migrated SuperClaw ClawHunt account auth to the ClawHunt website login bridge. What changed: added ClawHunt Google bridge URL generation with `source=superclaw`, a protected desktop login start endpoint, a localhost callback that validates state/source and persists the returned ClawHunt token, CLI handoff-code exchange that writes the same auth store, source metadata in auth status, and desktop Settings buttons for ClawHunt Google/website login instead of making the account-password form the primary path. Why: SuperClaw login must match ClawHunt's own website/Google account system while still marking SuperClaw as the login source and producing one shared backend account effect for CLI and desktop. Impact: ClawHunt account auth, desktop Settings login UX, CLI ClawHunt auth commands, auth status payload, and docs/tests only; manual `cph_...` agent-key fallback and existing agent-key creation remain available. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_clawhunt_auth.py`, `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_clawhunt_exchange_handoff_code_saves_shared_account`, `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_api.py::test_api_clawhunt_browser_login_start_and_callback_persist_shared_account tests/test_api.py::test_api_clawhunt_browser_callback_rejects_missing_superclaw_source tests/test_api.py::test_api_clawhunt_browser_callback_exchanges_handoff_code`, and `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx --testNamePattern "opens ClawHunt website login"`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/clawhunt_auth.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `tests/test_clawhunt_auth.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `docs/desktop-beta-quickstart.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added Settings-controlled app appearance preferences. What changed: added a Settings appearance section with System/Light/Dark preferences, persisted the preference in `localStorage`, applied `html[data-theme]` before React boot to avoid theme flash, and synced the selected preference into the Tauri desktop window theme. Why: the marketplace default view should stay focused on server-provided plugin listings and installed/local categories, while configuration, package install, and governance details should appear only after the user enters that layer. - Promoted developer plugin creation into the main desktop marketplace flow. What changed: moved the `Create plugin` entry into the marketplace search/filter/refresh control row, added an expandable developer creation panel for submission creation, package upload, and review status, and kept audit/governance tooling inside the advanced operator disclosure. Why: developers should start plugin creation from the same marketplace surface where plugins are discovered and managed, instead of hunting through operator-only diagnostics. - Documented the plugin submission approval and signing trust boundary. What changed: added a dedicated protocol document for developer upload review, server-side approval, digest/hash calculation, isolated signing, registry allow state, entitlement, revocation, updates, key rotation, and local CLI mapping; linked it from the developer guide and ecosystem framework. Why: `superclaw plugin submit` can legitimately fail at the signing boundary when no SuperClaw private key is available, but production acceptance needs a clear rule that signing only happens after automated and required manual approvals for the exact package digest. Impact: documentation only; plugin review gates, signing code, registry sync, entitlement enforcement, revocation behavior, and runtime execution are unchanged. Verification: local document review and `git diff --check`. Files: `docs/plugin-submission-approval-signing.md`, `docs/plugin-developer-guide.md`, `docs/plugin-ecosystem-framework.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added a normalized local agent runtime contract across agent backends. What changed: introduced `LocalAgentRuntimeSpec` helpers for CLI subprocess, JSON-RPC app-server, and API-agent transports, and started recording that contract in worker transcripts for Codex app-server, Codex CLI, Claude Code CLI, Hermes CLI, Bobo, OpenClaw, Gemini agent, and Anthropic agent runs. Why: Hermes separates runtime/provider selection, transport shape, and session boundary before running a turn; SuperClaw needed the same observable contract before it can route and compare Codex app-server, Claude Code, Hermes, and other local agent runtimes as one family. Impact: additive transcript/runtime metadata only; existing backend commands, permission projection, timeout handling, and UI inventory behavior are unchanged. Verification: covered by `tests/test_local_agent_runtime.py`, existing worker backend regressions, py-compile, and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/local_agent_runtime.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `tests/test_local_agent_runtime.py`.

- Added a persistent Codex app-server worker backend. What changed: introduced `codex-app-server` as a first-class SuperClaw backend that starts `codex app-server` over JSON-RPC stdio, opens an ephemeral thread rooted at the worker repository, reuses that thread across turns, maps SuperClaw permission policy to Codex sandbox/approval settings, bridges command/file/permission approval requests, interrupts cancelled or timed-out turns, records raw runtime events in the transcript artifact, and exposes the backend through API/UI agent inventory and runtime settings. Why: SuperClaw's prior Codex path only spawned one-shot `codex exec` processes, while the Hermes comparison showed that a persistent app-server runtime gives stronger session continuity, lower startup overhead, richer event projection, and safer approval control. Impact: additive local backend capability only; the existing `codex` CLI backend remains unchanged and plugin directories are still rejected in favor of SuperClaw MCP proxy config projection. Verification: `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src /Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m py_compile packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/codex_app_server.py packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src /Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py -q`, `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src /Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py -q`, `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src /Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_ui_contracts.py -q`, `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src /Users/leongong/Documents/superClaw/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py -k "runtime_status_agents_and_plugins_contract or runtime_config" -q`, `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/codex_app_server.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py`.

### Fixed

- Hardened Codex app-server protocol parity with Hermes. What changed: Codex app-server sessions now tolerate `sessionId` thread-start responses, consume structured `item/completed` notifications for final assistant text and tool iteration counts, treat `<turn_aborted>` markers as terminal retire signals, and explicitly decline MCP elicitation requests instead of leaving Codex waiting on an unsupported method. Why: Hermes' app-server adapter handles these protocol edge cases so local agent turns do not hang or overcount tool activity. Impact: Codex app-server runtime robustness only; backend selection, permission mapping, CLI backends, and UI inventory are unchanged. Verification: covered by `tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py`, py-compile, and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/codex_app_server.py`, `tests/test_codex_app_server_runtime.py`.

### Added

- Updated the desktop application icon to the new SuperClaw logo artwork. What changed: replaced the Tauri bundle icon source with the supplied black-on-white SuperClaw mark at 512x512 RGBA so Tauri can generate the macOS `.icns` bundle icon. Why: the packaged desktop app should use the current SuperClaw branding instead of the earlier minimal placeholder icon. Impact: desktop packaging and LaunchServices icon appearance only; renderer UI, runtime APIs, plugin contracts, and delivery orchestration are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:config`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run tauri:build`, Tauri source icon inspection, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/icons/icon.png`.

- Added a typed plugin configuration contract for desktop-managed plugins. What changed: extended the plugin manifest and configuration-policy schemas, developer guide, ecosystem framework, and GitHub Scanner fixture so `configuration.settings[]` can declare optional `validation` and `ui` descriptors, made the desktop configuration panel render typed text/URL/textarea/number/switch/select controls from those descriptors, added a normative requirements document, changed the config-save API to validate setting names and values against the installed plugin manifest, made secret set/delete APIs reject undeclared secret names, enforced URL and numeric step rules server-side, and added semantic configuration contract gates to developer upload review and local package verification before signing or caching packages. Why: plugin authors need a rigorous source-of-truth contract for what appears in the desktop `Manage configuration` panel instead of treating config URLs, panel URLs, marketplace listing metadata, or credentials as independent runtime configuration sources. Impact: plugin configuration protocol, desktop plugin configuration UX, developer upload gates, local package verification, and local API validation only; plugin execution, entitlement, revocation, marketplace listing, and production secret storage semantics are unchanged. Verification: `PYTHONPATH=packages/superclaw/src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_install_and_configuration_control_plane tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_config_set_rejects_control_character_values tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_config_set_requires_manifest_descriptor tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_config_set_validates_manifest_setting_type_and_rules tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_secret_set_requires_manifest_descriptor tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_install_local_and_configuration_control_plane tests/test_plugin_config.py tests/test_plugin_local_verification.py tests/test_plugin_submission.py::test_developer_upload_rejects_manifest_sensitive_setting_default tests/test_plugin_submission.py::test_developer_upload_rejects_manifest_secret_like_setting_default tests/test_plugin_submission.py::test_developer_upload_rejects_invalid_configuration_contract tests/test_plugin_distribution_pack.py -k 'not smoke_scripts'`, `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run tauri:build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:launch-smoke`, installed `/Applications/SuperClaw.app` smoke JSON, and `git diff --check`. Files: `schemas/superclaw-plugin.schema.json`, `schemas/plugin-configuration-policy.schema.json`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_config.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugins.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_submission.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `examples/plugins/github-scanner/superclaw-plugin.json`, `examples/plugins/github-scanner/configuration-policy.json`, `docs/plugin-configuration-protocol-requirements.md`, `docs/plugin-developer-guide.md`, `docs/plugin-ecosystem-framework.md`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_plugin_local_verification.py`, `tests/test_plugin_submission.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Fixed macOS SuperClaw bundle post-processing. What changed: the macOS bundle preparation script now preserves Tauri's generated bundle and executable, removes only legacy plist keys that interfere with LaunchServices, and writes `PkgInfo` without rebuilding or ad-hoc re-signing the `.app`. Why: deep verification showed the previous rebuilt local `.app` could report `code has no resources but signature indicates they must be present`, and attempts to re-sign the copied executable caused macOS to kill the app before Tauri setup ran. Impact: desktop packaging/install verification only; renderer UI, runtime APIs, plugin configuration protocol, marketplace behavior, and delivery orchestration are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:config`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run tauri:build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:launch-smoke`, installed `/Applications/SuperClaw.app` smoke JSON, normal `open -a /Applications/SuperClaw.app`, and process check for `/Applications/SuperClaw.app/Contents/MacOS/superclaw_desktop`. Files: `apps/desktop/scripts/prepare-macos-bundle.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Reshaped the desktop web workbench into a Codex/OpenCode-like app shell. What changed: the app now opens as a chat-first blank canvas with a session-first left rail, top `Plugins` marketplace entry, `New Session`, `Recent Sessions`, a centered `What can I help you build?` composer, slash-command hints, a left-bottom account popover for user info, settings, and EN/ZH language switching, plus run/evidence/verdict panels hidden until the right-side context drawer is opened. Why: the prior beta shell looked like an evaluation dashboard instead of the intended Codex-style AgentOS workspace, so operators had to understand internal eval controls before they could simply chat, configure, or route delivery work. Impact: frontend layout, default composer mode, monochrome theme, and app-shell i18n only; run orchestration, direct-chat API, evidence bundle contracts, plugin governance, desktop runtime supervision, and ClawHunt task semantics are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:beta-acceptance`, in-app browser visual smoke, Gemini review, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`.

- Promoted Settings and Plugins into full-page app workspaces with enforced app i18n rules. What changed: Settings and Plugins now render as structural `.workspace-page` surfaces with a localized top-left return action instead of a modal/popup overlay, the Settings page keeps runtime details and token rotation after the layout change, local dry-run launch returns to chat and opens the Run Cockpit context rail, and the Settings/Plugins plus core chat/context/evidence user-facing static copy is wired through EN/ZH dictionaries while runtime technical data remains raw. Why: the Codex-like app shell should keep chat as the default workspace and use full product pages for settings/plugin management instead of cluttered popups. Impact: frontend layout, i18n coverage, and regression policy only; runtime APIs, plugin verification contracts, evidence semantics, and ClawHunt task data are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, in-app browser verification for default chat/Settings/Plugins/right context, Gemini review, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `docs/codex-like-agentos-app.md`.

- Made the Codex-like sidebar and plugin marketplace behave as first-class app modules. What changed: the sidebar is now an independent fixed-height app rail with persistent collapse/expand state, internal recent-session scrolling, collapsed icon-only controls, and a visible account icon; the Plugins workspace now opens on a marketplace-style discover/install surface with search, catalog cards, install readiness, installed-plugin strip, and advanced diagnostics hidden behind a disclosure. Why: the desktop shell should not scroll the sidebar with page content, and the Plugins page should primarily help users choose and install trusted plugins instead of showing raw governance diagnostics first. Impact: frontend structure and product UX only; plugin verification, install gates, entitlement sync, runtime policy, and local API contracts are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, Gemini review, and `git diff --check`. - Added draggable resizing for the desktop sidebar. What changed: the expanded sidebar width now uses a persisted `--sidebar-width` value, exposes a vertical resize separator, clamps drag width between 220px and 380px, and keeps the collapsed icon rail fixed at 76px. Why: the sidebar is an independent app module and should support operator-controlled width like mature desktop workbenches instead of being locked to a hard-coded 260px. Impact: frontend shell ergonomics only; workspace routing, plugin marketplace behavior, runtime APIs, and evidence workflows are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, and `git diff --check`.
