- Aligned the macOS titlebar controls and responsive chat canvas for the desktop app. What changed: added a shared titlebar layout script, synchronized Tauri traffic-light position with CSS titlebar tokens, applied the macOS inset through decorum at runtime, and centered the chat goal pane within the available workspace while letting the right context rail compress the chat area when opened. Why: the Codex-like desktop shell needs the native window controls, collapse button, and blank chat state to feel structurally correct instead of visually drifting or staying left-biased on large windows. Impact: desktop window chrome and frontend layout only; runtime APIs, plugin contracts, delivery orchestration, and evidence semantics are unchanged. - Made desktop-launched agent subprocesses inherit a usable local toolchain path. What changed: added a desktop toolchain PATH/env helper, taught Codex executable discovery to search common macOS GUI-missing locations such as Homebrew and `~/.local/bin`, passed that environment into Codex mode probes, direct-chat subprocess execution, and desktop sidecar service startup, and wrote sidecar stdout/stderr to a local runtime log while starting it in a separate process session. Why: apps launched from Finder or LaunchServices often lack the interactive shell PATH, so a configured local Codex agent could appear missing or fail when started from the desktop app even though it works in Terminal. Impact: local subprocess environment, sidecar launch diagnostics, and agent discovery only; backend protocols, plugin contracts, delivery orchestration, evidence semantics, and user secrets remain unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_desktop_toolchain_contract tests/test_cli.py -k "direct_chat or shell_auto_intent"`, `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_runtime.py tests/test_desktop_runtime.py`, `npm --prefix apps/web test -- --run`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/desktop_runtime.py`, `tests/test_runtime.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_desktop_runtime.py`.

- Fixed the macOS desktop bundle preparation path for local installation. What changed: the Tauri bundle now declares the app icon, and the desktop build runs a macOS post-processing step that preserves the working linker-signed release executable, removes stale resource signatures, writes `PkgInfo`, and removes the legacy Carbon launch marker before copying the app bundle. Why: ad-hoc deep signing the generated bundle on this machine rewrote the executable into a SIGKILL-at-start binary, while the unsigned bundle lacked standard icon resources and could fail LaunchServices validation. Impact: desktop packaging/install reliability only; renderer UI, runtime APIs, plugin contracts, and delivery orchestration are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:config`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run tauri:build`, installed `/Applications/SuperClaw.app` opened with `frontmost=true, visible=true, windows=1`, Computer Use visual inspection, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/desktop/package.json`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `apps/desktop/scripts/prepare-macos-bundle.mjs`, `apps/desktop/tests/desktop-config.test.mjs`.

- Documented the near-term TUI and desktop app roadmap. What changed: added a dedicated roadmap for improving the current prompt shell, introducing a future full-screen TUI, and maturing the Tauri desktop app around one shared SuperClaw runtime service. Why: the project needs a concrete execution plan for the Codex-like AgentOS direction without replacing the existing CLI or duplicating orchestration logic in the UI layer. Impact: documentation only; CLI, API, desktop runtime, plugin governance, delivery orchestration, and ClawHunt behavior are unchanged. Verification: local document review and `git diff --check`. Files: `docs/tui-desktop-roadmap.md`.

- Resolved 22 capability-atlas gap units to their real GitHub sources and corrected availability grades. What changed: a resolution pass located the `declared` skills and empty orphan submodules across all 152 Arxchibobo repos — 17 units have dedicated repos and 5 live in `gtm-claw-capability`, so those 22 were re-graded from `declared`/empty-`vendored` to `availability: external` with a corrected `source_url`; the three empty submodules with no GitHub source (`comfyui-skill-openclaw`, `infographic-skill`, `slides`) were honestly downgraded from `vendored` to `declared`; the remaining 52 declared units are git symlinks into the OpenClaw host's `company-skills/` workspace and stay `declared`. New availability distribution: 92 local, 55 declared, 54 external, 44 vendored. Why: the initial scan optimistically graded 18 empty orphan submodules as `vendored` and left resolvable skills as `declared` gaps, so the coverage report overstated what was actually on disk. Impact: catalog metadata accuracy only; loader, CLI, API, and suggestion ranking are unchanged (`capabilities doctor` ok, coverage ready dropped 154→136 to reflect reality). Verification: `python -m pytest tests/test_capability_atlas.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_api.py`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_atlas.json`, `tests/test_capability_atlas.py`, `docs/capability-atlas-scan-notes.md`, `docs/declared-skill-sources.md`.

- Hardened the capability atlas after a real-usage probe of every SuperClaw surface. What changed: unknown facet filter values now fail loud instead of silently returning an empty set — `superclaw capabilities list/search` exit 1 with the known-values list, and `GET /api/capabilities?category=…` returns `422` with the same detail (new `validate_facets()` helper); `capabilities suggest --limit 0` and negative limits now return an empty suggestion list instead of falling through to `max(1, limit)` and emitting one unrequested result. Why: a live probe across the CLI, FastAPI service (verified `/api/capabilities*`, run loop, SSE), and the five-target harness emit/validate round-trip found that facet typos read as "no matches" and that the limit floor leaked a result at `--limit 0`. Impact: additive validation and a bounded edge-case fix on read-only capability surfaces; catalog data, run orchestration, and all other surfaces (full 767-test suite green, harness round-trip ok=true for codex/cursor/opencode/gemini/claude-code) are unchanged. Verification: `python -m pytest tests/test_capability_atlas.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_api.py`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_atlas.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_capability_atlas.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_api.py`.

- Fixed process-environment leakage between CLI/TUI shell tests. What changed: `test_cli.py` and `test_tui.py` now snapshot-and-clear env vars that shell commands write into live `os.environ` (`/config set`, `/setup`, `/login`) via a `_guard_process_env` helper / inline guard, so a `monkeypatch.delenv(..., raising=False)` on an initially-unset variable no longer records an empty undo entry that restores the polluted value at teardown. Why: running `test_cli.py` before `test_api.py` previously failed `test_api_agents_include_configure_hints_and_config_state` because `SUPERCLAW_CODEX_EXECUTABLE=/tmp/codex` leaked across files. Impact: test-only hygiene; product behavior unchanged. Files: `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_tui.py`.

- Added the embedded capability atlas: a typed, queryable profile of 245 ecosystem capability units. What changed: introduced `superclaw.capability_atlas` with a packaged `capability_atlas.json` catalog covering self-built skills (90 declared, 62 vendored sources scanned), vendored external skills, all 81 wshobson/agents plugins, the 14 OpenClaw built-in skills, ljg-skills (21 found upstream), and external tools/services — each unit carrying category, origin, source URL, description, trigger phrases, integration kind, and an availability grade (`vendored`/`local`/`external`/`declared`); added `superclaw capabilities summary/list/show/search/suggest/coverage/matrix/adaptable/doctor` CLI commands, public read-only `GET /api/capabilities`, `/api/capabilities/{id}`, `/api/capabilities/coverage`, and `/api/capabilities/suggest` routes, a `skills/superclaw-capability-atlas` operating skill, and trigger-based `suggest_capabilities()` goal ranking for run planning. The loader fails closed on unknown categories/integrations/availabilities, duplicate ids, and missing data. Why: SuperClaw's planning had no machine-readable inventory of the 238+ capability units cataloged in the all-skills manifest, so goal intake could not consult the ecosystem's real capability profile and coverage gaps (59 declared-but-missing skill sources, 18 empty orphan submodules) stayed invisible. Impact: additive read-only metadata surface; run orchestration, evidence, verification, plugin governance, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are unchanged; `adaptable` units reuse the existing harness adapt/emit pipeline rather than adding a new execution path. Verification: `python -m pytest tests/test_capability_atlas.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_api.py tests/test_harness.py` (183 passed; the one failure is a pre-existing env-var pollution in `test_cli_shell_config_persists_non_secret_values_but_not_secret_values` that only reproduces when test_cli runs before test_api, tracked separately). Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_atlas.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/capability_atlas.json`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `skills/superclaw-capability-atlas/SKILL.md`, `docs/capability-atlas.md`, `docs/capability-atlas-scan-notes.md`, `tests/test_capability_atlas.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `README.md`.

- Added a one-step RunningHub media render chain. What changed: introduced shared `render_runninghub_media_task` orchestration, `superclaw media render`, protected `POST /api/media/render`, and a Workbench `Generate and wait` action that submits media generation, polls status/outputs, extracts `output_urls`, summarizes every step artifact, and attaches generate/status/outputs artifacts to the selected run EvidenceBundle. Why: media generation needed a single operator-grade end-to-end path instead of requiring manual task-id copying across generate/status/output calls. Impact: additive media workflow only; dry-run remains protected, live calls still require runtime keys and node info, API keys remain redacted, and every persisted step artifact keeps the existing run-root ownership guard. Verification: covered by provider, CLI, API, app-shell, static UI, and web build regressions. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/media.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_media.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_web_static.py`, `docs/runninghub-media.md`, `README.md`.

- Added run-bound RunningHub Media Studio controls in the Fusion Shell. What changed: the workbench `Media Studio` tab now submits media generation requests, supports dry-run/live mode, accepts prompt/source image/nodeInfoList/input JSON values, queries task status and outputs, extracts provider `fileUrl` values into `output_urls`, downloads the latest sanitized media artifact, and refreshes the selected run EvidenceBundle after media actions. Why: RunningHub support needed to be operable from the desktop/web shell, not just from CLI/API calls, so end-to-end media generation can be reviewed beside run evidence. Impact: additive frontend/provider workflow only; provider keys remain server-side, default dry-run behavior is preserved, and live generation still fails closed without configured runtime keys plus RunningHub node data. Verification: covered by app-shell, provider, and static UI regressions plus web build. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/styles.css`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/media.py`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_media.py`, `tests/test_web_static.py`, `docs/runninghub-media.md`.

- Added run-attached RunningHub media evidence. What changed: `superclaw media generate/upload/task-status/outputs` and the matching `/api/media/*` routes now accept optional `run_id`, constrain run-scoped artifact writes to the run artifact root, append sanitized media artifacts to the EvidenceBundle, emit `artifact.added` plus `media.*.recorded` events, and return a run artifact download URL. Why: media generation needed to participate in SuperClaw's evidence, audit, and delivery chain instead of only writing standalone provider artifacts. Impact: additive evidence attachment only; standalone media artifacts still work without `run_id`, keys remain runtime-only/redacted, and out-of-run artifact paths fail closed. Verification: covered by focused API/CLI regression tests. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/media.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `docs/runninghub-media.md`.

- Added governed RunningHub media generation for image and video tasks. What changed: introduced a secret-safe `superclaw.media` provider wrapper with round-robin `SUPERCLAW_RUNNINGHUB_API_KEYS`, built-in template ids for text-to-image, image-to-image, image-to-video, and text-to-video, dry-run artifact capture, local media upload support, live task submission, task status/output queries, protected `/api/media/*` routes, `superclaw media status/templates/generate/upload/task-status/outputs` CLI commands, and a Fusion Shell `Media Studio` entry that records catalog evidence without exposing keys to the browser. Why: SuperClaw lacked first-class image and video generation, so delivery agents had no governed path for media creation artifacts. Impact: additive media surface only; keys remain runtime-only, generated artifacts are redacted, and live calls fail closed unless keys plus RunningHub `nodeInfoList` or configured node mappings are present. Verification: covered by focused API/CLI/web tests and documented in `docs/runninghub-media.md`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/media.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `.env.example`, `docs/runninghub-media.md`.

- Added structured API-agent transcript stream events. What changed: Gemini and Anthropic API-agent loops now persist redacted `stream_events` in worker transcript artifacts for model responses, tool calls, tool results, finish signals, terminal status, and model-call errors, and runtime comparison now reflects transcript stream capture while keeping live model passthrough as the remaining gap. Why: text transcripts made agent loops inspectable by humans, but replay/audit tooling could not machine-read the model/tool turn structure. Impact: additive transcript metadata and runtime-comparison accuracy only; live event streaming, worker scheduling, tool execution, redaction policy, chain verdicts, desktop/TUI surfaces, plugin governance, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are otherwise unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py tests/test_runtime.py -q`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `tests/test_runtime.py`, `docs/agent-comparison.md`.
- Added evidence metadata finding events for primary-evidence truncation. What changed: worker-result evidence normalization now emits `evidence.finding` events for newly recorded non-verification findings such as `primary_evidence_truncated`, while adversarial verifier findings continue to use `verification.finding`; tests prove long primary output creates metadata events without misclassifying truncation disclosure as verifier output. Why: truncation metadata was visible in the final evidence bundle, but operators following the event stream could not see when evidence became truncated. Impact: additive event/audit visibility only; evidence normalization, chain verdicts, verifier events, worker scheduling, API/TUI event streaming, plugin governance, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are otherwise unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py -q`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`, `docs/claude-code-sourcemap-notes.md`.
- Added first-class runtime permission/context events for real worker attempts. What changed: serial and parallel worker execution now emit `permission.requested`, `permission.decided`, and `context.usage` events with task/backend/attempt identity, the effective non-interactive permission policy, primary output usage, truncation metadata, and evidence surface counts. Why: the Claude Code sourcemap comparison called out permission and context-usage runtime events as an inspectability gap, while SuperClaw already had the persisted event stream needed to expose those decisions without adding an interactive permission loop. Impact: additive event visibility only; worker scheduling, permission enforcement/projection, cancellation, evidence artifacts, chain verdicts, desktop/TUI surfaces, plugin governance, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are otherwise unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py -q`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/claude-code-sourcemap-notes.md`.
- Added explicit primary-evidence truncation disclosure. What changed: command and worker-result evidence now retain truncation metadata when primary output exceeds the 4000-character evidence budget, worker backends record original redacted-output length before writing the capped tail, and normalized evidence adds a non-failing, non-verification `primary_evidence_truncated` warning finding pointing reviewers to full-output artifacts when needed. Why: long worker/MCP-style output was already capped for evidence payload hygiene, but reviewers could not always tell whether leading context had been omitted from the primary evidence surface. Impact: additive evidence metadata and finding visibility only; output caps, artifact/transcript capture, chain-verdict proof/failure semantics, orchestration, desktop/TUI surfaces, plugin governance, and ClawHunt delivery flow are otherwise unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_models.py tests/test_state.py tests/test_worker_backends.py -q`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/models.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `tests/test_models.py`, `tests/test_state.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`.
- Made Textual a default SuperClaw runtime dependency so `superclaw tui` can launch from a normal install without requiring a separate `superclaw[tui]` extra. What changed: moved `textual` into the core Python dependency set, kept the legacy `tui` extra as a backward-compatible no-op alias, and updated the TUI bootstrap regression to treat import failures as a generic broken-environment error instead of documenting optional-install instructions. Why: the roadmap's last real TUI acceptance gap was that `--dump-snapshot` worked but the actual full-screen `superclaw tui` still failed on a default install because Textual remained optional. Impact: default installs now carry the TUI runtime by default so shell/TUI operators can launch the full-screen cockpit out of the box; headless API, runtime service, desktop beta, plugin governance, ClawHunt flows, and delivery/evidence semantics are otherwise unchanged. - Added a desktop-home shortcut for local dry-run delivery. - Hardened the TUI CLI snapshot plugin-selection regression test against host-local shell defaults. What changed: the `test_cli_tui_dump_snapshot_accepts_plugin_id` case now sets `SUPERCLAW_SHELL_CONFIG_PATH` to a temp file before invoking `superclaw tui --dump-snapshot`, so the assertion no longer depends on whatever backend is persisted on the operator's machine; this preserves the original product behavior while making the TUI acceptance path hermetic. Why: a strict acceptance rerun on the current mainline exposed that the test was silently reading the developer's persisted `backend=codex` preference and failing even though the CLI/TUI code was behaving correctly. - Reshaped the desktop web workbench into a Codex-like app shell. What changed: the app now opens in chat mode, expands the left rail into a labeled sidebar with brand, workspace navigation, recent runs, and account status, replaces the oversized eval-first hero with a `SuperClaw Chat` conversational canvas, moves the composer and slash-command hints to the center of the workflow, and keeps eval controls as a secondary drawer while run/evidence/verdict panels act as right-side context. - Fixed task-derived desktop runs so they always follow the currently selected ClawHunt task even while a newer detail payload is still loading. What changed: task-driven run launchers now derive their task/problem payload from the current selected task id unless the loaded detail matches that id, and the control plane shows a loading card instead of keeping a stale detail panel visible while the replacement payload is in flight. Why: after guarding against late stale responses, there was still a narrower correctness gap where users could click `Use selected task` or trigger a home local dry-run during the detail refresh window and accidentally launch against the previous task because the old detail object was still considered authoritative. Impact: correctness hardening for ClawHunt task-derived runs and task-browser UX only; goal/run APIs, runtime supervision, TUI/plugin governance, acceptance runners, and evidence semantics are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:beta-acceptance`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`.

- Fixed a desktop task-browser race so stale ClawHunt task-detail responses no longer overwrite a newer selection in the workbench. What changed: the web workbench now tags each ClawHunt detail request, ignores late responses from superseded selections, and covers the behavior with a regression test that delays task `101`, switches to task `202`, then proves the older detail payload cannot replace the newer view. Why: desktop home local dry-runs and `Use selected task` both rely on the selected ClawHunt detail card, so a slow earlier response could silently point operators back at the wrong market problem after they had already switched tasks. Impact: correctness hardening for ClawHunt browsing and task-derived runs only; goal creation, local dry-run policy, runtime supervision, TUI/plugin governance, and evidence semantics are unchanged. Verification: `npm --prefix apps/web test`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, `npm --prefix apps/desktop run test:beta-acceptance`, and `git diff --check`. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`.

Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_ui_contracts.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`.

- Added a first-class plugin runtime diagnostics surface to the AgentOS control plane. What changed: extended the shared plugin-status contract with diagnostics endpoints, added protected `/api/plugins/diagnostics` and `/api/plugins/diagnostics/events` routes backed by local plugin evidence analysis, streamed plugin diagnostics over SSE, and surfaced a new `Plugin runtime diagnostics` card in the Plugins workspace with failure/slow-call/sandbox-kill summaries plus downloadable JSON. Why: the roadmap still called out plugin diagnostics as a distinct event/control-plane requirement, but the product only exposed install/governance/configuration surfaces and kept runtime diagnostics behind local CLI analysis. Impact: additive diagnostics visibility only; plugin install/update/uninstall behavior, runtime policy enforcement, entitlement gating, prompt shell, TUI, desktop runtime supervision, and delivery/evidence semantics are unchanged. Verification: covered by API/UI contract tests, frontend test/build, and full consolidation verification. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/ui_contracts.py`, `apps/api/main.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_api.py`.

- Added Eval artifact download ownership enforcement. What changed: `/api/evals/{eval_id}/artifacts/{artifact_id}` now serves report-listed artifacts only when the resolved file path is inside that eval's directory. Why: Eval reports are downloadable delivery evidence, so a polluted `report.json` must not turn the Eval artifact endpoint into an arbitrary local-file read path. Impact: Eval artifact download safety only; valid eval evidence JSON, Markdown/PDF reports, fusion export artifacts, normal eval generation, run artifact downloads, verifier behavior, and Fusion gates are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_eval_artifact_download_rejects_paths_outside_eval_dir -q` returned `200` before the guard; green single test plus existing Eval artifact download regressions after implementation. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/evals.py`, `tests/test_api.py`.

- Added run artifact download ownership enforcement. What changed: `/api/runs/{run_id}/artifacts/{artifact_id}` now serves filesystem artifacts only when the resolved path is inside that run's artifact root, while SuperClaw local fusion artifact URIs continue to use their dedicated loader. Why: EvidenceBundle artifact downloads should not become an arbitrary local-file read path if a polluted artifact ref points outside the run's evidence directory. Impact: artifact download safety only; valid worker logs, transcripts, EvidenceBundle artifacts under `artifact_dir/<run_id>`, fusion artifact references, eval artifacts, verifier behavior, run execution, and protocol export are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_run_artifact_download_rejects_paths_outside_run_artifact_root -q` returned `200` before the guard; green single test and existing `test_api_backends_run_listing_cancel_and_artifact_download` after implementation. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_api.py`.

- Added canonical Fusion artifact id enforcement. What changed: `/api/fusion/artifacts/{artifact_id}` and EvidenceBundle-linked fusion artifact downloads now reject noncanonical alias ids instead of cleaning caller input into another valid artifact id. Why: fusion artifacts are evidence records, so public artifact retrieval must be fail-closed when the requested identifier is not exactly the persisted canonical id. Impact: artifact retrieval safety only; valid generated `fusion_*` ids, action recording, payload digests, EvidenceBundle attachment, permission gates, source fingerprints, native verification, workbench navigation, and plugin projection are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_api_rejects_noncanonical_artifact_id_alias -q` returned `200` before canonical-id enforcement; green single test after implementation. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added secret-safe Fusion action reference identifiers. What changed: Fusion action artifacts now run artifact refs and permission result `artifact_id`/`policy_id` values through the canonical secret scanner before persistence, replacing secret-like public identifiers with safe defaults. Why: earlier sanitization dropped paths, URLs, metadata, and extra secret fields, but allowed identifier fields could still carry tokens into the fusion action JSON and attached EvidenceBundle artifact metadata. Impact: Fusion evidence sanitization only; valid non-secret artifact ids, kinds, permission artifact ids, permission policy ids, payload digests, human-gate decisions, API routes, workbench navigation, plugin projection, and native verification gates are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_action_refs_and_permission_ids_redact_secret_like_values -q` failed before identifier sanitization; green single test after implementation. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added a sanitized invalid-action response for the Fusion action API. What changed: `/api/fusion/actions` now catches invalid fusion component/action identifiers and returns `400` with `FUSION_INVALID_ACTION`, a generic `invalid fusion action` detail, and no fusion artifact write instead of relying on an unstructured framework error that could echo user input. Why: the public fusion action recorder should fail closed with machine-readable diagnostics and no orphan artifacts when a caller sends an unknown imported component. Impact: API error contract only; valid passive actions, active-network human-gate enforcement, EvidenceBundle attachment, artifact loading, plugin projection, workbench navigation, child-app startup, and native verification gates are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_api_rejects_invalid_component_with_sanitized_error_without_orphan_artifact -q` failed before the structured response existed; green single test after implementation. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added recursive active-network intent detection for Fusion Osiris actions. What changed: `record_fusion_action` now inspects nested payload keys, including camelCase names such as `scanType` and `targetPorts`, so scan/probe/fingerprint intent cannot bypass the human gate by hiding active parameters below a passive-looking tool call. Why: the previous fail-closed gate covered known scanner tools, action/tool aliases, and top-level payload keys, but nested action options could still record an Osiris action without the required permission result. Impact: Fusion action-recording policy only; passive DNS/WHOIS-style lookups with ordinary target payloads remain ungated, artifacts still store only payload digests, and no child-app source, plugin manifest, MCP projection, workbench navigation, or native test command behavior is changed. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_nested_scan_payload_keys_fail_closed_even_with_passive_tool_name -q` failed before recursive payload-key detection; green single test and `pytest tests/test_fusion.py -q` after implementation. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added authenticated Plugins catalog opening in the Fusion Shell. What changed: the Plugins tab still records `surface_opened` first, but it now opens the protected `/api/fusion/capabilities` catalog by fetching it with `authHeaders()`, rendering a JSON blob in the popup, and revoking the object URL later instead of navigating a raw browser tab that cannot carry `X-SuperClaw-Token`. Why: the plugin/MCP projection surface is control-token protected, so a normal deep link could show 403 even after a successful evidence record. Impact: frontend workbench Plugins navigation and static UI contracts only; API protection, plugin manifests, MCP projection shape, EvidenceBundle attachment, permission gates, child-app startup, and artifact persistence semantics are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q` failed before the authenticated plugin catalog fetch existed; green `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q`, `npm.cmd test --prefix apps/web`, and `npm.cmd run build --prefix apps/web` cover the protected catalog opening path and frontend build. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_web_static.py`.

- Added record-before-navigation gating for Fusion Shell deep links. What changed: `openFusionSurface` now opens a blank popup synchronously, records `surface_opened` through `/api/fusion/actions`, and only then navigates the popup to the child app; failed or unreachable action recording closes the blank window instead of opening Osiris, open-design, or OpenPencil outside the evidence trail. Why: evidence-recorded deep links still let a child app open before the protected SuperClaw action endpoint accepted the record, which could leave navigation untracked if `SUPERCLAW_CONTROL_TOKEN` or the API failed. Impact: frontend workbench navigation ordering and static UI contracts only; API action recording, EvidenceBundle attachment, permission gates, child-app startup, plugin manifests, iframe previews, control-token validation, and artifact persistence semantics are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q` failed before blank-popup record-first navigation existed; green `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q`, `npm.cmd test --prefix apps/web`, and `npm.cmd run build --prefix apps/web` cover the record-before-navigation surface and frontend build. Files: `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/test_web_static.py`.

- Added evidence-recorded Fusion Shell deep links. What changed: child-product `Open` actions in the Fusion Shell now call `openFusionSurface`, open the fixed local child-app URL, and record a `surface_opened` action through `/api/fusion/actions` with a `fusion-surface-open` artifact ref; if a run is active, the action artifact attaches to the run EvidenceBundle. Why: the first fusion workbench used safe deep links and explicit `Record evidence action`, but simply opening Osiris, open-design, or OpenPencil could escape the evidence trail. Impact: frontend workbench navigation and static UI contracts only; API action recording, permission gates, child-app startup, plugin manifests, iframe previews, control-token validation, and artifact persistence semantics are unchanged. Verification: RED `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q` failed before `openFusionSurface` existed; green `pytest tests/test_web_static.py -q`, `npm.cmd test --prefix apps/web`, and `npm.cmd run build --prefix apps/web` cover the recorded deep-link surface and frontend build. - Added first-class Runs and Evidence tabs to the Fusion Shell. Runs and Evidence render SuperClaw-native run/eval and EvidenceBundle controls, while child-product tabs keep the governed `/api/fusion/actions` recording path. - Added authenticated EvidenceBundle JSON downloads in the workbench. What changed: the Evidence pane download control now fetches `/api/runs/{run_id}/evidence` with `authHeaders()`, refreshes the local bundle, and saves `superclaw-run-{run_id}-evidence.json` instead of only showing a status message. Why: proof-before-payment is incomplete if the visible Evidence download affordance does not produce the signed run evidence JSON under `SUPERCLAW_CONTROL_TOKEN` protection. Impact: frontend evidence UX and static UI contracts only; EvidenceBundle persistence, API evidence endpoints, verifier behavior, run artifacts, Eval downloads, fusion action recording, and control-token validation are unchanged. - Added authenticated Eval artifact downloads in the workbench. What changed: taught the packaged desktop shell to honor `SUPERCLAW_DESKTOP_WORKBENCH_SMOKE_FILE` by starting a local SuperClaw sidecar through the existing CLI bridge, calling the protected local API for direct Codex chat, creating a dry-run goal/run pair, fetching run evidence, stopping the owned runtime, and writing the full workbench transcript to JSON before exit; added a `test:workbench-smoke` script that launches the built `SuperClaw.app` bundle with an isolated temp state path and asserts the packaged shell can complete that direct-chat plus delivery/evidence business loop end-to-end; and documented that proof in the desktop beta quickstart. - Added clearer chat and delivery working-state feedback to the Runtime Shell. What changed: introduced machine-readable turn lifecycle lines for scripted shell sessions, differentiated full-screen working indicators by `chat` vs `delivery`, emitted explicit completion states for successful delivery turns, and surfaced a distinct `waiting_for_human_gate` turn status instead of falling through without a final shell state. Why: Phase 1 of the TUI/Desktop roadmap still required clearer working states for interactive shell usage, and the previous shell output exposed failures but did not provide a consistent start/completion contract across direct chat and delivery turns. Impact: additive shell UX and testability only; intent routing, run orchestration, evidence format, API contracts, plugin verification, and existing slash-command behavior are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_cli.py -k "shell or interactive"` and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`.

- Added guided shell setup and safe persistent history to the Runtime Shell. What changed: added `/setup [BACKEND]` to guide backend, mode, repo, and backend-specific runtime configuration from inside `superclaw shell`, persisted backend/mode/repo defaults into shell config, persisted shell history to a local file next to the shell config, and filtered secret-bearing commands plus password-style setup prompts out of persisted history. Why: Phase 1 of the TUI/Desktop roadmap still required an onboarding path that lets an operator configure the selected backend without reading docs, plus safe history persistence that does not leak agent or API secrets into local shell history. Impact: additive shell UX only; run orchestration semantics, evidence format, API contracts, plugin verification, ClawHunt submission flow, and existing `/mode`, `/backend`, `/config`, and `/agents` behavior are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_cli.py -k "shell or interactive"` and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/runtime_config.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`.

- Added Phase 1 shell inspection commands and grouped help to the Runtime Shell. What changed: added grouped `/help` output, high-signal `/status`, recent-run `/runs`, detailed `/run RUN_ID`, concise `/evidence RUN_ID`, and `/plugins doctor` inspection commands, extended slash-command completion to multi-word shell commands, and added shell regression coverage for the new inspection loop. Why: the Codex-like AgentOS shell needed stronger operator-facing visibility before a full-screen TUI or desktop shell can feel trustworthy, and Phase 1 of the TUI/Desktop roadmap explicitly calls for run/evidence/plugin inspection inside the existing `superclaw` shell. Impact: additive shell UX only; orchestration semantics, evidence schema, plugin verification, API contracts, ClawHunt submission flow, and existing `/mode`, `/backend`, `/config`, and `/agents` behavior are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_cli.py -k "shell or interactive"` and `git diff --check`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`.

- Added plugin install and local configuration management to the Codex-like web shell. What changed: added protected sidecar endpoints for verified registry install, installed-plugin configuration inspection, non-secret setting writes, and secret set/delete flows; extended `/api/plugins/status` with install/config contract metadata and root-key readiness; taught the Plugins workspace to install registry plugins, select cached plugins, inspect required settings/secrets, and manage local plugin configuration without leaking secret values into UI payloads; and added API plus web regression coverage for the new control-plane loop. Why: the AgentOS/plugin-market roadmap requires the desktop shell to do more than inspect registry metadata, and operators need a first-class local flow to install a verified plugin and complete its required configuration from the same Codex-like control surface. Impact: additive plugin control-plane capability only; plugin verification rules, fake-cloud registry semantics, entitlement enforcement, evidence format, task orchestration, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_runtime_status_agents_and_plugins_contract tests/test_api.py::test_api_plugin_install_and_configuration_control_plane`, `npm --prefix apps/web test`, and `npm --prefix apps/web run build`. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_config.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/app-shell.test.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/static-ui.test.mjs`.

- Added a plugin control plane to the Codex-like web shell. What changed: enriched `/api/plugins/status` with registry and governance summary counts plus status links, added a dedicated `Plugins` workspace to the App shell, taught control-plane reads to work when the local sidecar does not require a control token, wired protected reads for cached plugins, local registry metadata, revocations, and runtime policy, added the missing `/v1` Vite proxy for live dev flows, and added render/static tests that cover plugin control rendering and tokenized fetches. Why: the AgentOS roadmap needs an operator-facing plugin surface for marketplace readiness, local cache visibility, and governance inspection instead of hiding plugin state behind CLI-only or fake-cloud-only endpoints, and the local desktop shell must not fail open or go blank depending on token mode or dev proxy gaps. Impact: additive API/UI visibility only; plugin verification, entitlements, revocation enforcement, runtime policy semantics, task orchestration, and ClawHunt delivery behavior are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_runtime_status_agents_and_plugins_contract`, `npm --prefix apps/web test`, `npm --prefix apps/web run build`, and fresh-browser verification against a clean Vite dev server with `/v1` proxy enabled. - Added a settings and agent control plane to the Codex-like web shell. What changed: added protected ClawHunt auth status/login/logout/profile endpoints, enriched `/api/agents` with configuration and model remediation metadata, made protected frontend probes consistently send the control token, added a dedicated settings/agent-control workspace with left-rail account state, config editing, agent doctor cards, and inline Human Gate recovery actions, and introduced a render-level Vitest shell test alongside the existing static smoke check. Why: the desktop shell needed a real operator-facing control surface for authentication, runtime configuration, and local agent readiness instead of requiring CLI-only setup or partially broken token-gated fetches. - Added a web run cockpit for recent-run control and recovery visibility. What changed: the App workbench now loads recent runs from `/api/runs`, hydrates selected run detail and evidence, streams active run events with bounded client-side buffers, exposes `refresh`, `resume`, `reconcile`, `cancel`, and `verify` controls for the selected run, and surfaces task-graph, lease, runtime, and plugin status in the existing Codex-like shell. Why: the desktop roadmap needs a real operator cockpit with visible working-state and recovery actions instead of a prompt-only launcher, especially for interrupted or failed local runs. Impact: additive web-console UX only; backend orchestration semantics, evidence format, plugin runtime policy, ClawHunt delivery flow, and existing CLI/service contracts are unchanged. - Added a runtime config control plane for App and service clients. What changed: introduced a shared `runtime_config` module for shell-config persistence rules, added protected `/api/config` and `/api/config/set` endpoints with a safe allowlist and secret redaction, expanded `/api/runtime/status` with backend/mode/config metadata, persisted non-secret CLI `/config set` values into the shared config store, and wired the web workbench to read runtime defaults and save the current backend as the shell default. Why: the Codex-like desktop roadmap requires a real configuration contract between CLI, service, and UI instead of separate ad-hoc env handling, and the settings surface needs a safe way to inspect and persist runtime defaults without leaking secrets. Impact: additive config/API/UI capability only; backend execution semantics, plugin runtime policy, evidence format, ClawHunt delivery flow, marketplace commerce, and existing auth flows are unchanged. Verification: `.venv/bin/python - <<'PY' ... pytest.main([...]) ... PY` covering new runtime-config API and CLI cases, plus `npm --prefix apps/web test` and `npm --prefix apps/web run build`. - Added a desktop sidecar runtime contract and local service entrypoint. What changed: introduced `superclaw service` to run the local FastAPI sidecar directly from the CLI, added `/api/runtime/status` as a desktop-oriented readiness summary, added `/api/agents` as a normalized local agent inventory, added `/api/plugins/status` as a lightweight cached-plugin status contract for future desktop/web consoles, protected those discovery/status endpoints behind the existing control token gate, and enabled local-dev/Tauri CORS origins for browser-based sidecar access. Why: the Codex-like App plan needs a stable local service surface before a Tauri or Web console can supervise SuperClaw, inspect runtime health, and render agent/plugin readiness without scraping CLI output. Impact: additive CLI/API capability only; no task orchestration semantics, evidence format, plugin execution rules, ClawHunt submission flow, marketplace commerce, or existing shell behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_control_token_protects_execution_surfaces tests/test_api.py::test_api_runtime_status_agents_and_plugins_contract tests/test_api.py::test_api_sidecar_cors_allows_local_web_origins tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_service_runs_uvicorn_with_runtime_state_path tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_service_reports_missing_uvicorn_dependency` covers the new service startup entrypoint plus runtime/agent/plugin status contracts, auth protection, CORS preflight, and friendly missing-dependency handling. Files: `apps/api/main.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`.

- Added profile-scoped native gate semantics for `fusion test`. What changed: `superclaw fusion test --profile <name>` now computes `summary.native_ok` from the selected profile's required steps instead of inheriting the full native report's global `ok`, while still returning only selected native results. Why: staged fusion verification should allow an operator to gate `pencil`, `design`, or `osiris` independently without unrelated component failures making the selected profile look broken. Impact: profile-scoped summary semantics only; full `all` profile gating, native report loading, child app startup, third-party command execution, plugin manifests, and permission gates are unchanged. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_test_profile_gate_ignores_unselected_native_failures` covers the previous profile pollution path, and manual `fusion test --profile all/pencil --json` checks cover current gates. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added Docker Compose execution for `fusion start --execute`. What changed: the command still emits the launch plan, and explicit `--execute` now runs `docker compose --profile <all|osiris|design|pencil> up --build -d` from the fusion root with structured `started`, `failed`, `timeout`, `docker_unavailable`, and `compose_file_missing` diagnostics. Why: the public fusion start shortcut should actually launch the selected isolated product profile while still failing closed and reporting operator-useful errors when Docker or the compose file is unavailable. Impact: additive real runner semantics for the execute flag only; safe start-plan output, Docker Compose profiles, fusion status/test/audit behavior, plugin manifests, permission gates, output redaction, and third-party source trees are unchanged. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_start_execute_runs_docker_compose_profile tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_start_execute_fails_closed_when_docker_compose_fails tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_start_execute_fails_closed_when_compose_file_is_missing tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_changelog_describes_execute_runner_not_legacy_placeholder` covers start execution, fail-closed errors, redaction, missing compose files, and release-note parity. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Added fail-closed exit semantics for `fusion test`. What changed: `superclaw fusion test` now computes `summary.ok` and exits non-zero after emitting JSON/text diagnostics when the native report is missing, failed, blocked, incomplete, or has unreported required steps. Why: the fusion verification surface needs to behave like a real CI/release gate, not just a passive summary that can succeed while required upstream checks are absent. Impact: stricter CLI gate semantics only; no native report collection, upstream command execution, fusion action artifacts, plugin manifests, or child app startup behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_test_cli_fails_closed_when_native_report_is_missing` covers the previous false-success path, and `python -m superclaw.cli fusion test --profile all --json` covers the current passing gate. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added a reported SuperClaw self-check to `fusion test`. What changed: `superclaw fusion test --profile all --json` now runs the in-process fusion capability audit as the SuperClaw step and includes that pass/fail result in the step summary, so a complete native third-party report no longer leaves the aggregate plan with `not_reported_steps=1`. Why: the fusion end-to-end verification surface should make its own SuperClaw projection/audit gate explicit instead of looking partially unreported even when upstream native checks are present. Impact: safer verification summary semantics only; no upstream native report loading, child app startup, plugin manifest projection, action artifact recording, permission gates, or third-party command execution behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_test_reports_superclaw_self_check_when_all_native_steps_exist` covers the RED/GREEN behavior, and `pytest tests/test_fusion.py` covers the full fusion contract. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`.

- Added an operator-safe fusion status CLI. What changed: `superclaw fusion status` now mirrors the safe `/api/fusion/status` shape, supports `--json`, summarizes imported component readiness, capability counts, and the active-probe gate, and omits local source paths/source directories unless the operator explicitly uses `fusion doctor`. Why: fusion orchestration needed a stable command-line status surface for end-to-end checks without exposing `third_party` source locations to machine-readable status consumers. Impact: additive CLI/status output and safer default fusion status serialization only; no child app startup, native verification, plugin manifest, artifact recording, permission gate, API action, or upstream source behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_status_cli_uses_operator_safe_api_shape tests/test_fusion.py::test_fusion_api_status_action_and_artifact_flow` covers CLI JSON/text status and API status non-leakage. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/cli.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/fusion.py`, `tests/test_fusion.py`, `README.md`.

- Added a Codex-like SuperClaw desktop AgentOS app plan. - Added shell intent routing and Codex direct chat mode. What changed: the interactive Runtime Shell now supports a persistent `mode` setting with `auto`, `chat`, and `delivery`, adds `/mode auto|chat|delivery`, `/ask MESSAGE`, and `/deliver TASK`, routes ordinary questions such as greetings to a read-only Codex direct-chat path, and keeps explicit implementation/delivery requests on the existing SuperClaw evidence and verification chain. Why: the shell should behave like a usable AgentOS CLI instead of forcing every natural-language input into the delivery pipeline, while preserving the original delivery harness as one route. Impact: additive shell UX and routing behavior only; no evidence format, orchestrator topology, ClawHunt submission, plugin runtime, marketplace, payment, or server API behavior is changed. - Added a persistent interactive SuperClaw shell. What changed: bare `superclaw` now opens the Runtime Shell by default, while `superclaw shell` remains an explicit alias; the shell provides a Hermes-style continuous CLI loop on top of the existing chat/session/run pipeline, starts with a designed lobster-claw branded dashboard, includes Context/Authentication/Slash Commands sections, renders a visible `lobster-claw>` input frame in real terminals, uses `prompt-toolkit` for wide-character-safe line editing and slash-command completion menus, reports selected local-agent readiness with explicit configure hints, adds `/agents` for all backend readiness, and supports slash commands for `/help`, `/exit`, `/session`, `/last`, `/backend NAME`, `/repo PATH`, `/config`, `/config set NAME VALUE`, `/agents`, `/login CLAWHUNT_AGENT_KEY`, `/logout`, `/me`, and `/plugins` while preserving `superclaw --help`, subcommands, and the scriptable `superclaw chat --message/--continue` entrypoint. Why: operators need a human-facing persistent AgentOS-style CLI with configuration, login, conversation, command discovery, local-agent readiness, safe editing for Chinese/wide characters, and a recognizable product identity in one place without removing automation-friendly command mode. Impact: additive CLI UX plus `SUPERCLAW_CLAUDE_EXECUTABLE` support for the Claude CLI backend; login is current-shell scoped and does not persist or print the ClawHunt key; full color/banner rendering and prompt-toolkit TUI behavior are TTY-scoped so scripts and CI stay plain; no evidence format, ClawHunt submission behavior, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or server API behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_shell_runs_multiple_turns_in_one_session tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_shell_exposes_config_login_and_status_commands tests/test_cli.py::test_shell_full_dashboard_contains_claw_brand_and_input_prompt tests/test_cli.py::test_shell_slash_command_completer_lists_matching_commands tests/test_cli.py::test_shell_toolbar_redacts_agent_key tests/test_cli.py::test_shell_agent_status_lines_include_config_hint tests/test_worker_backends.py::test_claude_backend_uses_env_configured_executable tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_main_without_args_starts_shell` covers two shell turns in one persisted chat session, runtime status commands, current-shell auth, safe config output, plugin list, secret non-disclosure, lobster-claw dashboard rendering, the visible input prompt, slash-command completion candidates, toolbar secret redaction, local-agent readiness hints, Claude executable configuration, and the bare-entrypoint shell default. - Added sanitized child execution export to delivery protocol manifests. What changed: the ClawHunt delivery protocol adapter now exports `child_evidence.executions` from persisted `EvidenceBundle.child_executions`, including child task/run identity, parent task identity, backend, status, depth, cancellation mode, timeout, evidence owner, chain verdict, and evidence artifact id while excluding local `evidence_path` values. Why: Phase 3 requires child execution to be visible in persisted state and evidence, and delivery packages need a platform-consumable child execution summary instead of only a count. Impact: protocol adapter export and roadmap documentation only; no child scheduling, fan-out aggregation policy, DAG dependency behavior, cancellation propagation, verifier rules, ClawHunt submission transport, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or production artifact upload is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_protocol_adapter.py::test_delivery_protocol_manifest_exports_sanitized_child_execution_summaries` covers child summary export and local path exclusion. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/protocol_adapter.py`, `tests/test_protocol_adapter.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added public stale-worker PID reconcile entrypoint coverage. What changed: API and CLI regression tests now prove same-host dead worker-pid leases can be reclaimed through `/api/runs/{run_id}/reconcile` and `superclaw reconcile`, not only by calling the orchestrator directly. Why: Phase 2 requires stale-run reconciliation to be a first-class command or public path; internal liveness tests alone did not prove the operator-facing entrypoints exercised the same dead-pid recovery contract. Impact: public-entrypoint test coverage and roadmap documentation only; no heartbeat daemon, background reconciler, cross-host process verification, pid-start-time validation, cloud worker tracking, cancellation semantics, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_api.py::test_api_reconcile_reclaims_dead_worker_pid_lease_before_ttl tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_reconcile_reclaims_dead_worker_pid_lease_before_ttl` covers API and CLI dead-pid stale lease reclamation, persisted audit events, and lease clearing before TTL. Files: `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added local interrupt-resume integration coverage. What changed: a new orchestrator regression starts a real local execution path, completes the first task, simulates a process interrupt after the next task is persisted as `running`, recreates both `StateStore` and `SuperClawOrchestrator`, then reconciles and resumes the run without rerunning the completed task. Why: Phase 2 explicitly requires an interrupt-and-resume local run integration test; prior coverage proved resume from constructed stale state but not a storage-backed interruption after real local worker execution. Impact: local runtime recovery test coverage and roadmap documentation only; no OS-level crash injection, background reconciliation, heartbeat workers, external process detection, orphan process cleanup, cloud worker recovery, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_interrupt_resume_local_run_without_rerunning_completed_task` covers fresh-store resume, preserved pre-interrupt evidence, and remaining task execution. Files: `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added local forced-kill evidence regression coverage. What changed: POSIX local backend cancellation now has a regression test for workers that ignore normal termination and require kill escalation, proving `WorkerResult`, transcript, and worker-log artifacts persist `cancelled=true`, `forced_kill=true`, and exit code `130`. Why: Phase 2 cancellation semantics require transcript evidence to distinguish normal cancellation from forced kill, and the backend unit contract previously only covered a cooperative cancellation path. Impact: POSIX local backend cancellation evidence coverage and roadmap documentation only; no Windows termination semantics, background cancellation scheduler, cross-host process supervision, pid-start-time validation, process-tree termination, cloud worker cancellation, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or production kill orchestration is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py::test_local_shell_backend_records_forced_kill_when_cancelled_process_ignores_terminate` covers forced-kill evidence persistence for a SIGTERM-ignoring worker. Files: `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added local backend evidence size-limit hardening. What changed: local worker-log and transcript artifacts now retain complete redacted stdout/stderr, while primary command output and worker attempt previews remain capped at 4,000 characters in the serialized evidence bundle. Why: Phase 2 requires large stdout, stderr, and transcripts to live behind artifact references instead of bloating or truncating the primary evidence contract. Impact: local backend artifact retention and evidence-size regression coverage only; no artifact size quotas, compression, retention policy, object storage upload, remote backend transcript parity, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production log streaming is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_worker_backends.py::test_local_shell_backend_moves_large_output_to_artifacts_and_caps_primary_evidence` covers full artifact retention for large stdout/stderr plus primary evidence caps. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/backends.py`, `tests/test_worker_backends.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added verification restart-resume regression coverage. What changed: runs persisted in `verifying` with a completed task graph are now covered by a restart test that recreates `StateStore` and `SuperClawOrchestrator`, resumes verification without rerunning completed workers, preserves pre-restart worker evidence and verifier findings, and emits `verification.completed` plus terminal run events. Why: Phase 2 requires verifier findings to remain durable if the process exits between execution and final verdict; the existing ordering test covered same-process persistence but not restart-resume from the persisted verification stage. Impact: verification restart auditability coverage and roadmap documentation only; no resumable verifier jobs, process-level crash injection, verification checkpointing inside verifier rules, background reconciliation, external worker liveness, cancellation semantics, verifier rule expansion, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_resume_verifying_run_after_restart_without_rerunning_workers` covers restart-resume from `verifying` without worker reruns or finding loss. Files: `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added cancellation restart-recovery regression coverage. What changed: stale active runs with persisted cancellation events are now covered by a restart test that creates a fresh `StateStore` and `SuperClawOrchestrator`, reconciles the run to terminal `cancelled`, preserves prior cancellation evidence, and rejects resumed execution after finalization. Why: Phase 2 requires cancellation records to survive restart; existing cancellation tests covered same-process stale reconciliation and active subprocess cancellation but did not pin the new-process recovery path. Impact: cancellation restart auditability coverage and roadmap documentation only; no background cancellation scheduling, cross-process signal delivery after restart, external worker supervision, pid-start-time validation, heartbeat liveness, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_reconcile_preserves_cancelled_run_after_restart` covers persisted cancellation recovery after restart. Files: `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added same-host worker-pid stale lease reclamation. What changed: acquired run-mutation leases now persist the local worker pid and host, and the orchestrator can reclaim a fresh same-host lease before the normal TTL when the recorded worker pid is provably gone from the local process table; reclaimed leases record `run.lease.stale` with pid, host, and stale reason. Why: Phase 2 defines stale runs as active workers that are no longer alive or whose execution lease cannot be proven; relying only on TTL delays deterministic reconciliation for crashed local workers. Impact: local same-host liveness proof and audit metadata only; no heartbeat daemon, cross-host process verification, pid-start-time validation, background reconciliation, cloud worker tracking, cancellation semantics, resume scheduling, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_reconcile_reclaims_dead_worker_pid_lease_before_ttl` covers dead-pid lease reclamation before TTL, and `pytest tests/test_state.py::test_state_store_acquires_and_releases_run_mutation_lease_atomically` covers persisted pid/host lease metadata. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/models.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/state.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_state.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added resume evidence-preservation regression coverage. What changed: eligible stale local runs now have a dedicated regression test proving `resume_run()` preserves prior worker results, worker-log artifacts, and command evidence after `reconcile_run()` continues the remaining tasks and writes the final `evidence-json` artifact. Why: Phase 2 requires interrupted runs to resume without losing prior evidence; without an explicit test, the current `get_evidence()`-before-continue behavior could regress into blank-bundle recreation or artifact loss without detection. Impact: resume auditability coverage and roadmap documentation only; no process-level interrupt injection, worker pid or heartbeat liveness, external process detection, background reconciliation, cancellation semantics, resumable verification jobs, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_resume_preserves_prior_worker_results_and_artifacts` covers stored evidence and final `evidence-json` preservation. Files: `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added verifier-finding persistence ordering. What changed: after `run.verifying`, adversarial verifier findings are now applied to the `EvidenceBundle` and saved before `verification.finding` and `verification.completed` events are emitted, so the completed verification verdict is derived from stored evidence that already includes verifier findings. Why: Phase 2 requires verifier findings to be persisted before the final verdict; otherwise a process exit between verification events and the later evidence save can leave an auditable run without the findings that drove its verdict. Impact: verification evidence persistence ordering only; no process-level crash recovery, resumable verification jobs, external worker liveness, background reconciliation, cancellation semantics, verifier rule expansion, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py::test_orchestrator_persists_verifier_findings_before_verification_completed` covers the stored evidence snapshot at `verification.completed`, and `pytest tests/test_models.py tests/test_state.py tests/test_orchestrator.py tests/test_cli.py` covers the related runtime regression set. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added fresh-lease reconciliation guard. What changed: public `reconcile_run()` now respects fresh persisted `RunMutationLease` records instead of clearing them before acquisition, classifies those runs as `active_writer`, leaves the run status and lease intact, and `resume_run()` inherits the same guard when active runs are reconciled before resume. Why: Phase 2 single-writer semantics require reconcile/resume to prove a lease is stale before taking mutation authority; otherwise a live writer can be incorrectly robbed of its persisted run lease. Impact: reconcile/resume lease safety only; no worker pid or heartbeat liveness, external process detection, background reconciliation, resume scheduling, cancellation semantics, verification persistence, protocol export, plugin runtime behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py tests/test_cli.py -k "fresh_persisted_lease or fresh_persisted_run_mutation_lease"` covers public reconcile, resume, and CLI reporting for fresh persisted writer leases. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added worker-result evidence idempotency. What changed: `EvidenceBundle.add_worker_result` and normalization now collapse repeated writes for the same logical worker attempt by replay identity while preserving distinct attempts or distinct outcomes, and worker result output remains capped before persistence. Why: Phase 2 requires repeated evidence save paths to keep the externally visible bundle stable and reviewable instead of growing duplicate worker rows during replay or retry bookkeeping. Impact: evidence normalization only; no scheduler retry policy, task attempt allocation, backend execution, cancellation, verification verdicts, protocol export, ClawHunt submission, plugin proxy behavior, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_models.py tests/test_state.py` covers duplicate worker result collapse, replacement of repeated attempts, preservation of distinct attempts/outcomes, and primary evidence text caps. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/models.py`, `tests/test_models.py`, `tests/test_state.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added evidence-backed resume eligibility. What changed: stale-run reconciliation now requires the persisted `EvidenceBundle` to exist and decode successfully before a `queued`, `running`, or `verifying` run can be classified as resumable, and missing or unreadable evidence now fails closed with a persisted `failed` run plus `run.failed` event instead of creating a blank evidence bundle. Why: the Phase 1 resume contract requires evidence to be recoverable before retrying work, otherwise SuperClaw could resume from task graph state while silently losing the audit package. Impact: stale-run eligibility only; no scheduler policy, task frontier algorithm, cancellation behavior, DAG execution, backend runtime, plugin proxy, ClawHunt submission, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production recovery tooling is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_orchestrator.py -k "reconcile or resume"` covers normal resumable runs, duplicate writer handling, missing evidence fail-closed behavior, unreadable evidence fail-closed behavior, and existing frontier failure behavior. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator.py`, `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.
- Added storage-level run mutation lease guards. What changed: `StateStore` now exposes acquire, require, and release methods for the single active `RunMutationLease` on a run, records `run.lease.acquired` and `run.lease.released` success events, persists `run.lease.lost` or `run.lease.release_rejected` before fail-closed mismatch errors, and `SuperClawOrchestrator` now uses the same StateStore lease contract instead of a separate hand-written persisted mutation path. Why: Phase 1 requires a deterministic single-writer guard that execute, resume, and reconcile flows can call before mutating persisted run state. Impact: local state-store and orchestrator contract consolidation only; no scheduler policy, stale-run classification, cancellation flow, DAG execution, backend runtime, plugin proxy, ClawHunt submission, cloud sync, marketplace, payment, or production lease TTL behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_state.py tests/test_orchestrator.py` covers acquisition, release, duplicate acquisition rejection, stale lease-loss event persistence, wrong-owner release rejection, stale lease reclamation, duplicate writer rejection, and execution-time lease-loss fail-closed behavior.
