- New `anthropic-agent` worker backend (`AnthropicAgentBackend`) — a self-contained tool-using agent loop that drives **Claude (Opus 4.8 by default) via the Anthropic Messages API** and executes the model's `tool_use` blocks for real (`run_shell`, `write_file`, `read_file`, `list_files`) inside the sandboxed repo checkout, iterating until the model stops requesting tools (`end_turn`) or the iteration/time budget is exhausted. Unlike the single-shot `anthropic` backend (which only records text), this one truly executes; unlike the `claude` CLI backend, it needs **no Node/agent-CLI install** (stdlib `urllib` only) — making it the recommended way to run **real execution at Opus 4.8 in headless containers / Cloud Run**. Shares the path-sandboxed real-execution tool layer (`_RealToolExecution`) with the `gemini` backend, and a text-only turn with no real action is scored *not delivered* (exit 1). Registered in `default_backends()` as `anthropic-agent`; `doctor` reports `anthropic_agent_model`. Configurable via `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_AGENT_MODEL` (falls back to `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_MODEL` then `claude-opus-4-8`), `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_AGENT_MAX_ITERATIONS`, and `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_AGENT_MAX_TOKENS`. Execution stays operator/orchestrator-gated — never the public web chat.
- New `gemini` worker backend (`GeminiAgentBackend`) — a self-contained, dependency-light tool-using agent loop that drives **Google Gemini** through its OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions endpoint and executes the model's `tool_calls` for real (`run_shell`, `write_file`, `read_file`, `list_files`) inside the sandboxed repo checkout, iterating until the model calls `finish` or exhausts its iteration/time budget. Unlike `anthropic` (single completion) and the CLI backends (which shell out to external agent CLIs needing their own creds), this backend owns the loop and needs **no Node/agent-CLI install** — only a Gemini API key — making intelligent real execution possible in headless containers / Cloud Run. File-system tools are path-sandboxed to the repo checkout; the model HTTP call uses stdlib `urllib` only. Registered in `default_backends()` and selectable via `--backend gemini` (and in `validate`'s default backend set); `doctor` reports `gemini_model`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` presence, and `backend.gemini` availability. Configurable via `SUPERCLAW_GEMINI_API_KEY` (or `GEMINI_API_KEY`), `SUPERCLAW_GEMINI_MODEL` (default `gemini-2.5-flash`), `SUPERCLAW_GEMINI_BASE_URL`, `SUPERCLAW_GEMINI_MAX_ITERATIONS`, and `SUPERCLAW_GEMINI_MAX_TOKENS`. A text-only model turn with no real tool action is scored as *not delivered* (exit 1) to guard against the "brain, not hands" failure mode. Time/cancel bounds and `.log`/`.json` artifacts come from the shared `_synthetic_result` path, so the `WorkerResult`/evidence/verdict pipeline is unchanged. Execution remains operator/orchestrator-gated — never invoked from the public web chat (RCE boundary).
- New `bobo` worker backend (`BoboCliBackend`) that drives the local **Bobo** CLI's autonomous `run` loop (`bobo --print --full-auto run --effort … --max-iterations … "<task>"`), giving SuperClaw real, hands-on execution alongside the `claude` (Claude Code) and `hermes` backends. Registered in `default_backends()` and selectable via `--backend bobo` (and included in `validate`'s default backend set); `doctor` reports `bobo_model` and `backend.bobo` availability. Tunable via `SUPERCLAW_BOBO_EXECUTABLE`, `SUPERCLAW_BOBO_MODEL`, `SUPERCLAW_BOBO_EFFORT`, and `SUPERCLAW_BOBO_MAX_ITERATIONS`; a bypass `permission_policy` escalates to `--yolo`. Time/cancel bounds and `.log`/`.json` artifacts come from the shared `run_command` path, so the `WorkerResult`/evidence/verdict pipeline is unchanged. CLI execution remains operator-only — never invoked from the public web chat (RCE boundary).

### Security

- Hardened the adversarial verifier's `secret_redaction` rule to detect a much broader set of real-world secret formats — AWS access key ids (long-term and temporary), AWS secret-key assignments, OpenAI/Anthropic `sk-`/`sk-ant-`/`sk-proj-` keys, Google `AIza` keys, Slack `xox*` tokens, JWTs, GitHub fine-grained PATs, and generic `password`/`secret`/`api_key`/`token`/`client_secret` assignments — instead of only ClawHunt, classic GitHub, Bearer, and PEM patterns.
- Hardened the `backend_readiness_classification` rule with a broader, agent-backend-focused set of auth/quota/raw-mode failure markers (e.g. `please log in`, `authentication failed`, `claude login`, `session expired`, `quota exceeded`, `insufficient_quota`, `rate limit exceeded`, `too many requests`, `credit balance is too low`, `stdin is not a tty`), so fake "ready" backends are classified as readiness failures across more phrasings.
- Both rules remain fail-closed and detection-only by design (the verifier gates the run rather than silently redacting), and the broadened patterns were regression-checked against the full suite to avoid false positives on benign evidence.
- Unified secret scanning into a single canonical source of truth (`superclaw.secrets_scan`) shared by both `runtime.redact_secrets` (scrub-before-persistence) and the adversarial `secret_redaction` detector, closing a drift gap where newly detected secret formats were still being written to transcripts unredacted.
- Broadened `backend_readiness_classification` to also scan success-status (`<400`) probe bodies and the submission response, so an auth/quota/raw-mode prompt echoed inside a 2xx probe or a submission payload is caught; negative-path (4xx/5xx) probe bodies are deliberately excluded so legitimate protected-endpoint evidence is not misclassified.
- Added a new fail-closed `artifact_integrity` adversarial rule that rejects malformed artifact evidence (missing kind/path, invalid sensitivity, duplicate artifact ids) and dangling worker artifact/transcript references that do not resolve to a declared artifact.
- Added a fail-closed `plugin_policy_boundary` adversarial rule. What changed: the verifier now rejects model-visible direct plugin directory exposure, entitlement/license token material, revoked plugin invocations recorded as successful, and secret-bearing raw plugin output fields. Why: Section 24 requires adversarial verification to prove plugins stay behind the SuperClaw proxy and fail closed when package policy is bypassed. Impact: detection-only verifier hardening; no proxy execution, backend command construction, cloud sync, marketplace, or payment behavior changes. Verification: `pytest tests/test_adversarial.py tests/test_adversarial_hardening.py` covers clean proxy evidence plus policy-bypass, revoked-success, revoked-denial, and secret-output cases. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/adversarial.py`, `tests/test_adversarial.py`, `tests/test_adversarial_hardening.py`, `docs/plugin-developer-guide.md`, `docs/plugin-ecosystem-framework.md`.
- Hardened API request validation with Pydantic field bounds (`concurrency` 1..64, `budget_seconds`/`timeout_seconds` 1..86400, payment `amount` >= 0) and non-empty constraints on required ids/paths/messages, rejecting resource-abuse and empty-string inputs with HTTP 422.

### Fixed

- Fixed dev-signed plugin archive execution after verify/cache. What changed: `plugin pack` now signs a temporary staged package instead of mutating the developer source manifest, and `.scplug` extraction restores archived Unix permission bits so cached MCP sidecar entrypoints remain executable. Why: a real CLI plugin lifecycle smoke found that packed hello-world plugins verified successfully but failed at invocation with `Permission denied` after cache extraction. Impact: plugin developer source trees stay clean after packing, and verified `.scplug` packages can be invoked through the SuperClaw plugin proxy; no protocol, cloud, marketplace, entitlement, or payment behavior is changed. Verification: `pytest tests/test_plugin_devkit.py tests/test_plugin_local_verification.py tests/test_plugin_proxy.py`, the full plugin regression suite, and a real CLI E2E covering `plugin pack`, `plugin verify`, `plugin list`, `plugin call`, and `plugin evidence-list`. Files: `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugin_devkit.py`, `packages/superclaw/src/superclaw/plugins.py`, `tests/test_plugin_devkit.py`.
- Hermes workers now receive the target repository root as an explicit absolute-path contract and are told that the final `superclaw_worker_result` marker is evidence formatting only, preventing Hermes from completing work in its own configured project directory while SuperClaw records a false successful delivery.
- Harness TOML export escaping is now Python 3.11-compatible; multiline and quoted string values are escaped before f-string interpolation instead of using backslashes inside f-string expressions.
- Made the pytest warning filter compatible with Starlette versions that no longer expose `starlette.exceptions.StarletteDeprecationWarning`, so local test runs do not fail during warning-filter parsing.
- Eliminated the transitional `StarletteDeprecationWarning` (httpx/starlette `TestClient`) that surfaced on every CLI command and in the test summary, via a scoped warning filter at the eval-harness import boundary plus a pytest `filterwarnings` entry.
- Run-mutation leases are now reclaimed after a TTL (`RUN_MUTATION_LEASE_TTL_SECONDS`) when no live in-process writer holds the runtime lock, so a crashed or hung writer can no longer block execute/resume/reconcile indefinitely; a live long-running writer is never robbed of its lease.
- Domain `ValueError`s raised by API routes now surface as a unified HTTP 400 `{detail}` contract instead of leaking as unhandled 500s (endpoints needing a specific status, e.g. resume → 409, still catch locally).
- Harness CLI `adapt-agent`/`adapt-skill` now report a clean `error:` message with exit code 1 for a missing source file or unknown `--target` instead of an unhandled traceback.
- De-flaked the real-subprocess cancellation timing tests by widening their wait/join windows (5s → 30s), eliminating false failures under heavy machine load while preserving identical coverage.

### Tested

- Added a parallel-frontier cancellation stress test (cancel reaches every concurrent worker) and an end-to-end SSE test asserting fan-out progress (`child_run.spawned`, `child_fanout.completed`) streams over `GET /api/runs/{id}/events`.

### Added

- Added an `openclaw` worker backend that can run an OpenClaw-compatible CLI behind the same SuperClaw orchestration/evidence contract. It auto-detects `openclaw`, `open-claw`, `claw`, or `bobo` on `PATH`, supports `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_EXECUTABLE`, `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_ARGS` (default `--print`), and `SUPERCLAW_OPENCLAW_MODEL`, and participates in `doctor`, `validate`, and `default_backends()`.
- Added a local `hermes` worker backend that routes SuperClaw tasks through the Hermes CLI (`hermes --oneshot ...`) with a short non-interactive worker contract, records normal worker evidence/artifacts, supports `SUPERCLAW_HERMES_EXECUTABLE` plus model/provider/toolset/skill overrides, and participates in `default_backends()` / `/api/backends` / web backend selection like `codex` and `claude`.
- **Claude Opus 4.8 worker model** is now the default. The `claude` worker backend (Claude Code CLI) defaults to model `claude-opus-4-8` (override via `SUPERCLAW_CLAUDE_MODEL`), and a new direct **`anthropic` backend** calls `claude-opus-4-8` through the Anthropic Messages API (`anthropic` SDK + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, override via `SUPERCLAW_ANTHROPIC_MODEL`; `available()` gates on the key, with an injectable `message_fn` for deterministic tests). The default worker backend across `run_goal`/CLI `run`/`chat`/API is now `claude` (was `local`).
- Worker backends now issue a **real goal/task-solving prompt** (goal + task + acceptance criteria, instructing real repository changes) instead of the prior one-line smoke prompt, while still emitting the parseable `superclaw_worker_result … status=completed` marker. `doctor` reports the configured Claude/Anthropic models and key/backend availability.
- Opus 4.8 is now the default across **every ClawHunt-facing entry point**, not just the library/API default: the ClawHunt webhook auto-run (`SUPERCLAW_WEBHOOK_BACKEND`) defaults to `claude` (was `local`), and the web workbench defaults its backend selection to the Claude Opus 4.8 backend (falling back to first-available only if `claude` is unavailable) instead of always picking first-available `local`.
- Parallel subagent fan-out: `SuperClawOrchestrator.spawn_child_runs(...)` spawns multiple child subagents for one parent task, runs them concurrently under a bounded worker pool, and aggregates their outcomes into one parent-visible verdict via a `ChildAggregationPolicy` (`all_succeed` / `any_succeeds` / `best_effort` / `consensus` / `quorum`). A failed fan-out records a `subagent_fanout` finding that fails the parent's chain verdict, and a `child_fanout.completed` event captures the aggregate.
- Fan-out aggregation now also supports `consensus` (strict majority) and `quorum` (configurable threshold) policies, and synthesizes every child's outcome into the parent's unified report at `backend_summary['subagent_fanouts']`.
- Cross-subagent synthesis: fan-out now detects verdict conflict (mixed FAIL/non-FAIL children), records a verdict distribution, and folds a deduped union of child findings into the parent report; `ChildFanoutResult.conflict` exposes it.
- In-execution self-expansion: a worker can return `WorkerResult.discovered_tasks` and the execute loop merges them into the live task graph mid-run (validated in place; fail-soft via a `task_expansion` finding on invalid expansion), so discovered subtasks join the frontier without restarting the run.
- Intra-run frontier parallelism: independent ready tasks in a single run now execute concurrently under a bounded worker pool when `concurrency > 1` (the slow `backend.run` calls run in parallel while all state mutations stay serial). `concurrency = 1` keeps the exact sequential path, so it is fully opt-in and backward compatible. Covered by concurrency stress tests (peak-concurrency assertion + no-lost-updates under load).
- Topology-driven fan-out: `TaskTopology.EXPLORE_FANOUT` and `TaskTopology.REVIEW_CONSENSUS` (plus a `TaskNode.fanout` spec) make a task-graph node auto-expand into a parallel subagent group at execution time — no manual `spawn_child_runs` call — gated on its aggregation policy and failing closed otherwise.
- External fan-out controls: `superclaw fanout <run_id> --branches N --description ...` CLI command and `POST /api/runs/{run_id}/fanout` endpoint drive `spawn_child_runs` for outside callers.
- Dynamic task-graph expansion: `expand_task_graph(run_id, new_tasks)` (and `POST /api/runs/{run_id}/expand`) adds discovered subtasks to a live run's graph after validating the merged graph (no cycles/dangling/duplicate ids), so newly discovered work joins the frontier on the next execution or resume.
- Short-term engineering roadmap for protocol skeleton, runtime robustness, and later protocol expansion in `docs/short-term-roadmap.md`.

### Changed

- The per-task worker lock is now run-scoped (`worker:{run_id}:{backend}:{role}`) instead of global, so independent runs (including parallel fan-out children) no longer collide on the same backend/role while within-run serialization is preserved.
- Concurrent child→parent synchronization is serialized by a dedicated guard so parallel fan-out children cannot lose-update the shared parent run/evidence row.
- Subagent nesting depth is now configurable via `SuperClawOrchestrator(max_subagent_depth=N)` (default 1), enabling controlled depth>1 subagent trees while still failing closed beyond the configured limit; the redundant `parent_run_id` guard was replaced by a pure depth-based gate.
- Locked in `TaskGraph` dependency-cycle, self-dependency, and dangling-dependency validation (including on `from_dict` load) with explicit regression tests.
- Adversarial verifier findings now carry explicit machine-readable rule contract fields: `input_fields`, `fail_mode`, `severity`, and `remediation`.
- Eval command execution now bootstraps the active interpreter directory into `PATH` and the checkout source root into `PYTHONPATH`, so wrapped local agent helpers can run deterministically inside isolated eval workspaces.
- Reconciler now finalizes stale runs as `cancelled` when persisted cancel events or worker-cancelled evidence already prove a cancellation outcome, instead of collapsing every non-resumable stale run into `failed`.
- Added a first-class task-attempt contract so each worker execution records attempt index, wall-clock timestamps, and distinct attempt-scoped artifacts.
- Added a persisted run-mutation lease guard so execute, reconcile, and resume paths enforce single-active-writer semantics per run.
- Added a latest-delivery-protocol export adapter that can derive a ClawHunt `agent_package_manifest` and latest submission payload shape from stored run evidence without mutating the core bundle.
- Added a constrained DAG task-topology option with a shipped fanout/fan-in dependency pattern, deterministic frontier execution, and resume-safe dependency persistence.
- Added a one-level subagent lifecycle contract with persisted parent-child run linkage, child-evidence aggregation, and cancellation propagation.
- Hardened run-state handling with explicit transition rules, persisted verifying stage, and terminal failed/cancelled event coverage.
- Added stale-run reconciliation and deterministic resume flows backed by persisted execution context and task-graph progress.
- Normalized evidence persistence so duplicate probes/commands/artifacts/findings collapse deterministically and primary evidence text is capped.
- Routed run cancellation through the orchestrator so active cancel, ignored terminal cancel, and forced-kill evidence follow one persisted contract.
- Moved ClawHunt submission payload shaping into a named protocol adapter so API and CLI submit flows no longer hand-build v1 payloads inline.
- Expanded adversarial verification to classify backend auth/quota blockers and to validate submission-response/artifact consistency.

### What changed

- Added a rule registry for the adversarial verifier and mapped each existing profile finding to an explicit contract with traced input fields and remediation guidance.
- Extended `VerificationFinding` serialization so rule contract metadata round-trips through persisted evidence bundles.
- Added `_eval_command_env()` so eval subprocesses inherit a deterministic Python bootstrap instead of depending on an ambient `python` alias or current working directory import path.
- Added an eval regression test for wrapper scripts that invoke `python`, and used the existing delivery-gap tests to verify fake Claude/Codex lanes recover from the bootstrap gap.
- Added stale-cancelled reconciliation so `reconcile_run()` can derive a terminal `cancelled` status from persisted cancellation events or worker evidence.
- Extended orchestrator, CLI, and API tests to cover stale runs that must reconcile to `cancelled` rather than `queued` or `failed`.
- Added persisted `RunSession.task_attempts` counters and extended `WorkerResult` with `attempt_index`, `started_at`, and `finished_at`.
- Updated backend artifacts and transcripts to include attempt-scoped IDs instead of overwriting the same task log path on repeated execution.
- Taught the orchestrator to advance and persist task-attempt counters before each worker execution and to emit attempt indices in task and command events.
- Extended model, backend, and orchestrator tests to cover task-attempt round-tripping, attempt-scoped transcripts, and resumed execution with a prior attempt count.

### Why

- Phase 3 requires every verifier rule to declare the stored input fields it relies on, whether it fails open or closed, its severity, and an expected remediation path. The prior implementation emitted severities but left the rest implicit in code branches.
- The local eval harness was still leaving four delivery-gap tests red because wrapped fake agent executables launched from isolated workspaces could not resolve `python` or the checkout import path. That made verifier hardening and runtime-block classification look broken even when the underlying fixtures were correct.
- Phase 2 explicitly requires stale-run reconciliation to distinguish `resumable`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. Without this branch, persisted cancellation evidence could be misreported as a failure after the worker thread disappears.
- Phase 1 still required a real task-attempt model rather than an implicit one-shot worker record. Without explicit attempt indices and per-attempt artifact identity, retries or resumed re-execution could not be audited cleanly.

### Impact

- Persisted verifier findings are now self-describing enough for later audits and external consumers to understand why a rule fired and how to remediate it.
- Future verifier hardening can add rules through one contract surface instead of burying semantics in ad hoc finding construction.
- Fake Claude/Codex eval lanes now execute from isolated workspaces with the same active interpreter and import surface as the main test process.
- Runtime-blocked eval lanes can now reach their real auth/quota fail-closed branches instead of collapsing earlier on a missing `python` command.
- Operators now get deterministic cancelled reconciliation for stale runs that were already on a persisted cancellation path.
- CLI and API reconciliation surfaces expose `classification=cancelled` and `status=cancelled`, which keeps audit output aligned with stored evidence.
- Repeated execution of the same task now produces distinct persisted worker records and distinct artifact IDs instead of overwriting prior logs.
- Attempt metadata is available in evidence, transcripts, and task events, which makes future retry and replay semantics easier to audit.
- The core execution model stays backward-compatible for one-shot tasks while becoming precise enough for resumed or repeated execution paths.

### What changed

- Added a persisted `active_mutation_lease` contract on `RunSession` with explicit `execute`, `resume`, and `reconcile` modes.
- Enforced single-active-writer semantics in the orchestrator so duplicate reconcile or resume attempts become deterministic ignored operations instead of racing state writes.
- Reclaimed stale persisted run leases during reconciliation and emitted explicit `run.lease.stale`, `run.lease.acquired`, `run.lease.released`, and `run.lease.lost` events.
- Failed closed when a run loses mutation authority mid-execution, including a persisted critical evidence finding.
- Extended orchestrator and model tests to cover stale lease reclamation, duplicate writer rejection, and lease-loss fail-closed behavior.

### Why

- The short-term roadmap still required a real concurrency guard for run mutation. Without a persisted single-writer contract, resumed, reconciled, or repeated control-plane operations could race and silently rewrite run state.

### Impact

- Only one active executor, reconciler, or resumer can mutate a run at a time, and that ownership is now visible in persisted run state and events.
- Duplicate resume and reconcile requests now resolve deterministically instead of creating ambiguous concurrent writes.
- Lost mutation authority now fails closed with evidence, which keeps run recovery and audit semantics aligned with the roadmap.

### What changed

- Added a `clawhunt.delivery_protocol.v1` adapter that derives a ClawHunt Delivery Protocol manifest from normalized run evidence.
- Added latest-contract payload shaping for `solution_text + github_pr_* + agent_package_manifest` while degrading unsupported fields like attachments, raw evidence, and cost tracking explicitly into adapter metadata.
- Added CLI and API export surfaces so a stored run can emit a machine-readable delivery protocol payload without mutating persisted evidence.
- Extended protocol adapter contract tests to cover the manifest schema and non-mutation guarantees.

### Why

- The short-term roadmap still required actual delivery-protocol export, not only a legacy v1 solution shim. Without a manifest export layer, SuperClaw could not prove that its evidence model maps cleanly into ClawHunt's newer standardized delivery contract.

### Impact

- SuperClaw can now produce a latest-style ClawHunt delivery payload from any stored run, including a generated `agent_package_manifest`.
- The adapter boundary remains explicit because protocol-only fields stay in the adapter layer instead of leaking into core run/evidence models.
- Operators can inspect or hand off the export through CLI or API before any future authenticated submit path exists for that contract.

### What changed

- Added a typed `task_topology` contract with `linear` and `implement_fanout` topologies.
- Shipped a persisted non-linear `implement_fanout` graph where verify depends on two implementation branches before review can run.
- Updated orchestrator execution to consume dependency frontiers deterministically instead of assuming a purely linear chain.
- Added CLI and API support for selecting the constrained DAG topology and covered DAG resume behavior with integration tests.

### Why

- Phase 3 requires at least one executable, resumable, and persisted non-linear dependency pattern before DAG support can be considered shipped. The previous control plane had dependency fields but still executed as a hard-coded line.

### Impact

- SuperClaw can now persist and execute a limited non-linear graph without weakening evidence order or resume semantics.
- Dry runs, local runs, API-created runs, and resumed runs all preserve the selected topology in execution context.
- Invalid DAG states now fail closed if pending work has no satisfiable dependency frontier.

### What changed

- Added persisted parent-child execution metadata to run sessions and evidence bundles.
- Added orchestrator-managed child spawning with explicit `child_run.spawned`, `child_run.*`, and `child_evidence.added` event coverage.
- Enforced a maximum subagent depth of `1` and rejected child-of-child spawning.
- Propagated parent cancellation into active child runs and preserved the child cancellation outcome in parent-visible state.

### Why

- Phase 3 requires subagents to become a first-class auditable concept before any richer DAG work is allowed. Without an explicit lifecycle contract, child work would stay invisible and cancellation semantics would drift.

### Impact

- Parent runs can now expose child execution ownership, backend choice, verdict, and evidence location without mutating child evidence in place.
- Operators can review child evidence from the parent bundle and see cancellation propagation in the event log.
- Recursive subagent trees remain blocked, which keeps the first orchestration upgrade constrained and testable.

### What changed

- Added an explicit run status contract for `created`, `queued`, `running`, `verifying`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, and `WAITING_FOR_HUMAN_GATE`.
- Rejected invalid persisted run status transitions in the state store.
- Persisted `run.verifying` and `verification.completed` as explicit orchestration events.
- Emitted `run.failed` for terminal failure flows and preserved cancellation compatibility.

### Why

- SuperClaw needs a stable control-plane state machine before resume, stale-run reconciliation, and protocol adapter work can be implemented safely.

### Impact

- Invalid run state mutations now fail fast instead of silently rewriting persisted state.
- Verification is now a first-class persisted stage in the run lifecycle.
- Existing cancel and human-gate compatibility flows remain allowed.

### What changed

- Persisted run execution context and task-graph progress so interrupted runs can be reconciled and resumed deterministically.
- Added control-plane reconciliation that classifies stale `queued` / `running` / `verifying` runs as active, resumable, or fail-closed.
- Replaced placeholder resume surfaces in the CLI and API with orchestrator-backed resume and reconcile operations.
- Promoted stale-run recovery from a documented gap to a runtime capability.

### Why

- The next roadmap milestone requires resume and stale-run handling to be state-backed and observable before protocol adapter work or richer task topologies are introduced.

### Impact

- Operators can now reconcile abandoned runs and resume from the remaining task frontier instead of restarting from scratch.
- Recovery behavior fails closed when persisted graph or execution metadata is insufficient, preserving evidence integrity.
- CLI, API, and runtime capability reporting now describe the same recovery behavior.

### What changed

- Added primary-evidence normalization helpers in the core evidence model and applied them before persistence.
- Deduplicated repeated probes, commands, artifacts, and findings by stable content or artifact identity while preserving insertion order.
- Capped command output and stored worker-result output previews to 4,000 characters per entry so primary evidence remains loadable without streaming.
- Normalized evidence on `StateStore.save_evidence()` so direct bundle mutations still persist into a review-stable contract.

### Why

- Phase 2 requires evidence updates to be idempotent and replay-safe. Without normalization, resumed or repeated save paths can inflate primary evidence and weaken auditability.

### Impact

- Repeated writes of the same normalized evidence no longer create visible duplicate ledger entries.
- Primary evidence now stores bounded previews while leaving large logs and transcripts in artifacts.
- Existing run, API, and verifier flows keep the same verdict semantics with a more stable persistence contract underneath.

### What changed

- Added an orchestrator-owned `cancel_run()` path and switched CLI/API cancellation to use it instead of mutating state directly.
- Prevented already `completed` or `failed` runs from being rewritten to `cancelled`; they now emit `run.cancel.ignored`.
- Added explicit `worker.forced_kill` events and preserved forced-kill evidence when a worker ignores graceful termination.
- Stopped emitting `run.completed` for failed or cancelled terminal outcomes so terminal events align with the actual final status.

### Why

- Phase 2 requires cancellation semantics to be durable and auditable. Direct state mutation allowed terminal runs to be retroactively rewritten and blurred the difference between a cancellation request and a real cancelled outcome.

### Impact

- CLI and API now return whether a cancellation request was accepted, ignored, or immediately terminal.
- Operators can distinguish graceful cancel from forced kill in stored events and worker evidence.
- Terminal event streams are less ambiguous because cancelled or failed runs no longer also emit `run.completed`.

### What changed

- Added a dedicated `clawhunt.v1.solution` protocol adapter module that produces submission payloads from stored evidence without mutating the core bundle.
- Updated `ClawHuntClient.submit_solution()` to accept adapter-built payload objects while keeping a compatibility path for plain string calls.
- Switched the API webhook submit path, `/api/clawhunt/submit`, and CLI `submit` command to build payloads through the adapter instead of shaping JSON inline.
- Recorded unsupported future-facing fields such as `agent_package_manifest` and `cost_tracking` explicitly in adapter metadata instead of silently dropping them.

### Why

- The roadmap requires an explicit adapter boundary between SuperClaw-owned evidence and external ClawHunt submission contracts. Without that layer, every protocol change would force edits across handlers and client code.

### Impact

- The v1 request contract is now isolated behind one named adapter surface.
- Future protocol expansion can add new export shapes without rewriting API and CLI submission flows.
- Core evidence storage remains SuperClaw-owned and is not mutated as a side effect of request shaping.

### What changed

- Added `backend_readiness_classification` to fail closed when command or worker output contains auth, API-key, raw-mode, or quota exhaustion markers.
- Added `submission_artifact_consistency` to validate that submitted runs carry a consistent response payload plus durable evidence artifacts.
- Extended the adversarial profile idempotency contract so the new findings replace prior copies instead of duplicating across repeated verification passes.

### Why

- Phase 3 verifier hardening needs to catch false-positive “success” states where a backend never really became usable, and to ensure a claimed submission still has enough durable evidence attached to review later.

### Impact

- Backend login prompts and quota exhaustion messages now turn into explicit failing verifier findings instead of being treated as neutral text.
- Submitted runs without `evidence-json` or worker evidence artifacts now fail verification even if a response payload exists.
- Re-running the adversarial profile remains stable and machine-readable.
